# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/).
Versioning: the release line was reset on 2026-10-21 — releases are 0.0.x and
increment in patch order (0.0.1, then 0.0.2, then 0.0.3, and so on) until
otherwise specified; the release tag must always equal package.json's version.

Repo conventions: measured claims cite their receipts (`docs/testing-strategy.md`, `benchmarks/memory-eval/*/RESULTS.md`). Changes to agent behavior — prompts, default models, routing, guard rules, hooks, memory injection — are filed under **Agent Behavior**; the policy lives in [docs/BEHAVIOR.md](docs/BEHAVIOR.md).

## [Unreleased]

### Changed

- **landing: the orphan round's cut pays its scroll bill** — two rounds landed without paying the gate: the 0.0.26 stop took the page to 3,224 words (24 over, CI red on main since the ledger round — the deploy-verify tripwire named it), and the 0.0.27 stop took it to 3,288. Compressed authored copy across the receipts, dex, hero, loop, changelog-rail, FAQ, showcase, alphabet and finale bands — no generated figure or gated literal touched — plus the two newest rail titles' bold prefixes (their bullet bodies byte-identical); the budget spec now measures 3,150 words (12.2vh, every band ≤ 2.5vh). This entry rides Unreleased, so it costs the page zero words: the rail renders cut stops only.

## [0.0.28] - 2026-10-26

### Added

- **engine: the hardening round — the detached engine's verdict, and six honest-failure lanes** — the memory system's core-USP question was re-litigated end to end: is the detached engine (the self-learning daemon on :3030) stable, viable and scalable, or should the memory system move into the agent process? The research round behind the verdict: a ~54-source 2026 landscape survey ([docs/detached-vs-embedded-research.md](docs/detached-vs-embedded-research.md)) plus a source-level reverse engineering of ten competitor repos at pinned SHAs ([docs/memory-competitor-source-deep-dive.md](docs/memory-competitor-source-deep-dive.md)) — Letta V1 and current, Mem0, Cognee, Graphiti, MemOS full-stack and local plugin, LangMem, Memobase, A-MEM — each read at the file level for process model, write path, background consolidation, durability, crash behavior and shipped-bug catalogs. The verdict, recorded with tiered evidence in [docs/detached-engine-hardening.md](docs/detached-engine-hardening.md): **retain**. Every system that runs continuous memory consolidation ships a locally-supervised daemon — OpenAI's own `codex-app-server-daemon` (pidfile + file-lock lifecycle, `shutdownGraceSeconds`, embedded fallback) validates the hybrid almost feature-for-feature; MemOS's local plugin is a per-agent better-sqlite3 daemon with a KeepAlive supervisor; Supermemory local is a single self-contained memory-server binary. The embedded alternative is worse on the axes that matter: oven-sh/bun#40659 (`BEGIN IMMEDIATE` does not exclude across processes on Windows, closed as not planned) makes the single-writer daemon the only durable SQLite shape, and embedding puts 20-minute watchdog'd jobs inside the interactive process. What shipped instead is the six-lane hardening the sweep exposed, each mapped to a published precedent: **H1 — the outbox dead-letter lane** (a queued note whose ingest keeps failing was retried every drain pass forever, silently; MemOS's dead-letter quarantine and Graphiti's 202-then-drop loss #1707 are the precedents): `UNOWN_OUTBOX_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (5) tracked per file in `notes/outbox-state.json` with the last error, then the file moves to `notes/dead/` with a `.reason.txt` sidecar — never deleted, `mv` back to retry; unparseable files dead-letter on first sight; the dead count surfaces in `/engine`, doctor's new `outbox dead-letter` row and `engine status`. **H2 — delivery retry-before-outbox** (Letta's client contract): a NETWORK-failed note POST gets ONE bounded ~600ms retry before the durable fallback — a note landing inside the engine-restart window delivers directly instead of waiting for the 5-minute note-sync drain; HTTP statuses never retry (an answering engine is deliberate); the 2.5s delivery budget still bounds everything. **H3 — `/api/health` lifecycle** (the codex-app-server-daemon contract): a cheap dedicated probe — no 500-run scan, no bridge fetch — answering `{ok, phase: warming|ready|draining, pid, uptimeMs, drainBudgetMs, version}`, with `/api/overview` carrying the same fields additively; the phase flips to draining FIRST so a stopper can wait out the drain instead of racing it. **H4 — the in-flight job drain registry** (Cognee's background-task registry): `withBusy` tracks the promise of the work holding the busy mutex at the single choke point every job holder goes through; `beginDraining()` refuses new jobs with the 409-class draining conflict; graceful shutdown awaits the in-flight job bounded by `UNOWN_ENGINE_DRAIN_BUDGET_MS` (10s default) before closing the store — a deliberate stop lets a mid-flight dream or harness cycle FINISH instead of orphaning its `running` rows for the next boot's `recoverOrphans` sweep (live-verified: a SIGTERM under a live `auto-dream` logs the bounded wait and the honest close-under). **H5 — Windows pidfile recovery**: the `cmd /c start /b` lane (the bun#31603 escape) never learned the engine's pid — cmd.exe exits right after `start` — so the pidfile was never written and `engine status`/`engine stop` were Windows-blind; the supervisor now polls the engine post-spawn (bounded 15s, foreign-port-aware) and writes the pidfile from `/api/overview`'s pid. **H6 — cross-install token discovery** (resilience.md R11's binary↔source token split, closed): the engine mirrors its per-boot token to `$UNOWN_HOME/engine.token` — the one tree every install shape resolves identically — at the same post-bind commit point, ownership-checked at drain, self-healed in maintenance; every engine client resolves a pair (own data dir first, shared mirror second) and the 401 retry re-presents whichever side rotated OR the other side's token — a binary CLI probing a source-repo engine heals on the first request instead of reporting `blocked · token` forever. Tests: five new files, 44 new tests (the dead-letter subprocess probe with a closed store; the delivery-retry fetch matrix; the health route + phase contract + drain registry + draining-conflict 409 class; the injectable Windows pid-recovery poll; the token-pair resolution + cross-install 401 heal against a live Bun.serve endpoint); the full self-learning suite at the cut: **4,825 pass / 0 fail across 208 files**. The three new knobs (`UNOWN_OUTBOX_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, `UNOWN_ENGINE_DRAIN_BUDGET_MS`, the note-retry note) are documented in `.env.example` and `docs/configuration.md`; `docs/resilience.md`'s R4/R5/R6/R11 rows and the design-references table carry the new mechanisms with their precedents. **[Agent Behavior]** (the engine lifecycle contract — `/api/health` phases + the drain's refusal semantics — filed in [docs/BEHAVIOR.md](docs/BEHAVIOR.md))

### Fixed

- **engine: `unown engine start` on a Windows binary install opened a dialog instead of an engine** — every spawn attempt popped "B:/~BUN/root/unown.exe — The system cannot find the drive specified." (a ShellExecute `ERROR_INVALID_DRIVE`) and the engine stayed down; binary installs on any OS never got a spawned engine at all. Two independent faults composed into it, both on the detach path (`spawnEngineProcess`). **(a)** it asked `process.argv` whether it was running a compiled binary — and `process.argv` can never answer that: measured on bun 1.4.2, a standalone reports `["bun", "<virtual entry path>", …args]` on **every** platform (`B:\~BUN\root\unown.exe` on Windows, `/$bunfs/root/unown` on Linux), so argv[0] is the literal `"bun"` rather than the executable and the `!argv[1] || argv[1] === argv[0]` guess matched nothing. A binary install therefore took the source branch and handed its engine child Bun's virtual path as its first argument, so `engine serve` was never what the child ran. The entry decision now comes from `IS_COMPILED_BINARY` (`import.meta.url` — the detector `packaging.ts` already owned and `tools.ts` already used, while the supervisor had simply diverged from it), extracted as `buildEngineServeArgv` so the rule is a function rather than an inline ternary. **(b)** the empty-string window-title slot that the v0.0.17 fix added never reaches cmd: a `windowsVerbatimArguments` spawn gives an empty argv entry no token at all (measured against `cmd /c echo`), so `start` consumed the QUOTED EXECUTABLE as its title and ShellExecuted whatever followed — which fault (a) had just made be the virtual path, hence the dialog. The slot is now the literal `'""'` token, which survives verbatim. Both halves are required: fixing only (a) leaves `start` trying to execute `engine`. **Why this survived a test written for exactly this surface:** `test/windows-spawn-verbatim.test.ts` pinned the argv as DATA and asserted `spec.args[3] === ""` — it encoded the fault, because a list assertion cannot see either the command line the spawner emits or what the child receives. The suite now has a runtime leg: it compiles a real standalone binary from `test/fixtures/spawn-argv-entry.ts`, feeds that binary's OWN measured `execPath`/`argv` through the production builders, spawns it for real and asserts the argv it records. Verified red-able: reverting the title token fails both the command-line pin and the spawn (the child never records), and reverting the entry detection fails the argv pin and the spawn (the child receives the virtual path). `scripts/start.ts` carries the same `start /b ""` shape and is NOT affected — it spawns without verbatim, where the default escaper quotes the empty arg; it is the one to re-check if that leg ever gains the flag. Verified live on the reporting host: the installed v0.0.26 `windows-x64` binary pops the dialog and leaves the port unserved, while a `windows-x64` artifact built from this tree answers `engine: UP (pid …)` with no dialog and `GET /api/overview` → `ok:true`. No behaviour change for source installs.

- **engine: a source→binary install transition left a logon engine pointed at the OLD checkout** — live-caught on the reporting host: `install.json` read `mode: binary` while `HKCU\…\Run` still launched `engine-service.ps1`, generated the day before by the previous source install, running `bun.exe <checkout>\bin\unown engine serve`. A compiled install writes no service definition and `engine install --auto` skips with reason `compiled` — so nothing ever removed the old one, and every logon booted an engine rooted at the checkout with a **different memory database** from the binary's `UNOWN_HOME` (both stores were live on that host: a 466 KB repo DB and a 212 KB `~/.unown` DB). Whichever wins `:3030` serves one brain; the other keeps writing notes nobody reads. `reconcileStaleService` now closes it: on the `compiled` skip it asks `serviceStatus()`, and a definition found there is by construction residue, so it is removed and reported — never silently, because an autostart that vanishes must be explainable to whoever set it up. It passes `remember:false` into `uninstallEngineService` so the cleanup does NOT write the `engine-service.disabled` opt-out marker: that marker means *the user* opted out, and leaving it here would suppress the service on every later `--source` install of the same machine. Only the `compiled` reason reconciles — CI, the `UNOWN_ENGINE_AUTOSTART=0` kill switch and a remembered opt-out all describe this process, not the machine's history, and tearing down a real service over one of those is the worse bug. The lane was also unreachable where it was needed: only `scripts/install.ts` (the source installer) ever ran `engine install --auto`, so both binary installers now invoke it once at the end of an install — a call that can only reconcile, never install, and is non-fatal in both shells. Pinned by three cases in `test/engine-service.test.ts` (residue removed with `remember:false`; nothing on disk stays silent; the non-compiled skips never reconcile), mutation-verified: disabling the hook or flipping the marker flag each turns one red. On the reporting host the Run value was removed and the stale script moved aside rather than deleted.

### Changed

- **docs: the binary lane's "no engine" claim was false, and eight surfaces repeated it** — `docs/install.md`, `docs/macos.md`, `docs/windows.md`, `docs/quickstart.md` (twice), both binary installers' post-install note, the `INSTALL.txt` shipped inside every release archive, and the `resolveServiceSpec` refusal text all said a binary install has *no engine*. It has one: `unown engine start` from a release artifact serves the API on `:3030` with state under `UNOWN_HOME`, measured on this host. `docs/resilience.md` had even documented "a binary install talking to a source-repo engine" as a recognized state, so the docs contradicted each other. The boundary is now stated for what it actually is — a binary install ships the CLI **and** an on-demand engine, and does NOT ship the llm-bridge (an archive is `unown`, `VERSION`, `LICENSE`, `INSTALL.txt` and nothing else) or the always-on OS service; so the jobs that call a model need a bridge to reach, and logon autostart is the source lane's. The auto-skip line and the service refusal message changed with them (both still match the `/source-install/` and `/--source/` pins). Left alone deliberately: the landing page's `CLI only` install-chip label — that is a scroll-budget and claims-gated surface, and "CLI first" still reads true there as a label for the fast path.

## [0.0.27] - 2026-10-26

### Added

- **subagents: stress round four — the sync lane's pid-kill orphans** — the round-3 blog's "not covered" ledger named exactly this candidate: sync-lane children of a SIGKILLed parent were covered on the signal paths (the child-reaper registration) but carried no journal, no heartbeat, and no sweep of their own. Live-reproduced first, against the same metered endpoint: a parent `kill -9`ed mid-sync-run (the child mid-bash in a worktree) left the sync child AND its bash grandchild alive with ZERO durable trace — no `.unown/fleet/` at all, nothing for a second boot to notice, the orphan killed by hand at the end of the probe because no tool would. The competitor sweep says the shape is architectural, not exotic: Claude Code files it as "the orphan problem is architectural" (#33979: subagents as separate processes orphan when the parent dies unexpectedly; also #18405, where a user killing an unknown orphan took down their parent session — the never-kill-what-you-cannot-identify lesson, lived), the agent SDK tracks auto-termination-when-parent-dies as a standing feature request (#132/#142), Codex ships both the orphaned-residual-process bug (#15379) and the durable-reconciliation feature request (#54428) the fleet lane already answered in round 3. Findings and fixes: **R4-F1 — the sync lane's own children of a `kill -9` parent**: the fix extends round 3's journal + heartbeat + sweep to BOTH lanes instead of growing a drifting twin — every `runSubagentOnce` attempt (including structured-output retries and fallback-model rotation, each a fresh child process) appends `{op:"spawned", lane:"sync", pid, parentPid, promptFile, …}` to the same append-only `.unown/fleet/journal.jsonl`, where `promptFile` is the mkdtemp system-prompt file path — the one unique string in a `--no-session` child's argv, and what the sweep's `ps -o command=` identity probe matches on POSIX; the child self-writes `<id>.hb.json` `{pid, ts}` ~1×/s from its boot (`UNOWN_SYNC_HEARTBEAT` carries the path parent→child, internal plumbing like the fleet channel vars, and stripped from bash-spawned grandchildren so a grandchild can never keep the file fresh under its own pid); a parent that OBSERVES the exit journals `finished` with the honest status (`done`/`failed`/`timeout`/`aborted`/`spawn_error`) — one entry per attempt — while a parent killed mid-run writes nothing and the next boot's sweep owns the orphan from there; a worktree-isolated sync child journals its `worktree` amendment PER ATTEMPT so whichever attempt was live at the crash correlates the tree for orphan-marking (kept on disk, the same kept-tree rule); and the boot line is now lane-honest — `subagent crash sweep: 1 live orphan(s) reaped (0 fleet, 1 sync), …`. **R4-F2 — the journal lived where the boot wasn't** (found while designing R4, both lanes affected): round 3 keyed BOTH the writer (FleetManager default) and the sweeper on the session's literal cwd, so a session booted from a subdirectory journaled spawns a repo-root boot could never find — invisibility by construction, one directory short; both sides now resolve the git repo TOPLEVEL (a hardened read-only `git rev-parse` probe riding gittools' GitSpawn exec-sink neutralizers), the sweep still checks the literal cwd path first so 0.0.26-era journals on real disks keep being found, and a worktree-isolated child's journal is derived from the SESSION cwd (never the disposable tree's own `.unown`). Every fix pinned: 13 new unit pins in `test/fleet-reaper.test.ts` (the sync decision table — live-parent skip, stale-hb abandon, REAL-bash cmdline-verified reap with the prompt-file token, pre-heartbeat POSIX reap, the empty-token wildcard guard, finished-untouched idempotence, lane-count boot line, per-attempt worktree dedupe; journal-dir git-toplevel resolution + non-git fallback + R3-era literal compat; the heartbeat writer's beat/stop/exit contract), the new `test/subagent-stress-round4.test.ts` (5 cross-module pins: the 1 s / 5 s freshness contract shared by both lanes, the env name, the vendored bash strip source contract, the journal entry shape, and the fleet manager + sync sweep meeting at ONE toplevel journal), plus an end-to-end regression in `test/cli/task-structured.test.ts`: a real CLI parent SIGKILLed mid-SYNC-child-run (the default lane, worktree-isolated), then a second real boot sweeps — journal `reaped` (heartbeat-verified, `sync-lane` in the reason), the orphan's heartbeat cleaned, the tree marked orphaned and kept, the orphan dead. Re-verified live both directions against the same endpoint: pre-fix (orphan + grandchild alive, zero trace, second boot silent) and post-fix (the second boot prints `subagent crash sweep: 1 live orphan(s) reaped (0 fleet, 1 sync)`, the journal carries `reaped — parent dead; live sync-lane orphan killed (heartbeat-verified)`, the hb file is cleaned, the orphan is gone). One more live-measured seam closed en route: `unown --version` is a fast path that never reaches `bootRuntime` — the sweep rides SESSION boots, which is what the docs always said but the probe now proves. One release-round find, caught by the 3-OS assertion before anything published: two of the new pins compared git's realpathed toplevel against macOS's literal `/var` spelling (the `/private/var` symlink — the same directory, a different string); the pins now compare canonical paths (the repo's own `test/_env.ts` pattern) and the tag was re-cut. The re-cut's assertion then caught a second, genuinely useful one: the round's own e2e killed the parent while the sync child was still EMITTING its boot/event stream — an orphan dies at its next stdout write when the dead parent's pipe EPIPEs it (the R3-documented death mode), so the sweep honestly recorded `abandoned — already gone` instead of the live reap the regression pins; the crash now lands in the child's quiet phase (the e2e gates on the bash grandchild existing — a bounded poll that survives a transiently-failing `ps`, replacing the blind sleep — and the R3 fleet e2e got the same gate), and the failure path now dumps the sweep arm, the heartbeat and the cmdline probe so the next race diagnoses itself. Twelve consecutive local re-runs green post-fix, both crash-sweep e2e tests. A third assertion round caught the gate's own portability: `ps --ppid` (select-by-parent) is a Linux procps extension — BSD ps on macOS has no such selection, so every poll exit-1'd and the gate never saw the (running, visible-in-the-journal) grandchild; both gates now list `pid,ppid,command` for all processes and filter by parent, one call on every POSIX platform. The full self-learning suite at the cut: **4,790 pass / 0 fail across 203 files**; root typecheck clean. **[Agent Behavior]** (the sync-lane lifecycle contract the journal now records — filed under the subagent report contract in [docs/BEHAVIOR.md](docs/BEHAVIOR.md))

- **ci: a gate for the live page, and the red-CI-on-main case is now named** — Vercel deploys `main` on push in parallel with CI, and `main` is not protected against an admin push (`enforce_admins: false`), so a commit that failed its gates can be — and was — production: `839600e` (2026-10-26T07:37Z) landed four failing jobs, two of them required contexts (`Unit tests (landing)`, `Build + test (windows-latest)`), and stayed live for ~59 minutes until `aa9a955` went green. Nothing said so. `scripts/verify-deploy.mjs` has been able to assert the deployed page since the blog round, but only ever ran when someone remembered to invoke it. `.github/workflows/deploy-verify.yml` wires it three ways: `workflow_run` on CI completion, a daily `schedule` tripwire for the drift no push can see (a CDN or alias change, an expired asset, a rollback that re-pointed production), and a manual dispatch against any origin. The CI-conclusion step is deliberately a SEPARATE failure from the page check: "the site renders" must never bury "the commit that shipped failed its own gates". What the lane proves is the integrity of whatever production serves — not that this commit is what production serves; the script has no build-id to compare against and Vercel's deployment identity is not repository state (in practice CI completes ~10 min after a push, the deploy lands in 2-4, so it reads the new one). Actual prevention is a dashboard setting this commit cannot touch: Vercel's "Skipped Commit Checks" deployment protection. Verified against live production before landing: `node scripts/verify-deploy.mjs` exits 0, every check ok.

### Security

- **ci: the release path stops trusting a moving upstream, and the token, the audit and the workflow scan all get narrowed** — five hardening changes to the CI/CD surface, each closing the gap between "the checkout is proven" and "what a user downloads or reads is proven". **(1) No more piped shell script on the release path.** syft was installed with `curl …/anchore/syft/main/install.sh | sh` inside the job that mints the OIDC identity signing the release — an unpinned, undigested dependency fetched from a moving branch while every `uses:` in the repo is SHA-pinned. It is now a version-pinned release tarball, verified with `sha256sum --strict` against the same tag's `checksums.txt`, behind a one-line guard that fails loudly if the checksum file does not name exactly that asset. **(2) `release.yml` is least-privilege per job.** The workflow granted `contents: write` + `attestations: write` + `id-token: write` to all six jobs — including the 3-OS matrix that hydrates provider data over the network and the legs that install PUBLISHED release assets. The workflow now declares `contents: read`, elevating only `build` (attestation identity) and `publish-release` (the release write) — the exact set GitHub documents for a job that attests and then verifies; OpenSSF Token-Permissions scores the workflow *and* the job, so the four jobs capped at medium by a shared grant are now clean. **(3) Two probe lanes had drifted behind their sibling.** `local-llm-probe.yml` and `nonllm-probe.yml` carried no `permissions:` block at all while `complementary-probe.yml` declared one — so the two lanes that `npm install` third-party packages and download model weights ran under whatever the repository default hands out. Both now declare `contents: read`, and all three probe checkouts write no git credential into the tree. **(4) The advisory audit now covers every manifest CI installs.** `bun audit --prod` reads the root manifest and `package.json` declares no `workspaces`, so a nested tree was invisible to the gate: the `nonllm` bench lane's `@huggingface/transformers` + prebuilt `onnxruntime-node`, installed on every probe run, had no advisory scan and no update automation. The security job (and its `full` mirror in `xplat.yml`) audits that committed lockfile with `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` — measured green at 0 vulnerabilities, so the gate is born passing — and refuses, rather than skips, a listed directory with no lockfile. A fourth Dependabot stream covers it, majors ignored because a major moves the encoder the H9 latency receipts were measured against. `benchmarks/edit-bench` stays out on evidence, not preference: it ships no lockfile, so `npm audit` errors ENOLOCK instead of answering. **(5) CodeQL reads the workflows themselves.** The single-language JS job is now a `javascript-typescript` + `actions` matrix — the publish path is part of this product's supply chain and was the one surface a JS-only scan could not see. Neither CodeQL leg is a required status context, and the matrix runs `fail-fast: false`, so a finding on either side surfaces without hiding the other.

## [0.0.26] - 2026-10-26

### Added

- **subagents: stress round three — the ledger's unpaid edges** — the previous round's "not covered" ledger listed exactly what this one paid off: the ask-park edge cases (stop while parked, the identical re-ask), the worktree name-collision seam, and the reaper registration for fleet children of crashed parents — all four live-reproduced against agnes-3.0-flash before any fix was written (plus two bonus defects the re-verification pass itself uncovered). Findings and fixes: **R3-F1 — a stop while parked killed the parked child**: the parked `ask_question` tool resolved only via steer/answer or the 600 s deadline, so a `task_control stop` landed as a wrap-up steer in a turn that could never start (the tool's `await` held it) and the child sat parked until the parent's 3 s tree-kill backstop SIGTERM'd it mid-park (measured live: exit 143, the ask file rotting on disk, the stop instruction never model-visible); the stop now UN-PARKS the tool with an honest typed cancellation — `[stop requested by the orchestrator] No answer will come. Finish your current thought in one short sentence and stop` — returned verbatim, never prefixed as a fabricated `[orchestrator's answer]` (the interrupt-fabricates-an-answer anti-pattern, Claude Code #87179), the child cleans up its own ask file on the way out, and the backstop grew to a 12 s UNREF'D grace (one wrap-up model round trip) with the new boot sweep as the backstop of the backstop. **R3-F1b — the stop path double-delivered the result** (found live during re-verification): `stop()` finalized, then the tree-killed child's close handler re-finalized — two identical "stopped" result messages to the parent model; `finalize()` is now idempotent per child. **R3-F2 — the identical re-ask was swallowed**: ask dedup was keyed on question TEXT, and only cleared when a tick observed the ask file absent, so an identical-text re-ask whose rm+rewrite landed inside one 1 s watcher window never reached the parent (live: a same-message double-ask delivered once and the child parked the full 600 s on a question the orchestrator never saw; the cross-turn re-ask worked only by timing luck — Gemini CLI #29026 is this exact bug, and the consensus is dedup on message ids, never content); every ask now carries a monotonic `seq` and the parent dedups on the `(ts, seq)` INSTANCE pair. **R3-F2b — concurrent ask_question calls clobbered the single waiter** (found live during re-verification): two `ask_question` calls in one assistant message execute concurrently, and the second park overwrote the child's one `answerWaiter` — the first tool's waiter was lost forever and the first resolve deleted the ask file out from under the still-parked sibling; parked waiters are now a QUEUE with a per-waiter question, an answer satisfies the asker(s) of the STANDING question (identical-text siblings all resolve on one answer), the ask file is refcounted so it outlives every answer but the last, and a stop cancels every parked ask at once. **R3-F3 — the worktree name-collision seam**: `slice(0, 48)` on the spawn id could strip the caller's `-<ts36>-<rand6>` suffix — at ≥ 47 chars of agent name the safeId had ZERO entropy (live: a 50-char agent's same-batch pair collided into a typed `create_failed`, and because the first tree was kept dirty, EVERY later spawn of that agent was rejected — a livelock until manual `unown worktrees remove`); the truncation is now entropy-preserving (the name's head-32 + the id's last 16, cap unchanged at 48 for MAX_PATH headroom), pinned by three same-name spawns landing three distinct trees. **R3-F4 — a crashed parent left live orphans with zero trace**: the fleet registry records finished children only, so a SIGKILLed parent (live: `kill -9` mid-child-bash) left the orphan running through its quiet phases (a mid-bash child until the bash ends — the dead parent's stdout pipe EPIPEs it at its next write; a parked child the full 600 s of upstream tokens) with NO registry entry, NO journal, control/ask files rotting, and the worktree journaled `spawned` forever; the fix is the tmux-consensus shape minus the daemon — a new `src/fleet-reaper.ts`: every spawn appends `{op:"spawned", pid, parentPid, …}` to an append-only `.unown/fleet/journal.jsonl`, the child heartbeats `{pid, ts}` ~1×/s (fresh + self-written is the strongest identity a bare pid can never give — a reused pid cannot fake it), and EVERY session boot sweeps: open entries whose PARENT is dead are orphans — live ones (heartbeat-fresh, plus a `ps` cmdline on macOS/Linux that names the child's session file) are tree-killed and journaled `reaped`, dead ones journaled `abandoned`, the rotting channels are cleaned, and an adopted worktree is marked `orphaned` in the worktree journal and KEPT on disk for manual recovery (the kept-tree rule — never auto-destroy possible work). Safety in order: a live parent always wins, never kill a pid you cannot identify (Windows without a heartbeat traces honestly instead of killing a possibly-reused pid), never sweep from inside a subagent; `UNOWN_FLEET_REAPER=0` is the kill-switch. Fleet AND sync subagents also now `registerChild` with the last-resort child-reaper (the same SIGINT/SIGTERM/exit hygiene LSP/MCP servers always had), and `dispose()` cleans the channel files while deliberately leaving journal entries OPEN for the next boot to trace honestly. Every fix pinned: 34 new unit pins across `test/fleet-child.test.ts` (unpark-on-stop verbatim text, the seq contract, the waiter queue, the heartbeat), `test/fleet-runtime.test.ts` (instance-keyed dedup incl. the same-tick window, finalize idempotence, the backstop's file hygiene, journal spawn/finish/worktree records), the new `test/fleet-reaper.test.ts` (the sweep's full decision table: live-parent skip, heartbeat-gated reap, stale-hb abandon, idempotence, worktree orphan-marking), `test/worktree.test.ts` (entropy-preserving truncation, no same-name collision, the livelock exit), and the new `test/subagent-stress-round3.test.ts` (reaper registration through a REAL spawn, the timing contracts), plus an end-to-end regression in `test/cli/task-structured.test.ts`: a real CLI parent SIGKILLed mid-fleet-run, then a second real boot sweeps — journal `reaped`, channels cleaned, worktree orphaned, orphan dead. The two criticals re-verified live against the same endpoint that exposed them (P1: stopped parked child exits 0 with its wrap-up sentence and a clean fleet dir; P4: `kill -9` → next boot prints `fleet crash sweep: 1 live orphan(s) reaped … 1 worktree(s) marked orphaned` and the journal carries the terminal record the dead parent could not write). The full self-learning suite re-ran green after the fixes (4,771 tests / 0 fail across 202 files); root typecheck clean. **[Agent Behavior]** (the typed stop-while-parked cancellation and the concurrent-ask resolution the model now reads — filed under the subagent report contract in [docs/BEHAVIOR.md](docs/BEHAVIOR.md))

### Fixed

- **landing: a reel whose webm fails to decode falls back to its mp4** — Chromium commits to the first `<source>` it picks and does not try the next one after a media decode error, and a hardware VP9 decoder can reject these reels outright (reproduced on Windows Edge and Chrome with an older Intel UHD driver: `PIPELINE_ERROR_DECODE` at ~0.4s; `--disable-accelerated-video-decode` and the mp4 sibling both play). The dex now swaps a failing webm for its mp4 once — resuming the visible reel automatically rather than leaving the stage frozen on its poster — so the webm-first ordering keeps its bandwidth win without stranding those readers. Pinned by the dex unit suite (one-shot swap, no reload loop) and a new e2e case that aborts every webm on the production build and requires the shown reel to reach HAVE_ENOUGH_DATA and advance its clock on the mp4.

### Changed

- **landing: the v0.0.25 cut pays its landing bill** — the new cut stop pushed the page over the 3,200-word scroll gate (3,205 in CI); the changelog rail's authored lead is compressed by eleven words — no generated number or gated literal touched — and the page now measures 3,191 words in the e2e budget spec (12.3vh, every band ≤ 3vh). The same change un-reds the blog renderer test, whose table/code/image pins had ridden `BLOG_POSTS[0]`: the 2026-10-26 chaos-round post legitimately landed without a table, so the feature set is now pinned to a rich fixture by slug while the newest post keeps its h1/prose/wrapper checks.

## [0.0.25] - 2026-10-26

### Added

- **subagents: stress round two — the chaos scenarios, seven live findings, and the fleet that no longer lies, leaks, or dies young** — the 2026-10-26 round drove the subagent system through the failure shapes the 2026-10-25 round never touched: nested CLI invocations from inside fleet children, watchdog kills mid-work, oversized reports, slow async children outliving the print-mode exit grace, and steering races — all live against agnes-3.0-flash in a fresh realistic lab (SplitLite, an expense-splitting API with planted quirks), plus a competitor sweep across Claude Code / Crush / Gemini CLI / OpenCode / Codex / Amp issue trackers that ranked the same failure families by real-user hit frequency. Findings and fixes: **R2-F1 — timeout kills were untyped**: a child killed by the 300 s wall-clock watchdog surfaced as a bare `exit 143` with its mid-sentence answer-so-far standing as the report (measured live: the orchestrator guessed "cut short" from prose); the section header now names the cause AND the budget (`timeout; killed at 300s budget`, via the extracted `subagentSectionStatus`), the body flags the text as answer-so-far mid-work, and the async lane's stop reason (timeout vs orchestrator request) rides the delivered result instead of dying in the log. **R2-F2 — tool-mismatch children burned their whole budget**: a read-only `explore` handed a WRITING task spent 42 turns / 133k tokens attempting the impossible instead of refusing; the boundary instructions now name the shape ("a task that needs a tool you do not have … is a missing-access blocker — return BLOCKED: <what you lack> in your FIRST reply") so weaker models map "I have no write tool" onto the typed refusal lane. **[Agent Behavior]** **R2-F3 — 16k report truncation was silent**: a 16,063-char report ended mid-word inside a code snippet with no marker, and the parent model had to infer the cut (miscounting the size by 12k in the process) — exactly the most-upvoted competitor complaint family (Claude Code #91580/#90544, Gemini CLI #22323, OpenCode #37852); both lanes now append a visible note naming the cap and the remedy (compress, or write the full report to a file and return the path). **R2-F4 (critical) — print mode hard-exited past a live fleet**: the 15 s hard-exit grace armed at the SPAWNED turn's `agent_end` fired while the async child was mid-bash — exit 0 (success!), zero result deliveries, an orphaned process tree, the child's work lost (live-reproduced three times with full process timelines; the v0.0.24 F4 fix only held for children finishing inside the grace window); `isFleetBusy()` (live children OR undelivered batched results OR an in-flight result turn) now gates the hard-exit timer into re-arming, and the 180 s stall watchdog counts fleet liveness as session liveness — the drain's 900 s contract and the exit guards finally agree. **R2-F5 (critical) — bash grandchildren stole the fleet control inbox**: the fleet child's bash tool passed `UNOWN_FLEET_CONTROL` (and ASK/ID/SESSION) to every process it spawned, so a nested `unown` grandchild's fleet-child extension raced the rightful child to consume steer/stop/answer commands every 250 ms — stolen commands were silently dropped and the child never saw them (live-reproduced: injected markers consumed, delivered nowhere; a controlled repro proved the child's own lane works when it is the sole poller); the vendored bash tool's spawn env now strips the four `UNOWN_FLEET_*` vars exactly the way it always stripped `PI_*` session vars — the control channel is private to the direct child. **R2-F6 — per-agent `timeoutMs` never composed with the fleet lane**: the docs promised it, the fleet watchdog read only the manager-level default (the `browser` builtin's 600 s budget silently ran to 900 s); each child's watchdog now resolves its own routing budget with the sync lane's 30 s–30 min clamp, and the stop reason reports the child's own budget. **R2-F7 — no fleet-wide concurrency cap**: async fan-out stacked unbounded processes across turns (the batch cap bounds one call, not the stack — Claude Code ships a 20-slot cap whose overflow error tells the model not to retry, and the competitor sweep ranked concurrency contention the #3 real-user family); `UNOWN_FLEET_MAX_CONCURRENT` (default 8, the widget row cap; 0 = unlimited; junk falls back to the default) now gates `spawnFleetChild` with a typed failure that names the cap, the env knob, and the remedy — wait for results, check `task_control {action:"list"}`, or stop a child — never a silent drop. Every fix is pinned: `test/subagent-stress-round2.test.ts` (24 pins — status-label precedence, boundary text, fleet truncation + stop-reason notes, cap resolver semantics, `isFleetBusy` legs, spawn refusal at the cap + slot reopening, stop-reason delivery) and three new end-to-end regressions in `test/cli/task-structured.test.ts` (the 16k truncation note through the real CLI; the fleet-env strip proven by a fleet child whose own `env | grep UNOWN_FLEET_` finds nothing; the print-mode run that stays alive 4× past a 2 s exit grace while its async child sleeps, then delivers the result — the exact prompt that orphaned pre-fix). The golden harness itself gained an async-lane fix: mock-bridge scripts now fire once per user text (steer-delivered fleet results land as user-role messages, so the old any-userText matcher re-fired scripted tool calls into an infinite spawn loop). The full self-learning suite re-ran green after the fixes (4,737 tests / 0 fail across 200 files). **[Agent Behavior]** (the boundary-instruction sentence, the truncation/timeout notes the model now reads, and the cap's retry-discouraging error text — all filed under the subagent report contract in [docs/BEHAVIOR.md](docs/BEHAVIOR.md))

## [0.0.24] - 2026-10-26

### Added

- **subagents: the stress round — ten live scenarios, six real findings, six fixes, and the receipts** — the subagent system had never been driven the way the docs promise it works: a real model, a real project, sequential AND parallel delegation, on the metered production endpoint. The 2026-10-25 round built a realistic task-manager project (`stress-lab`), ran ten scenarios through `unown -p` against agnes-3.0-flash (sequential explore / worker implementation / worker→reviewer loop / BLOCKED probe / structured output / timeout kill; parallel batch-of-4 mixed agents / worktree-isolated parallel writers / async fleet with steering / batch-overflow probe), and fixed what it found — every fix pinned by a new unit suite (`test/subagent-stress-round.test.ts`, 13 pins) and three end-to-end regressions (`test/cli/task-structured.test.ts`), and the two critical ones re-verified live against the same endpoint that exposed them. Findings and fixes: **F1 — merge guidance was model-guessed**: the orchestrator told the user two parallel worktree writers would "auto-resolve cleanly" while the real sequential merge hit three file conflicts; kept-with-commits worktrees now carry a computed merge preview (`git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only`, git ≥ 2.38, fail-closed to "unavailable") in the disposition detail — git's own answer, never the model's guess. **F2 (critical) — structured outputs were unusable through the model**: the live model passed `output_schema` as a stringified JSON string three times in a row and the preflight rejected each ("schema must be an object"), so the whole typed-fan-out feature silently degraded to prose-embedded schemas; the intake now parses string schemas leniently (`coerceOutputSchema`), reports the coercion back as a visible note so the model learns the object form, and an unparseable string still fails fast before any paid spawn with a fix-naming error. **F3 — safety refusals dodged the BLOCKED protocol**: a child refused an unsafe task with well-meaning free-form prose — safety worked, the typed signal did not; the boundary instructions now name safety/policy/ethics refusals explicitly as BLOCKED shapes ("do NOT write free-form refusal prose"). **[Agent Behavior]** **F4 (critical) — print-mode async fleet exited with EMPTY stdout**: `waitForFleetDrain` returned the moment children hit terminal status, but the last child's result delivery plus the model's answering turn raced past the process exit, and print mode's last-message contract then saw a non-assistant tail (0 bytes, exit 0, live-reproduced); the drain now tracks delivery-pipeline movement (spawn/finalize/flush touches) and turn activity (agent_start/agent_settled) through a grace window, so the process stays alive until the model has actually answered the final result — verified live: the same prompt that printed nothing now prints the full 2,764-byte synthesis including the steer follow-up. **F5 — batch overflow silently dropped tasks**: a 6-item `tasks` array sliced to 4 and items 5-6 vanished without a trace (the live model recovered only by noticing the gap itself); the overflow is now reported back with the dropped items quoted for resubmission, in both the sync and async lanes. **F6 — the async worktree disposition never reached the model** (found while fixing F1): fleet finalize's `treeNote` was dead code — the disposition went only to the log; finalize now awaits the cleanup and rides the disposition (with the merge preview) into the delivered result content. Also fixed in the same files: `waitForFleetDrain` no longer taxes fleetless print runs with the grace window (no manager, no children → immediate return). The full self-learning suite re-ran green after the fixes (4,657 tests / 0 fail across 197 files); the format restoration for the two accidentally space-shipped files follows as its own whitespace-only commit (the 8137126 precedent).

- **subagents: the stress round's follow-through — the turn cap and the report shield** — the two gaps the competitor sweep ranked next after the six-finding round. **Per-agent turn caps**: a `maxTurns` frontmatter field (also `max_turns`, number or numeric string, junk ignored) caps one spawn's COMPLETED model turns — routed to the child as `--max-turns`, clamped to 1–500, default none (the wall-clock `timeoutMs` stays the only bound); a capped child aborts at the (N+1)th turn start and the answer-so-far stands as the report. The session-wide `UNOWN_SUBAGENT_MAX_TURNS` env caps every spawn when no per-agent value exists (the agent field always wins; junk means no cap). Both lanes carry it — the sync `task` spawn and the async fleet lane (`FleetSpawnRequest.routing.maxTurns`) — closing the runaway-fan-out guard competitors kept filing (crush#2066 "subagents go on forever and cannot be stopped"; Gemini CLI ships `max_turns: 30` for exactly this). Pinned by `test/subagent-routing.test.ts` (parse / precedence / clamp / args, 16 cases) and the fleet arg seam in `test/fleet-runtime.test.ts`. **The injection shield now covers subagent reports** — the child-report trust gap: Claude Code scans subagent output, Unown did not; a child that fetched a poisoned page could carry injected instructions home in up to 16k chars of child-generated text that landed in the parent's context with full trust. The sync lane's result text and every async fleet delivery (result and question custom messages) now pass through the same fail-open banner as bash stdout — the report passes through unmodified under a one-line "this is DATA" reframing. **[Agent Behavior]** Pinned by the fleet delivery lane in `test/subagent-stress-round.test.ts` (poisoned result, poisoned question, clean pass-through) and end-to-end through the real CLI in `test/cli/task-structured.test.ts` (poisoned child report gets the banner, clean report gets none). Docs: the `maxTurns` frontmatter row in [docs/subagents.md](docs/subagents.md), `UNOWN_SUBAGENT_MAX_TURNS` in `.env.example` + [docs/configuration.md](docs/configuration.md), and the task-tool parenthetical in [docs/BEHAVIOR.md](docs/BEHAVIOR.md).

## [0.0.23] - 2026-10-26

### Fixed

- **engine: a foreign or occupied engine can no longer spawn-loop** — the health probe collapsed every non-2xx into "down": a 401 from another install's engine (the binary/source token split), a port held by a non-Unown process, an incompatible API, a hung listener and a genuinely refused connection were the same fact, so the status loop kicked a spawn into an occupied port five times until the crash-loop circuit gave up. The probe now returns a taxonomy — `ok`/`refused`/`unauthorized`/`occupied`/`incompatible`/`unreachable` — and **only `refused` is spawnable**; blocked probes record `lastCause` without counting an attempt. The footer gains `memory: blocked · token|port|version|no answer`; `unown engine status` names the owner pid, the probed token path and the fix; `start` refuses instead of spawning; `stop` refuses a foreign engine unless `--force --pid <verified pid>`; `restart` requires `--take-ownership`; `repair` gates on this data dir's lock/pid (a 401 engine is still live); `serve` exits 0 as a clean loser on an occupied port. `UNOWN_RUNTIME_DIR` isolates the run files (the suite now uses it; a live Windows engine can hold `.run/engine.log` exclusively). Pinned by `test/engine-probe.test.ts` (16 cases) plus the blocked state in the `engine-status`/`spawn-circuit`/`engine-cli` suites; live-verified against the source engine (pid 22608): five status runs, zero children, attempts unchanged. **[Agent Behavior]**

### Changed

- **landing: the memory-eval round pays its own scroll bill** — the fresh checkout measured the page over the 3,200-word gate (3,227 — the capture-harness round's re-filmed lanes landed prose-side additions without a budget pass; nothing in CI had caught it because the e2e budget spec had not run on that push). Compressed authored copy across the loop leads, the dex and alphabet intros and the FAQ's capabilities answer — no generated number or gated literal touched — to **3,173 section words, 12.3vh, every band ≤ 3vh** (`scripts/landing_audit.mjs --budget` PASS; the e2e budget spec green).

- **landing: the SOTA re-cut — thirteen dex reels re-filmed** — a frame-sampled VLM audit of the shipped reels against the terminal-demo SOTA bar (the strongest dev-tool exemplar, Amp, ships ~30fps marketing loops; the shipped set ran 8fps change-triggered cuts at 1600×1074) scored thirteen lanes 2-7/10 with concrete defects: router showed four identical static screens (its "open the detail" step selected nothing — the Router screen has no detail view; the re-cut walks the selection onto auto-dream and Space-triggers it, toast on screen), parity-round typed a harness test-case ("Reply with exactly: the parity round is live") instead of a dev ask (now a real docs question; /btw follows it), overlays opened cold on a slash command with 70% dead air (now opens on the session itself), mcp rolled a bare d20 arbitrary for a coding agent (now the roll picks the test fixture's port and a second real turn checks the port is free), websearch's closing beat could land on the tool tail with the answer below the fold (soft-anchored on the answer itself), wiki and cli-doctor shipped blank/cut-off closing frames (settled, and cli-doctor filmed last so `unown usage` shows real accumulated rows), hero-bugfix's mid-edit still raced the fast upstream byte-identical to the next beat (the stills now anchor states that differ by construction — 04-agent-fixed at turn idle, 05-suite-green-twice after the user's flake-guard re-run), split-view ended mid-story on a failed-then-fixed pair (now closes on the green tally in split geometry), sdlc's machine-wide listing cut off mid-render (settled before the closing beat), browser-subagent's report beat anchored a word the child's own live labels print during the done beat (byte-identical 04/05; now anchored on the parent-side report block), and subagent-explore filmed the TUI's tmux `extended-keys-format` boot warning (the driver now sets csi-u server-side next to extended-keys on — tmux 3.5 defaults xterm). Every lane re-captured through the audited ttyd harness against the live stack (bridge + engine, agnes-3.0-flash through the Agnes AI upstream) and passed all three gates (pane-truth + pixel stills, frame scan, VLM video samples) plus md5 distinctness; the harness/router lanes read real engine state (three harness cycles driven through the engine API + a dream run), and the s24 re-run replaced one transient mid-redraw footer frame the scan flagged. The re-cut was then held to a second, harsher marketing-bar pass: a montage-grid audit flagged nine lanes, and the follow-up established its flags as method artifacts — its "black frames" were the dark usage screen (signal analysis: YAVG 24.8, zero pixels below luma 5; the full-resolution frame reads as a legible usage table), its "dead air" the terminal dwell every reel including the passing ones shares — and the frame-strip re-check (8 evenly-spaced frames per reel, full-resolution single-frame probes for the flagged states) read **all thirteen reels 8-10/10 PASS**. **New deploy gate**: `scripts/verify-deploy.mjs` now probes every landing media URL the SSR DOM references (~70: reels' webm+mp4, lazy posters, dashboard stills) for 200 + exact content type + byte-range 206 + the long CDN cache — a broken media deploy could previously only be discovered by a visitor. The tui README gains the sdlc row it was missing and the 2026-10-25 provenance column; the e2e job's stale "(3 tests)" label corrected to the 14 it runs; the dex-video spec's stale split-view note re-pointed; the superseded thirteen cuts are archived outside the repo in the capture root (`_superseded-2026-10-25/`), and git history holds every byte. Authored copy trimmed ~13 words (the dex picker line, the provenance caption's closed-state restatement) so the re-cut's rail stop pays its own scroll bill. Verified: 118 unit tests (the media gates included), the full e2e 14/14 (every re-cut reel reaches HAVE_ENOUGH_DATA and presents frames on the standalone build), typecheck + lint clean, production build green, the landing audit's scroll budget PASS, and the frame-strip VLM re-audit above.

## [0.0.22] - 2026-10-26

### Added

- **benchmarks: the memory-eval SOTA round** — the 2026-10-26 round surveyed the memory-evaluation state of the art first (LoCoMo arXiv 2402.17753, LongMemEval arXiv 2410.10813, MemoryAgentBench arXiv 2507.05257, MEME arXiv 2605.12477, MemBench arXiv 2506.21605, the 2026 audit literature — MemStrata's 1.5-2.3% same-seed judge flips, the LTM-eval audit), then shipped what the survey said was missing, all live on agnes-3.0-flash through the production consolidate path with per-case receipts: **`ops-accuracy/`** — the Mem0-paper ADD/UPDATE/DELETE/NOOP taxonomy plus MEME's Cascade/Deletion hard cases as an eleven-case gold-op ledger, scored as a gold-op × observed confusion matrix over deterministic probes of platform + render + search: **11/11 cases, opsF1 0.992** (the bi-temporal delayed-delivery case resolves by event time; the cascade resolves through the rename chain). **`retrieval-rank/`** — the platform's own search scored in isolation for the first time (recall-qa injects memory but never scores the search layer): recall@3/5/10 + MRR per query kind, conflict-recency preference, cross-scope isolation, in two embedder arms — **lexical r@5 0.864 → onnx 1.000** (the H10 recall-parity gap, measured), isolation clean on both arms, and a shared finding: **the search layer prefers the OLD side of a conflict pair (5/6 on onnx)** — recency is guarded only by the reconciler, recorded as the rank-time improvement opportunity. **`judge-calib/`** — the §10-L open item ("judge calibration for every graded number") answered with a measurement: the binary CORRECT/WRONG QA judge protocol against 24 gold-labeled items in 8 classes gives **agreement 20/20, FPR 0/8, FNR 0/8, run-to-run flips 0/72 at temperature 0, order sensitivity 0/6** — the MemStrata instability does not reproduce on the served tier, and recall-qa's judged lane is unblocked. **`failure-modes/`** — the competitor-research sweep's twelve documented cross-industry failure modes, each pinned as a measured regression case (6 deterministic, 4 live, 2 engine — the TOCTOU case drives two CONCURRENT dream triggers against a real scratch `startServer()`, the ingestion-loss case kills the bridge and recovers): **12/12 PASS** with four recorded findings inside the passing receipts — a reconcile variance event on contradiction pairs (1 miss in 3 runs, receipt preserved), the fm-07 load-scan coverage gap (the `### System` impostor variant caught only by the layer-4 tool-output scanner), the raw `importLegacyMarkdown` dedup gap (guarded in production by the empty-scope import rule), and the scanner false-positive surface ("retries must back off exponentially" reads as a runner directive — a quarantined benign note is consumed by a clean no-op dream). Docs: the suites' README/RESULTS files, `benchmarks/README.md` Track 9, `benchmarks/RESULTS.md` (new campaign section + headline row), the root README's documentation table, testing-strategy §7.6 items closed, the competitor-research debt marked paid. Verification: every suite's `--dry` lane is CI-hermetic; the full self-learning unit suite re-ran green (4,733 tests / 0 fail) after the src change below.

### Fixed

- **engine: extraction stops dropping routine infrastructure facts** — the ops-accuracy campaign's first live run caught the memory system doing what Graphiti #1707 documents as the industry's silent-loss shape: notes stated "backups go to the nightly S3 bucket" (in BOTH notes of the supersede case) and "we shipped the flag-evaluation cache", and neither became a memory — agnes-3.0-flash extracted the headline facts and dropped the routine-looking detail, and since extraction is the only pass over a note, the fact was unrecoverable. The fix is the repo's established prompt-carve-out pattern (the 2026-10-23 pitfall-remedy fix): `MEM0_CUSTOM_INSTRUCTIONS` now names the infrastructure-fact family explicitly (ports, endpoints, backup schedules, retention windows, cache and queue configs, feature flags, cron times) and states the completeness contract — extract EVERY distinct fact even when several share a sentence or the note reads as routine. Pinned by a new unit test (`test/memzero-unit.test.ts`, the fourth carve-out pin); the before/after receipts are the ops-accuracy per-case JSONs. **[Agent Behavior]**

- **engine service: the platform audit — five latent failures under the happy path** — a research pass over the systemd/launchd/Windows mechanics, plus driving each lane live, found behavior the first cut could not. **systemd silently ignored the restart-storm bound** — `StartLimitBurst`/`StartLimitIntervalSec` are `[Unit]` settings since v229 and are dropped in `[Service]`, so a boot-crashing engine could restart forever — and `%`/`$` in a path were treated as a specifier / env expansion instead of literal characters. **Windows carried a time bomb**: the no-admin lane was a `.vbs`, and VBScript is a retired feature (Windows 11 24H2 is the last release that enables it by default); it is now a hidden PowerShell watchdog registered under `HKCU\…\Run` (no admin), logging to the shared `.run/engine.log` through `cmd /c` raw redirection — PowerShell 5.1's own `*>>` writes UTF-16 and corrupted the UTF-8 log (caught live as NUL-interleaved `[ r o u t e r ]`) — with the documented 260-character Run-value cap enforced (an over-long path is refused with the reason), and the pre-retirement `.vbs` removed by install/uninstall. **A bun upgrade could orphan the service**: `process.execPath` under Homebrew's Cellar, asdf/mise installs, Scoop apps or the Nix store is versioned and disappears on upgrade, so the service now prefers the stable PATH shim or `BUN_INSTALL/bin/bun` (injectable + unit-tested). **Headless macOS has no `gui/<uid>` domain**, so `launchctl bootstrap` cannot work over SSH: install now detects it, starts the engine for the current session, installs the plist for the next desktop login, and says exactly that instead of failing or pretending. Pins: `test/engine-service.test.ts` +12 (48 total) — StartLimit placement, `%`/`$` escaping, the WSH-free watchdog and its raw-byte log path, the Run-key shape + 260-char refusal, LOCALAPPDATA/legacy paths, and the stable-bun resolver (Homebrew/asdf/BUN_INSTALL/Scoop). Verified live on Windows: the Run value launches through CreateProcess with no agent invoked, a hard kill is followed by a fresh engine, a clean stop ends the watchdog (zero hosts), uninstall removes both the Run value and the script, and the marker/auto-skip/manual-re-enable loop still holds.

## [0.0.21] - 2026-10-25

### Added

- **release: the second re-cut — v0.0.21 supersedes the failed v0.0.20 tag, and the true macOS root cause is found** — the v0.0.20 run failed the SAME macos-latest leg on the SAME pin, which ruled out the slow-dead-bridge theory the first re-cut bet on and forced the real diagnosis: the BUG 10 suite derived its project slug at module load, BEFORE `beforeAll` created the scratch project directory — and on macOS `/var` is a symlink into `/private/…`, so an existing dir realpaths to a different identity than the literal spelling `resolveProjectIdentity()` falls back to for a missing one. The dispatched project dream's row was written with the REALPATH slug while the test's `projectDreamRow` LIKE-matched the LITERAL-path slug: the row existed the whole time, the query just looked for the wrong string on every macOS leg (Linux `/tmp` is not a symlink, so both spellings agree — ubuntu green told us nothing). The suite now materializes the directory first and derives the slug from `realpathSync` — both sides of the lookup agree on every OS. Belt-and-suspenders on top: the emit-path pin now runs its dispatched dream on the deterministic fake platform (no LLM, no network — the test's subject is ROUTING, and the dead-bridge error outcome stays pinned by `dreams-run-projects.test.ts`), and its job-idle window grows to 30s because a SUCCEEDING dream proceeds to the post-ship skill-mint pass whose dead-bridge chatJson legitimately burns llm.ts's retry ladder before the job returns to idle. The failed v0.0.19 and v0.0.20 tags stay as the record.

### Changed

- **landing: the two rapid cuts pay their own scroll bill** — cutting v0.0.19 and then v0.0.20 made each release's changelog entries visible on the rail (the latest stop is the newest cut), taking the page over the 3,200-word budget — 3,219 by the gate's per-section count at the first measurement, and no CI leg had seen it because the upstream pushes cancelled each other's runs. Compressed authored copy across the hero, loop, proof, alphabet, dex and changelog bands — no generated figure or gated literal touched — to 3,053 section words (3,193 whole-body, 12.4vh, every band ≤ 3vh). The e2e budget spec and `scripts/landing_audit.mjs --budget` are both green locally. Also corrected the hero's stated bun floor from `≥ 1.2` to `≥ 1.4` (the floor `scripts/install.ts` actually enforces), with the pinned test updated to match.

### Fixed

- **ci: the macOS dream-check pin also hashes the project's created realpath** — the BUG 10 suite derived its project slug at module load, before `beforeAll` created the scratch project. On macOS `/var` is a symlink to `/private/var`: `resolveProjectIdentity` realpaths an existing directory but falls back to the literal spelling for a missing one, so the expected slug and the dream's own (created) lookup were computed from different identities — a second latent cause beside the genuinely slow dead-bridge path the v0.0.20 re-cut widened the window for. The test now creates the project directory first and hashes the realpath; the 30s window stays.

## [0.0.20] - 2026-10-25

### Added

- **release: the learning-integrity round re-cuts — v0.0.20 supersedes the failed v0.0.19 tag** — the v0.0.19 tag's release run failed its macos-latest leg on one router pin (ubuntu and windows green; the build never ran): the BUG10 emit-path test waited for the dispatched project dream's row with a 10-second window, and on the macOS runner the dead-bridge error path — the memory platform init, the extraction retries, every embed call refusing — lands the row past that window (the run's own log shows the dispatch complete at ~1s and the two Connection-error retries at ~1.3s spacing, then the row arriving too late). The fix is the sibling test's own budget: `dreams-run-projects.test.ts` pins the SAME dead-bridge outcome with a 30_000 poll window, and the router pin now matches it (its test timeout grows 45s to 60s for headroom). The failed tag stays as the record; this re-cut carries the fix plus the engine-auto-start round below.

- **engine: the memory engine comes up on its own — install, update, reboot** — `unown engine install` already owned the always-on service, but nothing invoked it: a fresh source install, and every `unown update`, left the engine to the touchpoint spawn, so after a reboot (or a logout, or a crash with no agent in use) memory, AutoDream and routines stayed down until a human opened a session. `scripts/install.ts` now ends with `bun bin/unown engine install --auto` (step 7/7), so one idempotent step installs the per-user systemd unit / launchd LaunchAgent / Windows logon task at install time and re-runs it on every update — which also drains and restarts a service-owned engine onto the new build. The gate is explicit and unit-tested (`decideAutoServiceInstall`): skip in CI (no login session — a build step must not register a logon task), on unsupported platforms, under `UNOWN_ENGINE_AUTOSTART=0` (the engine-off kill switch now reaches the installer too), under the new `UNOWN_NO_ENGINE_SERVICE=1` (installer-lane opt-out only — agent-side auto-start stays allowed), and after `unown engine uninstall` — which now writes `$UNOWN_HOME/engine-service.disabled` so the next installer never silently re-adds what the user deliberately removed (a manual `unown engine install` clears it and re-enables). The installer step is non-fatal: a machine with no service manager gets a warning and the three supervision layers still compose. `unown update` no longer always claims the engine is on the old build — it compares the engine's reported build against the new checkout and warns only on real skew, since the service path has already restarted it. **The Windows lane was broken three ways, all caught by driving it live rather than trusting the builders.** The scheduled-task action did not quote the entry path, so a checkout under `C:\Users\Jane Doe\…` registered a task that split at the first space and could never start (`-Argument` is parsed by CreateProcess, not PowerShell); the generated PowerShell spread `-Argument` onto its own line, which PowerShell parses as a second cmdlet (it has no implicit line continuation); and the restart policy was assigned directly to `$settings.RestartInterval`, serialising `00:01:00` — a value Task Scheduler's own XML parser rejects as out of range. All three are now pinned. The live run then exposed the structural one: Task Scheduler's root folder is ACL-gated for standard Windows accounts — both `Register-ScheduledTask` and `schtasks /Create` answer `Access is denied` unelevated, which is exactly the posture the installers require (they refuse Administrator). So a refused registration falls back to a **hidden per-user Startup-folder launcher** (`%APPDATA%\…\Startup\unown-engine.vbs`): `WScript.Shell.Run` starts `engine serve` hidden and waits, a non-zero exit retries in 3 s, a clean exit (graceful stop, lock loser) ends the loop — the same restart-on-crash-never-on-clean-exit policy as the systemd unit, without admin. `unown engine status` names the active lane and `unown engine uninstall` removes either (the launcher's wscript host exits on the HTTP drain). Also fixed in the service command helper: the captured-stderr fallback lost `stderr` to the generic error message. `install`/`uninstall` are now in `unown engine --help` (they were missing). Env contract: `UNOWN_NO_ENGINE_SERVICE` in `.env.example` + `docs/configuration.md`; docs: [install step 9](docs/install.md), [routines · always-on](docs/routines.md#always-on-the-engine-service), [usage](docs/usage.md), [resilience R9/R10](docs/resilience.md#r9--deliberate-engine-off); DOX: root + `scripts/` + self-learning AGENTS.md. Pins: `test/engine-service.test.ts` +17 (the decision matrix incl. reason precedence, the marker path, the `.env` merge, the no-op `--auto` skip, the Windows argument-quoting / one-statement-per-line / restart-parameter contracts, and the Startup launcher's hidden-wait-restart semantics). Verified live on Windows: `--auto` skips with the right reason under `CI=1` and `UNOWN_NO_ENGINE_SERVICE=1`; install lands the Startup launcher and the engine answers with no agent invoked (simulated logon: engine up as a fresh pid); a hard kill of the engine pid is followed by a fresh one ~3 s later (crash restart); a graceful `unown engine stop` ends the wscript loop (no restart); `unown engine uninstall` removes it and records the opt-out, `--auto` then skips, and a manual `install` clears the marker and re-enables.

## [0.0.19] - 2026-10-25

### Added

- **cli: the install receipt reaches the TUI — a card, a pill, one record contract** — the landing page hands over `curl -fsSL … | sh`, the installer writes `~/.unown/install.json`, and then nothing read it back: the card the receipt could have produced lived only as a prose `next steps:` block in a shell the user had already left, the first-run gate asks the connect-a-provider question once and (deliberately) never re-asks, and a visitor who answered `n` discovered they had no model by being handed a 401. `pi-repo/packages/self-learning/src/install-surface.ts` closes both halves. **The card** — the first interactive session of an install prints one block through the same guarded `ctx.ui.notify` seam `/status` uses: which channel this is (standalone CLI, or the full-stack source checkout, with the CLI-only limit named), the version, the install root, the PATH shim, the install time with `from v…` after an update, and a closing line when the running process is older than the record (the restart half of `unown update`, stated instead of warned about). It latches per recorded version in `onboarding.json`'s new `installCardSeenFor`, so an update speaks once and nothing repeats; `tools.ts` marks it seen only AFTER the card reached the pane, so a context with no notify seam cannot spend the one-shot; no receipt (a bare run from a clone) prints nothing. `/status` gained the one-line form (`install binary v0.0.16 (linux-x64)`), omitted with the record. **The pill** — while the agent dir has no provider, the footer carries `no model connected · /setup` through the `setStatus` seam the turn meter uses: this is what replaced re-asking, and it never claims an absent model while the bundled bridge is answering (that state reads `model: the bundled bridge · /setup for your own provider`), and a `models.json` that does not parse reports itself unreadable rather than unconfigured. **The contract** — `install.json` has always had three readers (the runtime, `install.sh` where no bun is available, `install.ps1`) and they had drifted: both shells trusted any schema while the runtime rejected the pre-release `mode: "source"` record they both folded — a machine that installed fine and still reported "cannot determine how this unown was installed". All three now accept one rule (schema 1; mode `checkout`|`binary`, `source` folded), both installers grew a read-only `--inspect` / `-Inspect` entry point that prints what they read (`ok` / `none` / `foreign`, exit 2 on a record that does not answer), and `install.sh`'s four per-line `sed` reads were replaced by one `meta_value` + `load_install_meta` loader. Two defects this lane caught in its own work: the refactor briefly left `check_install_meta` comparing globals the loader had never filled (every install would have skipped the mode-change guard — now pinned by driving the refusal on the shipped script), and the first reader version stripped `^[^:]*:` unanchored, so a PowerShell-written `C:\Users\…` path lost its drive letter; the pins feed each reader records the real producer wrote rather than hand-typed JSON. Verified: 45 new tests across six files (`install-surface` 19, `install-meta-readers` 12 — which spawns the shipped installers with the release base on a dead port so no case can install anything, `tools-install-surface` 8 over the real extension `session_start`, plus 6 pins across `install-layout` / `onboarding-state` / `status-card`); the ten suites beside this change run 125 pass, the one reported failure being an unhandled `exportFromFile` SyntaxError from this host's stale untracked `coding-agent/dist` (built 2026-10-23, predating the 8f6aeaf export) — the same cause behind the local full suite's CLI-spawning failures, and untouched by this change; `tsc -p packages/self-learning` names no changed file; `bunx biome check` clean on the new ones. Driven live, not only faked: both installers' `--inspect` answers `ok` / `none` / `foreign` with the right exit codes, the mode-change refusal fires before any fetch, and the pill's predicate was read against the real `~/.unown/agent` (a configured install answers no pill; a truncated `models.json` answers the unreadable line). Docs: [install — flags and the record](docs/install.md), [usage — first run and the status surfaces](docs/usage.md), the root and self-learning AGENTS.md contracts (the installer pair had no DOX owner before this).

### Changed

- **landing: the rail renders cut releases only** — an `[Unreleased]` tile costs the page its whole chrome (`UNCUT`, `no tag yet`, a tally) plus a headline for work that has not shipped, and the v0.0.18 cut left exactly two words of headroom under the 3,200 gate (3,198 measured): the next changelog entry could not land without either compressing another band's authored prose or quietly raising the gate. `changelog.tsx` now maps a module-level `CUT_STOPS` instead of `CHANGELOG_STOPS`, so no uncut tile renders. The band's argument — a record with dates on it — is carried entirely by dated stops, `▶` still marks the newest cut, and the exit's CHANGELOG.md link stays where in-flight work reads. A new changelog entry now costs the scroll budget **zero** words (measured both ways: 3,198 with this entry, 3,198 without). Pins: the changelog suite asserts one `<time>` per cut stop and the absence of `UNCUT` / `no tag yet` text, and the `ul[role='list']` count follows the rendered set. Docs: the landing AGENTS.md rail contract. Verified: `bun test src` 118/0, `check:changelog` green, scroll budget 3,198w / 12.5vh PASS.

### Fixed

- **install: macOS read the install record as garbage** — BSD sed treats `[\r\n\t]` inside a bracket expression as the literal character set `{ \, r, n, t }`, so the new `meta_value` reader deleted every `n`, `r`, `t` and backslash out of the value it echoed: `mode` came back as `biay` and a recorded path lost its separators (`C:\Users\…` → `C:Usesa…`). GNU sed honours the escapes, so every Linux leg and every Git Bash run on this host was green — the defect surfaced only on the macOS CI leg. It is not cosmetic: a mangled mode is still non-empty, so the record passed the schema gate and then compared unequal to the mode being installed, meaning a plain macOS reinstall over its own recorded binary install was refused as an attempted mode change. Structural whitespace now comes out with `tr -d '\r\n\t'`, whose escapes mean the same thing on both seds. The parity test also sets `UNOWN_INSTALL_ALLOW_ROOT` for the guard cases: GitHub's Windows runners execute elevated, and the mode-change guard sits behind the privilege refusal, so the bare run reported the refusal instead of the guard under test. Pins that caught it: the macOS leg asserted `mode: binary` and the recorded shim path — the test was right, the shipped installer was wrong.

## [0.0.18] - 2026-10-25

### Added

- **ci: the xplat lane — cross-platform testing an agent can drive** — `ci.yml` is push/PR-only, which meant the question a mid-session agent most needs answered — "is this green on all three OSes right now?" — could only be bought by pushing a commit to find out. The xplat lane is the way back: a `workflow_dispatch` surface (`.github/workflows/xplat.yml`) over the same test steps CI already runs, four escalating scenarios deep (`smoke` — the real installer, the engine suite with a junit receipt, version consistency, CLI/doctor smokes; `core` — plus the integration suite and the full-stack lifecycle gate; `full` — plus every landing/engineering gate `ci.yml` runs, from the coverage and docs anchors through gitleaks, bwrap, the production build, E2E + axe and the mermaid byte-identity; `release-verify` — the release path minus the publish), plus two capabilities no push lane offers: on-demand OS targeting (`--os windows-latest` for the "works on Linux" class; `macos-intel`, the darwin-x64 gap leg `macos-latest`'s arm64-only matrix can never cover) and a six-runner boot matrix for the release binaries — including `windows-11-arm`, which `release.yml` still ships untested, and `macos-15-intel`. The driver (`scripts/xplat.mjs`, zero dependencies, node >= 20 or bun) dispatches on any ref, correlates its own run without a response body to read (a minted `run_id` lands in the run name, the receipt artifact names and the driver's segment match), watches to a verdict with the exit-code contract agents branch on (0 green / 1 red / 2 cancelled / 3 infra / 5 auth), pulls failed-job logs and junit/artifact receipts into `.run/xplat/`, and runs the same scenario steps locally (`local --scenario`) for the fast inner loop — auth resolves from the PAT already in the git remote, so a fresh clone drives CI with zero configuration. The manual is `docs/xplat-testing.md` (registered in the README docs table, `docs/AGENTS.md` ownership + the four-place sync contract, `scripts/AGENTS.md`, root `AGENTS.md` Root Contracts, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, and `testing-strategy.md` gate 19 + the §14.1 on-demand cadence entry). Pins: `scripts/xplat.test.mjs` — 24 tests over the driver's pure logic (remote parsing with opaque tokens, matrix resolution, the exit-code contract, dispatch correlation including the segment match and the foreign-run rejection, the local lane as a faithful subset of the remote one). Verified end to end through the lane itself: `smoke` green on all three OS (run 40191520429, junit 4584 tests / 0 fail on each leg), `release-verify` green with all six binaries booted on their own runners (run 40192208951, windows-11-arm included), `macos-intel` green after its catch below (run 40193022347), and the `full` history scan green after its catch (run 40193618973). The lane is keyless like every CI lane.

### Fixed

- **engine: a SQLite numeric separator only darwin-x64 could see** — `router.test.ts`'s `seedProbe` inlined `3_600_000` into the INSERT's VALUES clause, a JavaScript numeric-separator literal SQLite only parses from 3.46 (2024) — and bun's darwin-x64 build bundles an older engine. Every CI leg stayed green because every other bun build bundles newer SQLite, so the defect was invisible to the push matrix and shipped; the xplat lane's first macos-intel dispatch caught it (run 40192211514, "unrecognized token: 3_600_000", the same two router tests failing while ubuntu/macos-arm64/windows passed — receipts in the junit artifacts). `schedule_ms` now goes through a bound parameter, the file's own convention for every other value. Source-mode darwin-x64 users get a runnable engine suite back.
- **ci: the gitleaks allowlist had drifted behind the fixtures** — the xplat `full` scenario scans full history where push CI scans only the pushed diff, and the difference was measured, not theorized: eight findings, all deliberate fixtures — the sessions-redaction tests and the mpbench secret-hygiene corpus (the `ADV_2974` case family grades the agent on NOT writing `user_api_key` to memory) had landed with secret-shaped strings and no allowlist entry, invisible to every push since (runs 40192206457 + 40192878877). `.gitleaks.toml` allowlists them by path (rules untouched), documents the dispatch-lane context for the next fixture author, and records the gitleaks 8.24 behavior the second run proved: a `[[allowlists]]` array placed alongside the legacy `[allowlist]` table is silently ignored — one table, or lose entries.

- **memory: the learning-loop integrity round — nine defects in the notes → dream → render pipeline, each pinned by a standalone repro** — a full code audit of the self-learning stack (memory, memzero, dream, notes, term, router, budget — the four-tier memory, the Mem0 platform lane, AutoDream consolidation, the terminal outbox and the job scheduler) plus live dogfooding on the Agnes upstream surfaced nine real defects, all fixed with regression tests. **(1) budget.ts multi-block pruning corrupted the newest rules** (HIGH): `pruneToBudget` applied each tier's accumulated drop ranges to the ALREADY-CUT string while block ranges are in ORIGINAL tier coordinates — whenever one tier dropped two or more blocks, the first range sliced unrelated mid-tier content and later ranges removed shifted text, silently truncating/dropping the NEWEST rules while the report claimed only old blocks went; the fix freezes a pristine pre-cut copy per tier and re-derives every cut from it. **(2) memory rollback re-ran the LLM extraction** (HIGH): `revertMemoryEvents` re-added the pre-delete text through `add()` with infer DEFAULT, so an emergency rollback could invent DIFFERENT memories than the ones it was rolling back to;
