the re-add now rides the verbatim `infer:false` lane (a new id — mem0 keeps the old id's history, so the audit trail survives the revert). **(3) the salvage lane — a blank extraction was a dead end**: when a note's extraction round came back empty, the dream recorded nothing and the note stayed undreamed-forever (a deterministic failure loop for scaffolding-heavy notes); notes now get one compact governed extraction whose memories insert verbatim, and a total miss is signaled on the bus instead of looping. **(4) a partial embedder reindex was recorded as complete**: the backend marker flipped even when `reindexMemories` caught its own errors and returned a PARTIAL count — a silently mixed vector space (old-backend rows + new-backend rows, never retried, permanently degraded recall); the marker now flips only on a full pass and `mem0:reindex-partial` makes the degradation observable. **(5) the 40-note cap starved busy projects**: one noisy project could fill the whole undreamed window; terminal notes are now consumed per-project (oldest first per `project_path`, the window-function shape) so every project's lessons eventually consolidate. **(6) pathless legacy terminal notes were written into the engine's CWD**; they are now consumed and routed, not dumped. **(7) the >20k double-delivery**: the outbox file and the ingest stored differently-truncated copies of the same note, so the drain's exact-content dedup missed and the note was ingested twice; both sides now share one codepoint-safe cap helper so the bytes match. **(8) dream-check fired on mixed note kinds**: the trigger counted benchmark + terminal notes together, so terminal-only accumulation ran `runDream()` below its own threshold and manufactured a junk `no-notes` dreams row once per finished run; the count is kind-aware, and terminal-note pressure routes to the project dream lane (the lane that actually consumes terminal notes). **(9) surrogate-pair splits at every cap boundary**: a plain `slice` at a 200/300-unit cap could orphan a high surrogate at the boundary (mojibake in the note's first astral character); all caps now back off to a well-formed codepoint boundary. Verified: the full self-learning suite 4,653 tests / 0 fail (26 new pins across budget/notes/outbox/router/memzero-salvage), each defect reproduced standalone before its fix, and the live engine re-driven end-to-end on Agnes (dream consolidation, note consumption and memory injection all green).
- **mint: the solvability gate — an impossible task can no longer enter the suite as a "capability eval"** — live dogfood finding: the minted task `mint-fix-drop-right-while` shipped a test file whose line 14 (`dropRightWhile([1,2,3,4], v => v % 2 === 0)` must equal `[1,2]`) contradicts lines 13/15/17/18/19 under EVERY consistent semantics — verified empirically by running an independent correct implementation against the file (it fails line 14; no implementation can satisfy all fourteen assertions at once). The agent correctly refused and asked for a one-character test fix; the pre-gate pipeline treated "agent fails it" as proof of a good capability eval and activated the task anyway — and since `pickNextTask()` selects failing tasks FIRST, the impossible task burned one full agent run on EVERY cycle, forever, starving every real learning target (plus a failure note per run polluting the dream input). The fix is a solvability proof at the activation gate: the draft contract now carries a `reference` — the solved form of the non-test source files — and `proveSolvability()` runs it against the task's own verify in a sandbox before activation; a draft without a reference gets ONE bounded authored-reference call, and a task whose reference cannot pass its own tests is discarded with the failing log as the reason (`task:mint-unsolvable` on the bus). Hard-but-solvable tasks keep the capability-eval semantics exactly as before. Behind the gate, a defense-in-depth circuit breaker: a minted capability eval with `UNOWN_CAPABILITY_QUARANTINE_AFTER` consecutive failed baselines (default 6, 0 disables; infra `error` runs break the streak — they say nothing about solvability) and zero passes is quarantined (`active=0`, `status='quarantined'`, `task:quarantined` on the bus) — anything that slips past the gate or degrades later (environment drift, an upstream model change making a once-solvable task unreachable) is deactivated instead of starving the loop forever. Operator lanes for the new state: `POST /api/tasks/:id/quarantine` and `POST /api/tasks/:id/activate` (restoring a quarantined minted task returns it to `capability`), and `GET /api/tasks?all=1` lists inactive rows so the breaker's catches stay visible and reversible. Also fixed while porting the knob: `Number("")` is `0`, not NaN — an EMPTY `UNOWN_CAPABILITY_QUARANTINE_AFTER` fell back to disabling the breaker entirely (the same trap `MAX_ACTIVE_MINTED` documents); an empty value now falls back to the default. Verified live on the running engine: the contradictory task quarantined through the new endpoint, the default task list immediately excludes it, the next mint job mined the same failure and authored a SOLVABLE variant the agent then passed (honest discard), and the suite's task selection no longer schedules the impossible task; 26 new unit tests pin the reference-lane validation (files-subset, no-test-files, caps, empty/shape refusals), the gate's five decision paths (draft-pass, draft-fail, authored-pass, authored-fail, junk/unprovable), and the breaker's streak honesty (error/pass breaks, origin/status/pass-count gates, idempotence, the knob including the empty-string trap), suite 4,653 / 0.

### Changed

- **landing: the bar says one thing — four argument links, four record links behind "More"** — the header carried eight labels, two icon actions and a gold CTA in one row, and the crowding was measured, not felt: the row filled exactly at 1024px, which is why the labels had to step down to 12.5px below 1100px and why `Get started` read as the ninth link rather than the one ask. `NAV_LINKS` becomes `NAV_ENTRIES` with a `group`: the four that carry the argument (How it learns, Capability dex, Proof, FAQ) stay in the bar at the full 13px, and the four record surfaces (Dashboard, Alphabet, Changelog, Blog) fold into a new `NavMore` disclosure — link-styled, deliberately not `gb-btn-dark`-framed, so the header keeps exactly one filled action and the disclosure cannot compete with it. Below `lg` the section menu still renders all eight flat: a phone visitor gets one menu, not a menu inside a menu. The wordmark loses its `self-improving` tagline (the H1 says it, and a logo group carrying name + stage + category was the other half of the crowding) and keeps the beta badge. The disclosure follows the WAI-ARIA pattern the section menu already holds — `aria-expanded` + `aria-controls`, a `hidden`-toggled panel rather than a conditional so `aria-controls` always resolves and the links stay in the server render for crawlers and `verify-deploy` — closing on Escape (focus back on the trigger), on a jump, on a pointer-down outside, and on focus leaving. Docs: the landing AGENTS.md chrome contract + a new Work Guidance invariant (the header carries one filled action; new entries default to `more`). Verified: 118 unit tests green (two new shell tests pin the bar's four primaries and the panel's four record entries in order, that nothing framed sits inside the nav row, and the disclosure's whole contract — closed-and-out-of-the-tree, opens, every entry reachable, Escape returns focus to the trigger, a jump closes it, a pointer-down outside closes it), typecheck + lint clean, production build + full e2e 14/14 with axe-clean in both motion passes, scroll budget 12.5vh and 3,164 words against the 3,200 gate, and the five data gates green (`check:changelog` 17 stops / 281 entries, `claims` 63 literals, `benchmarks`, `blog`, `docs`). The open panel is invisible to the standing scans, so it was audited separately: 8.82:1 dark / 9.48:1 light, zero axe violations in both schemes. The bar was measured at 1024 / 1100 / 1280 / 1440 — no overflow anywhere and 224px of slack between the wordmark and the links at the `lg` floor, where the eight-label bar measured exactly full — and the three tracked OG images regenerated through `scripts/landing_og_shots.mjs`, since the header is in their frame.

## [0.0.17] - 2026-10-25

### Fixed

- **windows: the engine auto-start popped a "Windows cannot find '\C:\...\unown.exe\'" dialog** — every engine auto-spawn on a Windows binary install (and `unown setup`'s first health ping, which triggers one) showed a GUI error instead of starting the engine. Root cause: the detach spawn hands `cmd /c start /b "" "<exe>"` to the runtime with the executable ALREADY quoted, but without `windowsVerbatimArguments` — so Node/Bun's default Windows arg escaping re-escaped the embedded quotes into `\"C:\...\unown.exe\"`, a form cmd.exe does not understand (it has no backslash escaping). `start` consumed the quotes as delimiters and ShellExecute'd the residue — `\C:\...\unown.exe\`, the exact path in the dialog. The argv is now built by `buildWindowsEngineSpawnSpec` (pure, pinned): the SS64-correct empty-title slot, a once-quoted executable and `windowsVerbatimArguments: true` — the same fix `stdioSpawnSpec` (MCP stdio) shipped first. The monitor tool's win32 spawner carried the same latent class (any quoted Windows command — `type "C:\Program Files\..."` — would run the wrong thing); it now goes through `windowsMonitorSpawnSpec`, the `cmd /d /s /c` + outer-quote + verbatim shape. Pins in `test/windows-spawn-verbatim.test.ts` — including the regression proof that reproduces the dialog's path from the default escaper's output — 7 tests, suite 4584/0, tsc at baseline.

## [0.0.16] - 2026-10-25

### Added

- **landing: the blog — the longhand record of each feature and release** — the changelog rail says WHAT shipped; nothing on the site said how a feature works or why it exists. The blog (at `/blog`, one static route per post) fills that lane, built on the house generator pattern rather than a CMS: posts are markdown under `content/blog/` (strict frontmatter subset — title/description band/date/lastmod/tags/type/draft/media, every rule validated loudly: unknown keys fail, descriptions outside 70–160 fail, non-dated slugs fail, dead media paths fail), `scripts/landing_blog.mjs` (the one landing generator with a dependency — `marked`, dev-only) renders them into generated `src/components/blog/blog-data.ts` + the static RSS `public/feed.xml`, and `bun run check:blog` (new CI gate in the landing job) fails a build where either drifted. **The pages**: `/blog` (list-with-dates index — the Tailwind/leerob shape the field survey settled on), `/blog/[slug]` (static via generateStaticParams + `dynamicParams = false` — no request-time rendering anywhere; per-post `generateMetadata` with canonical/article OG, a server-rendered BlogPosting JSON-LD node, prev/next links), and a colocated `opengraph-image.tsx` drawing each post's 1200×630 social card through `next/og` in the landing palette. **The chrome**: `BlogNav` (compact header — wordmark home, beta badge, theme toggle; the seven in-page anchors stay on the landing page) + the landing `Footer` with a new `anchorPrefix` prop so its in-page anchors resolve from `/blog/*`; the blog layout imports `landing.css` (one design system, not a second one) and carries the RSS autodiscovery alternate; blog pages ship ZERO framer-motion so a no-JS visitor gets the complete article without a noscript restore. **The media answer**: `content/blog/TEMPLATE.md` (the authoring template with the per-type media checklist — feature posts carry a capture or stills from the audited `public/landing/tui/**` reels, release posts stay media-light linking the GitHub release, announcements ride the generated social card) and every post's `media:` frontmatter is existence-validated at generation. **Three seed posts**: the beta announcement, the tiered-memory feature post (embedding the real `memory-learn` reel + stills — reuse-first), and the v0.0.15 release post. **Access points**: the nav's one non-anchor entry ("Blog", path-refs through next/link), the footer's eighth link (the grid was seven of eight cells for a year), and the changelog rail's exit inset — the three "Blog" words cost 3 of the 4 words of headroom the beta round left, paid back by compressing the rail's exit caption (−3 authored words; no generated number or gated literal touched). **Sitemap + SEO surface**: `/sitemap.xml` now carries the pages that exist — landing, `/blog`, one URL per post (lastmod per post's own `lastmod ?? date`); `e2e/seo.spec.ts`'s one-loc pin became `2 + BLOG_TOTAL_POSTS`, read off the generated data; tag pages are a deliberate non-feature while the post count is single-digit (thin-content pages, not discovery). Verified: `node --test scripts/landing_blog.test.mjs` (9 pins — the grammar, the bands, the slug rule, drafts, reading time, feed escaping), 115 unit tests green (the new blog-pages suite: index order, meta rows, JSON-LD, prev/next, 404, static params, metadata), typecheck/lint clean, production build prerenders `/blog` + 3 posts + 3 OG cards as SSG, `bunx playwright test` green incl. the new `e2e/blog.spec.ts` (index, feed parseability + escaping, article SEO, PNG magic bytes, unknown-slug 404), `verify-deploy.mjs` grew the blog section (routes + h1 + feed link + autodiscovery + sitemap entries), and the changed-line coverage gate carries one justified exemption (the OG route — Satori wasm under happy-dom is impossible; e2e pins its output instead). Docs: [docs/blog.md](docs/blog.md) (the authoring manual), [content/AGENTS.md](content/AGENTS.md) + [src/components/blog/AGENTS.md](src/components/blog/AGENTS.md) (the new DOX), [landing-research.md — the blog](docs/landing-research.md#2026-10-25--the-blog-the-longhand-record), the root/src/scripts/docs AGENTS.md contracts, the README docs table.
- **landing: the beta mark — the page says plainly what stage the product is in** — the landing page never stated its maturity: a first-time visitor met full confidence copy with no signal that the release line is 0.0.x and formats still move. The status now ships as the pattern the surveyed field converged on (Vercel/Figma: a small badge next to the product name; Vercel feature pages + Raycast: one plain-language sentence next to the H1): **`BetaBadge`** — one bordered pixel chip in the console grammar (square corners, `gb-label`, gold on panel, plain text a screen reader announces, non-interactive) — with three homes: the header wordmark (next to "unown", visible at every breakpoint), the hero kicker (badge + the existing category line), and the footer ("Currently in beta." under the description). The plain-language half lives in the hero: "Unown is in beta — expect rough edges and moving formats; report what breaks." Wording settled against the ladder the research produced (private beta → public beta → beta → preview → early access): plain "beta", because the product is open, free, ungated — "preview" is the Microsoft-style softening and "early access" implies a waitlist this project does not run; the claim is defensible by the page's own honesty rule (0.0.x version line, a changelog that records format changes, issues as the reporting lane). Budget accounted: +18 words on a 66-word headroom, 12.5vh of 13vh; OG shots regenerated for the changed hero. Verified: `bun test src` green (new pins: the badge pair in the hero suite, the wordmark + footer note in the shell suite), typecheck + lint unchanged, the palette gate green (no new hex — the chip reuses tokens), the budget gate green, and `verify-deploy.mjs` now asserts both halves of the pair on the live deployment. Docs: [landing-research.md — the beta mark](docs/landing-research.md#2026-10-25--the-beta-mark-one-status-three-homes), the landing AGENTS.md chrome contract.
- **tui: OSC 7501 program status — the terminal knows what the agent is doing** (the 2026-10 convergence round, part 1/7) — the freshest cross-agent convergence found in the reverse-engineering sweep: Claude Code shipped it (v2.1.295, 2026-10-08), Crush followed (v0.98.0, 2026-10-08), Goose landed it the same month (v1.56.0, 2026-10-23) — Mitchell Hashimoto's Program Status Protocol (mitchellh.com/writing/program-status-osc7501, rev 0.4). Unown's TUI already *parsed* the protocol's feature-detection reply (`tui/src/stdin-buffer.ts` tolerated `7501;?` among XTVersion/Kitty replies) but never *emitted* — the half every 2026 terminal (Rex, Ghostty) actually consumes. Now the session lifecycle reports itself: `idle` on boot, `working` with the prompt preview when a turn starts, `blocked kind=question` while the ask_user_question dialog waits, `idle` with the answer preview at turn end, `clear` on shutdown (the spec's working/blocked-must-not-outlive-us rule). Spec-exact emitter (`src/osc-status.ts`): base64 msg/title with the 2048/192 decoded and 2732/256 encoded caps, id-path segment rules, the 4096-byte whole-sequence ceiling, TTY-only writes, `PROGRAM_STATUS=0` + `UNOWN_OSC7501=0` opt-outs, control-characters-become-spaces sanitization (deleting them would join words). Tests: `test/osc-status.test.ts` pins the grammar byte-for-byte, the limits, the gating and the lifecycle helpers.
- **tui: the turn meter — per-turn cost on screen, not three commands deep** (2/7) — per-turn token/cost visibility became table stakes without Unown noticing: Goose ships a per-message tokens/cost/TTFT row since v1.42 (Aug 2026), Gemini CLI's `/stats` grew model + tool subviews, Claude Code keeps context % and cost in the status line at all times. Unown had every *number* (`/context`, `/usage`, `/session`) but no live line — you ran a command to see what the last turn cost. Now every `turn_end` sets the footer chip **`turn 3.4s · ctx 62% · in 45.2k out 1.1k (cached 42k) · ~$0.0084`** through pi's `setStatus` seam (the same joined row the goal chip uses): turn duration, live context %, the /usage session-rollup attribution, and the usage-cli price model (`UNOWN_PRICE_<MODEL>_IN/_OUT` overrides; an unknown model's price is never invented — the segment drops). `UNOWN_TURN_METER=0` off. Tests: `test/turn-meter.test.ts` (gate, compact formatters, cost arithmetic, assembled chip).
- **agent: the monitor tool — watch a process, get its lines back** (3/7) — Claude Code's Monitor tool (Oct 2026) lets the agent react to what a process *prints* (a dev server's ready line, a watcher's error, a CI tail) instead of guessing from one-shot exit codes. Ported to pi's request/response tool seams honestly: `monitor` runs a command for a **bounded window** (default 30s, cap 120s), captures each stdout/stderr line with timestamps, and returns them as one tool result — ending on window expiry, the line cap (default 200, cap 2000), the process exiting, a `stop_pattern` regex match (the "listening on|ready in" case), or session abort; the result tail-caps at 200 lines so a `yes`-style pipe can never balloon the context (the F10 bounded-posture rule); the child gets SIGTERM then SIGKILL. Guardrails: the **bash deny-list applies to watched commands exactly as to one-shot bash** (exfiltration/destructive shapes refused pre-spawn), and plan mode allows only the read-only allowlist. Tests: `test/monitor.test.ts` (param clamps, all four stop reasons via an injected spawner, deny gate, rendering).
- **review: team checks — the review policy rides with the repo** (4/7) — Goose v1.40's `goose review` reads team checklists from `.agents/checks/*.md` and scope rules from `REVIEW.md`, each check its own model/tool policy; Unown's `/review` was prompt-only. Now `/review` discovers **`.unown/review/checks/*.md`** (project — versioned with the repo) and **`~/.unown/agent/review/checks/*.md`** (personal, every project), plus the shared context files `.unown/review/scope.md` / `<agentDir>/review/scope.md`. Check frontmatter: `title`, `severity` (P0–P3 floor, default P2), `model` (per-check reviewer override — the Goose per-check model parity, cloned onto the read-only reviewer subagent); the body instructs the reviewer. Each selected check runs as one more parallel subagent beside the built-in focuses, findings merge into the existing P0–P3 + SHIP pipeline, project checks win name collisions. New flag surface (Goose parity): **`--severity P1`** (display floor), **`--checks a,b`** (run only these), **`--dry-run`** (print the reviewer plan), **`--list-checks`**, alongside the existing base/PR/range/`--comment`. Tests: `test/review-policy.test.ts` (both lanes, frontmatter, collisions, flags, severity filter, plan renderer, check-driven prompts).
- **tui: the session recap — what happened while you were away** (5/7) — Claude Code's returning-user parity line: away ≥3 minutes → one-line recap on return, `/recap` on demand; Unown had auto-titles and cross-session `/insights` but no at-the-moment summary. The auto-recap renders on the first prompt after a real idle gap (default 3m, `UNOWN_RECAP_IDLE_MINUTES`, 0 kills): a **deterministic digest line** through the notify pane — the span, the shape of the missed work (turns, tool calls, files touched) and the last answer preview — instant, no LLM on the prompt path. **`/recap [turns]`** is the on-demand fuller form: the digest plus a one-sentence model summary through the same side-question lane `/btw` uses (never the transcript, the receipts ledger or AutoDream's mining), degrading to the digest when the model lane is down. Tests: `test/recap.test.ts` (threshold, digest facts, welcome-home line, question shape).
- **engine: idle compaction — a parked heavy session compacts itself before you return** (6/7) — Claude Code v2.1.299 (Oct 2026): a 200K-token conversation idle ~54 minutes is compacted before the 1h prompt cache dies — the return never eats the failure, and the summarization happens while nobody waits. Unown had threshold-based auto-compact on `turn_end` only; an idle session never compacted. Now the first prompt after a real gap checks the curve (`shouldIdleCompact`): **idle ≥ 45m (default) AND usage ≥ 50% → `ctx.compact()` fires before the request ships**, with the plain-language notice ("older turns were summarized while you were away…") and the tuning knobs named in it (`UNOWN_IDLE_COMPACT_MINUTES`, `UNOWN_IDLE_COMPACT_MIN_USAGE`; minutes=0 is the kill switch). The gap is consumed by the first prompt, so queued follow-ups never double-fire the window. Tests: `test/auto-compact-idle.test.ts` (the curve, the floor, the kill switch, env overrides, the notice).
- **cli: print mode never goes silent — stall progress on stderr** (7/7) — a live dogfood finding from this round's own verification: the Agnes flash upstream intermittently answers 200 streams with **zero content** (usage-log receipts: two 0-token completions), the client-side retries are silent, and `unown -p` sat quiet for its whole 180s stall budget in four consecutive fresh runs — indistinguishable from a hang to every unattended caller (CI, engine automations, a human at a terminal). Codex exec's JSONL lane and Claude Code's headless mode both assume silence is never the only signal; now the print watchdog emits **one stderr line at 50/75/90% of the stall budget** ("no session events for 90s — still waiting (stall guard at 180s…)"), each milestone at most once per stall window, stdout untouched, `PI_PRINT_STALL_PROGRESS=0` silences. Tests: `test/cli-stall-progress.test.ts` (the pure stepper: order, once-each, full-window simulation, the zero-budget guard).
- **cli: the stream-json alias + the unknown-flag guard** — the sweep's third convergence: every headless-first competitor (Claude Code, Codex `--json`, Gemini, Cursor, Amp `--stream-json`) exposes an NDJSON event stream, and unown already had one (`--mode json`, pi's JSONL events) under a different spelling — **`--output-format stream-json`** now maps onto it, so scripts written against those CLIs work verbatim (the `--output-schema` refusal composes through the same `jsonMode` key). And the same silent-misroute class the bare-typo guard refuses, fixed for flags: `unown --thinking high` used to send the literal words "--thinking high" to the model as prompt text (measured; R7's audit finding); now any unrecognized dash-token **exits 2 naming the flag** — with the standard **`--` end-of-options marker** so a prompt that itself starts with a dash stays spellable, and the fleet's spawn surface pinned green against the guard (the children spawn `--tools/--session/--model/--effort/--mcp-servers/--no-agents-md`, all accepted). Tests: four new pins in `test/cli/parse-args.test.ts` (the alias's jsonMode mapping, the schema-conflict contract, the guard's exit 2, the `--` escape, the fleet-surface acceptance); verified live: the guard refuses `--thinking` with exit 2, the alias streams the same NDJSON as `--mode json`, and `unown -- "explain what --verbose means"` answers through the escape. Docs: [usage — print mode](docs/usage.md#one-shot-headless-p-runs), `cli.ts` HELP.
- **engine: the visibility pair — `/status` and `/diff`** — the sweep's second convergence: Codex ships `/status` and `/diff`, Claude Code assembles the same facts across `/status` + `/model` + `/cost`, Crush renders inline diffs — unown had every FACT (version, model, thinking level, permission mode, engine health, worktree state, git branch, memory stack) but no one-glance assembly: "what am I running right now" needed three commands, and `/commit` existed with no way to SEE the tree before committing. `pi-repo/packages/self-learning/src/status-card.ts` registers both: **`/status`** renders the card (version + sha, session id/name/counts, model + provider + thinking, permission mode with config/session source + add-dirs, memory tiers with injected chars, engine up/down, the `--worktree` tree when the session runs inside one, git branch + modified/untracked), and **`/diff`** shows the working tree (change count, untracked list, staged+unstaged unified diff capped at 200 lines with a pointer, `--stat` for the compact view; clean and off-repo states named plainly). Everything is read-only — git through the hardened `runGit` read-only lane, the engine through the status snapshot (never a live probe on the hot path), memory through the terminal resolver. The pure renderer takes every input injectably, so `test/status-card.test.ts` (8 tests) pins the card's every line + fallback, the git collectors against a real repo, and both commands end to end against a fake pi ctx. Verified live in a real TUI session (PTY capture): the card renders `unown v0.0.15 (8f6aeaf) / session id 01a1702b · 2 message(s) / model agnes-3.0-flash via zai-bridge / permissions default / memory guide + policy + project + user + local · ~7,000 chars injected / engine up (v0.0.15) / git main · 7 modified, 2 untracked (/diff to view)` — three live-caught rendering defects (an unversioned `unownVersion()` path, the `EngineBuildRef` object printed as `v[object Object]`, and memory tiers read off a nonexistent `name` field) fixed before ship. Docs: [usage — the session commands](docs/usage.md#session-commands), `cli.ts` HELP; the /status card rides the live permission gate + `sessionWriteRoots()` wired through `bootRuntime`.
- **sessions: the completion round — bare `--resume` picker, delete/rename, HTML export** — the 2026-10-25 reverse-engineering sweep (Claude Code v2.1.310, Codex 0.164, Gemini CLI 0.64, OpenCode V1/V2, Crush, aider, goose, Amp, Cursor CLI — reports under `benchmarks/research/2026-10-25/`) converged on one finding: every competitor lets you *pick* a session at launch and *manage* the lifecycle afterward, while unown could only ever create sessions. Four gaps closed. **(1) Bare `unown --resume`** — with no argument, an interactive TTY opens pi's own launch-time session selector over BOTH unown stores (this project's `~/.unown/agent/sessions` lane, cwd-filtered like `-c`, unioned with the project's `.unown/sessions` imports — the same both-stores rule the rest of the resume lane obeys); cancel exits 0 (`claude --resume` / `codex resume` parity), and in a pipe it is refused with exit 2 pointing at `--resume <id|name>` and `-c`. The picker is pi's `selectSession` scaffolding, exported from the vendored package for exactly this (`packages/coding-agent/src/index.ts` gains `selectSession` + `exportFromFile` exports — wiring, no behavior change upstream). **(2) `unown sessions delete <id|name> [--force]` / `rename <id|name> <new-name>`** — the missing lifecycle half: resolution reuses the `--resume` grammar (path → id/prefix → latest `session_info` name) but STRICTER, because these are destructive: every multi-match refuses and lists candidates, including the same-named duplicates resume deliberately resolves newest-wins. Delete guards a file modified in the last 30 s as "probably a live session" (its next append would recreate a header-less orphan) — `--force` is the informed override. Rename appends a `session_info` entry with pi's exact semantics (sanitized name, collision-free id, `parentId` = the leaf — `sessions.ts:appendSessionInfoEntry`), so `unown --resume <new-name>` resolves immediately; it is the offline lane for what `/name` does live. **(3) `unown sessions list` now shows the session name** (auto-title or `/name`) — a titled session is addressable by delete/rename/resume, and the list says so. **(4) `unown sessions export --format html`** — pi's own themed, XSS-hardened `/export` renderer over an offline session file (the Claude Code `/export` shape): a standalone self-styled page, landing by default beside the md/json exports in `<cwd>/.unown/sessions/`. And `sessions export <id|name>` itself now resolves through the SAME grammar as delete/rename (id, id-prefix or name; multi-match refuses) — found live: the old lane matched filenames only, so a session you could resume/delete/rename by name was not exportable by that name. The secret redaction runs over the raw JSONL *before* rendering — a post-pass would be leaky, because the renderer syntax-highlights message text and a secret can be split across `<span>`s (found live: an `sk-…` key that a post-render scrub missed); `--raw` renders verbatim. Also fixed in parseArgs: `--resume` (and `--session`) used to swallow any following single-dash flag as the query value — `unown --resume -p "hello"` looked for a session named "-p" — now any dash-prefixed token means "next flag", the `--worktree` adjacency convention. Tests: `test/sessions-completion.test.ts` (17 tests / 53 assertions: the sentinel on both lanes, conflict exits, the adjacency fix, the rename primitive's shape + sanitization + refusal, delete by id/name/ambiguity/live-guard/--force, rename resolution through `resolveResumeQuery`, the name column, the HTML page's base64 `session-data` blob decoded and the `[REDACTED:*]` marker verified inside it, `--raw` keeping the secret verbatim) plus the 125-test regression sweep over resume/sessions/cli suites green. Docs: [usage — resume flags](docs/usage.md#unown--resume-idname-file), the `sessions` row, `SESSIONS_USAGE`, and `cli.ts` HELP.

- **engine: the startup mark — the Unown eye opens on boot** — the TUI's first screen was four lines of text with roughly a third of a 36-row pane left empty above `Context`, while the landing page carries a hand-drawn 28-form script whose signature is the eye. The terminal agent now opens with that mark: the eye draws its rim, the slit arrives amber, it blinks once, and the block hands the header back to pi's built-in one about a second later. The first interactive session on a machine gets the longer flourish instead — the five forms `unown` is spelled with, stroking in and decoding into letters, the landing page's own "each form decodes twice" beat — and records that in `onboarding.json`'s reserved `tipsSeen` lane, so every later boot gets the short one. Built as `pi-repo/packages/self-learning/src/splash.ts`: a pure frame table (variant × step × width × theme) behind a `Component`, installed through the public `ctx.ui.setHeader()` seam and released with `setHeader(undefined)`, so **no vendored `coding-agent` file is touched**. The invariants are what make it safe to have at all, and all of them are pinned: the `unown v…` wordmark is painted on every frame, built exactly as `interactive-mode.ts` builds it, so the TUI capture lane's `unown v` readiness probe never waits on an animation; no line ever exceeds the terminal width (below a variant's own minimum the art is *dropped*, never wrapped); colour comes from theme tokens only with no background fills, honouring the beauty contract; the editor keeps focus throughout and the interval is `unref()`'d, so the mark can neither delay a keystroke nor hold a process open; it plays once per process and only on `reason === "startup"`, so `/new`, `/resume` and `/reload` stay quiet; and it is suppressed by `quietStartup`, `NO_COLOR`, any non-TUI mode, or the new `UNOWN_NO_SPLASH=1`. Three defects were caught before the code was, by rendering the design as a playable browser mockup first: the W form's third row was one column short (a shear visible only on the frames that show the W), `✕` — which the landing page's mark field uses — measures 1.39 cells in a mono stack that falls back for it and silently breaks column alignment (the box-drawing `╳` is 1.00, and a glyph-range pin now keeps dingbats out), and a nine-row formation does **not** coexist with the loaded-resources block inside a fixed-height viewport — the flex column compressed the splash and painted the bottom marks underneath the header, which is why the mark takes those rows and gives them back on settle. Tests: `test/splash.test.ts` (28 tests / 6318 assertions — wordmark-on-every-frame, equal row lengths, no overflow across widths 20-140, narrow collapse, glyph safety, theme-token existence against `UNOWN_THEME.colors`, the duration budget read out of the frame table rather than typed, every gate and its reason string, latch idempotence, extension wiring per session reason, and hand-back-exactly-once / dispose-cancels on the live component). `bunx biome check` clean on both new files; `tsc` reports nothing in `splash.ts` or `cli.ts`; the full self-learning suite 4230 pass / 0 fail across 174 files. Landing side (the entry feeds the changelog rail): regenerated `changelog-data.ts` with `check:changelog` green, `check:docs` and `check:claims` (63 literals) green, typecheck clean, 103 unit tests pass, lint unchanged (its 3 warnings are pre-existing `scripts-dev` probes). Docs: [usage — the startup mark](docs/usage.md#the-startup-mark-2026-10-24), the `UNOWN_NO_SPLASH` row in [configuration](docs/configuration.md#terminal-agent-unown-cli) and `.env.example`, and the self-learning AGENTS.md contract. **Not yet verified live**: the end-to-end boot inside a real terminal was not driven on this host (no `tmux`/`ttyd`; the WSL distro has no `bun` and the tree's native modules are win32), so frame pacing over tmux/SSH and the visual seam of the handoff remain unconfirmed — the PTY capture lane at 110×30 and 80×24, with and without `UNOWN_NO_SPLASH=1`, is the outstanding check.

## [0.0.15] - 2026-10-24

### Added

- **release: v0.0.15 — the media-completion round ships — every dex entry filmed, the atlas review PASS** — this release cuts the two rounds that closed the landing's media gaps: **the media-completion round** (№ 010 Parity's last pending plate filmed via the sdlc lane — /context + the session workflow end to end; № 011 Browser's still reels filmed via the browser-subagent lane — the moving cut that retires the PTY stills; the dex ships 24 movies on 22 entries, zero stills, zero pending, and the gates pin all three: the e2e decode walk, the zero-pending contract, the 24=22+2 reel arithmetic) and **the sprite craft round** (the creature atlas's own VLM review lane flipped FIX→PASS: memory's card rides the drawer mid-pull, mcp's floating head is justified by a centered gold energy column with recessed sockets and seamed treads, lsp's spectacles read as glasses with a bridge and temple arms over evenly-breathing segments — all eleven sprites re-gated, IP-safety clean, and the stale ten-body-plan docblocks corrected to eleven). The release is verified like every release: the full engine suite 4350 pass / 0 fail, the integration suite 13/13, the bridge suite 161/161, the artifact plan gate (6 targets + both builder test files), the six per-OS binaries built and the linux-x64 archive extracted and EXECUTED locally (`unown --version` → 0.0.15, VERSION/LICENSE/INSTALL.txt in the payload), the landing production build + standalone smoke, the full e2e 9/9 with the decode gate walking all 24 movies, and the 3-OS release matrix this tag triggers.

### Added

- **landing: the media-completion round — the last plate filmed, every reel a movie** — the two surfaces a visitor could still click to and find nothing moving are closed with real captured footage, and the dex's contract hardens from "pending plates are stated honestly" to "no plate may ship unfilmed". **№ 010 Parity (the sdlc checklist) filmed** (`sdlc` lane): one real task gives the session real turns, then the checklist's NEWEST ports counted out loud — `/context` (the context-window view, the context round) and the session workflow end to end (the session-workflow round): `/sessions list` → `/sessions export --md` → the exported markdown on disk via `!head` → `/sessions list --all`; the poster is the sessions list itself, the face of the workflow. **№ 011 Browser's still reels filmed** (`browser-subagent` lane, the moving cut that supersedes the 2026-10-24 PTY stills exactly like split-view's cut before it): the sync delegation row (`task → browser`), the HUMANIZED live labels riding it (`browser: navigate / fill_form / click`), the finished row with the wall-clock total, the child's report quoting the live confirmation text (proof the whole loop ran against a real rendered page — a local form fixture the lane serves itself), then the async lane: the ◉ fleet widget row and the `/tasks` lifecycle registry. The dex now ships **24 movies on 22 entries, zero stills, zero pending** — the browser entry's two reels are labelled ("visitor check" / "subagent"), and the derived tally sentence pluralizes with its counts. **Gates hardened to the new contract**: `e2e/dex-video.spec.ts`'s pending test flips from "pending entries state their gap" to "zero pending plates, and every filmed pane mounts real media" (the defensive media-free check stays for any future plate); `dex.test.tsx` pins `TALLY.pending === 0`, `TALLY.stills === 0` and the 24 = 22+2 reel arithmetic; the provenance line composes with the data (the still-set clause folds away, the date claim "captured 2026-10-20" becomes "across the 2026-10 rounds", and the false "each poster is that scenario's first audited still" — stale since the pending-plates round re-pointed posters at story-subject stills — now says "one of that scenario's audited stills"). **Harness fixes the fresh clone earned** (captured against a from-scratch host, all found live): the ready anchor `unown v` retired when the startup banner became the loaded-resources block — both lanes' `waitReady` (`driver.js`, `pty_common.py`) now anchor on the block's last section (`── Themes ──`), so a fresh clone boots any lane; the cli-doctor lane's `kimi-k2.5` anchors (the ORIGINAL capture host's model id) become the printed headers ("Configured providers", "LLM usage") — any catalog serves; the onboarding "First flights" overlay is dismissed by the `<UNOWN_HOME>/onboarding.json` latch before any lane films (a fresh host's first boot would otherwise wait on the overlay forever); and `s23`'s fixture cleanup learns the `[h]ttp.server` pkill bracket (the pattern string rides the cleanup shell's own cmdline — pkill killed the step mid-run). Both new lanes ship through the full workflow: `run_lane.sh` end to end, pane-truth + pixel + VLM still audits 7/7 each, frame scans clean, 5/5 video samples clean, md5-distinct stills, H.264 faststart mp4 + VP9 webm at the lane geometry (1600×1074 movies, 2206×1480 stills). Verified: 103 unit tests green (the four media gates over the new files included), typecheck + lint green, production build + standalone smoke green, full e2e 9/9 with the decode gate walking all 24 movies.

- **landing: the sprite craft round — the atlas's own review lane flips FIX→PASS** — the creature pipeline's VLM review (`node scripts/creature_sprites.mjs --review`, the set audited as the landing ships it) returned **FIX** with three concrete craft defects, all repaired in the atlas code and re-gated: **memory** (the archive turtle) — the retrieved card floated six rows above the drawer with shell between, reading as a detached white fleck; it now rides the right drawer's frame and pokes two rows above the front, bottom rows INSIDE it, so the outline wraps card+drawer as one shape — mid-pull, half-out, unmistakably in use. **mcp** (the socket golem) — the floating head read as a glitch (an island with nothing justifying the air); a CENTERED gold energy column now bridges head-base to collar (the sprite is X-symmetric, so the wisp is too — the first off-center cut failed the symmetry gate live), the chest sockets' single navy row deepened to two so they read as recessed ports rather than buttons, and the tread band gained full-height navy seam columns so it reads as segments rather than a solid skirt (the first 1px notches vanished at 2x — measured against the 4x render before the second cut). **lsp** (the bookwyrm) — the touching spectacle rings read as one gold mask or "two separate eyes"; an explicit bridge bar across the tangent seam plus temple arms running to the head's edge make them read as GLASSES, and the segment stack's uneven air (1 row head-to-middle, 3.5 middle-to-bottom) is re-pitched to ~3 rows each so the "air between segments" the doctrine promises is what the eye meets. The stylistic notes the review also raised (sdlc's horizontal sprawl, router's thin signpost column, dream's small z's) are the v3 doctrine's own body plans and stand as designed. Stale doc-block drift fixed in the same change: `creature_atlas.mjs` and `creature.tsx` still said TEN body plans — the browser round's window form made it eleven. Verified: all pipeline gates green (edge-outline integrity, orphan clusters, 5-bit GBA grid, ≤15 colours, dark-ratio ≤30% — mcp 25%, lsp 28% with headroom, shared baseline), the review lane's verdict now **PASS** with IP-safety clean, the palette gate PASS, `bun test src` green (the creature and dex suites over the shipped PNGs), typecheck unchanged, and a 4x VLM read of each repaired sprite confirms the specific fix reads at display scale.

## [0.0.13] - 2026-10-24

### Added

- **parity: session workflow — markdown export, both stores** — two holes found by the 2026-10-24 reverse-engineering sweep (eight agents: Claude Code, Codex CLI, Gemini CLI, OpenCode, Crush, Amp, Aider, Goose): every markdown-export surface converged (Codex `/export`, goose `session export md`, Amp thread `.md`, Gemini `/chat share`) while Unown shipped JSON only — and Unown's own session workflow was BROKEN for the primary store: `unown sessions list`/`export` read only `<cwd>/.unown/sessions` (the import lane) while the TUI and print mode persist every real session to `~/.unown/agent/sessions` — the agent's own sessions were invisible. **`--format md`** (`unown sessions export <id> --format md [--out F] [--raw]` + `/sessions export --md`): a shareable markdown transcript — metadata table (model/provider/project/message counts/token totals/files/tools), todos as checkboxes, notes as quotes, the conversation with tool-call bullets and fenced tool results. CommonMark-safe fences (the fence always outgrows the longest backtick run inside the payload), 2,000-char tool-result truncation with an explicit marker, thinking blocks omitted (the share-artifact convention), and the same default-on secrets redaction as the JSON export (`[REDACTED:<type>]` markers + the count in the CLI notice; `--raw` opts out). **The both-stores fix**: `listUserSessions` (the user store, cwd-filtered by header with resolve-equal semantics, `--all` drops the filter — the Codex `resume --all` / Claude Ctrl+A lane), `listAllSessions` (merged, project-wins dedupe), `findUserSessionFile`; list/export search both stores; `unown-home.ts` extracts `resolveUnownHome` + `resolveUserSessionDir` from term.ts (call-time env read, one definition). Verified: 19 unit tests + the full suite 4,284 pass / 0 fail; live as a real user — the previously invisible session lists and exports to markdown (840 bytes, header + transcript verified), JSON back-compat intact, `--all` lists every machine session.
- **parity: /context — the context view** — Claude Code `/context`, aider `/tokens`, Gemini `/stats`, Codex `/usage` — every competitor ships a HUMAN-invokable context-window view; Unown had only the model-facing `get_context_remaining` tool. **`/context`** (context-viz.ts, pure): the utilization bar + used/remaining as ACTUALS from the host's own accounting (`ctx.getContextUsage()`, the same seam the agent tool reads; a labeled chars/4 estimate before the first response), then **where it goes** — the measured memory stack (per-tier tokens: guide/policy/project/user/local, the producer tier label winning over the virtual display path), the conversation estimate, and the system+tools residual (only with actuals — an estimate minus estimates compounds into fake precision) — plus the `/usage`-attribution session rollup and compaction advice banded at 50/70/85%. Verified: 11 unit tests, typecheck clean; live via the PTY harness 4/4 PASS against the real TUI with a real agnes-3.0-flash turn — 'Context — agnes-3.0-flash (zai-bridge) · window 524,288 tokens', the bar at 2.4%, 'Memory stack ~598 tokens (guide 165 · policy 146 · project 167 · user 54 · local 66)', the session rollup, the fresh-boot estimate lane.
- **parity: --max-tool-repetitions — the loop guard, exit 4** — goose ships `--max-tool-repetitions` (the loop guard) and Gemini documents a distinct exit code per headless stop reason; Unown had `--max-turns` (the budget bound) but no anomaly detector for the classic degenerate headless failure — a model stuck calling the SAME tool with the SAME arguments over and over. **`unown -p --max-tool-repetitions N`** (tool-repetition.ts): counts CONSECUTIVE identical tool calls (same name + same arguments, key-order canonicalized at every depth so a reordered-JSON model cannot dodge) across assistant message_end content blocks — including parallel identical calls within ONE message — and aborts the agent loop at the streak limit; a different tool or changed arguments resets the streak (healthy retry-with-changes never trips). The tool is named on stderr, the `--output-format json` envelope gains `loop_guard {tool, count, limit}`, and the run exits a distinct **4**. Print-mode exit contract: **0** success (a `--max-turns` cap with an answer is still 0 — `capped: true` is the signal) · **1** run error/empty · **2** usage · **3** schema-invalid · **4** loop guard. Verified: 15 unit tests + parse-args wiring; live against the real model — the forced-loop prompt trips at the limit ('tool bash called with identical arguments 3 time(s) in a row') with EXIT 4, the JSON lane carries `loop_guard` + exit 4, a healthy run exits 0, a bad value exits 2.
- **parity: post-edit auto-formatters** — OpenCode ships Formatters as a first-class config lane (gofmt/prettier auto-run after edits), aider runs its lint loop after every edit; Unown's PostToolUse syntax gate fed errors back but style changes had no lane. **`formatters.json`** (two scopes, the hooks.json precedent: `~/.unown/agent/formatters.json` always trusted; `.unown/formatters.json` trust-gated refuse-first): `{"formatters": [{"pattern": "<glob>", "command": "prettier --write"}]}` — first match wins (project rules replace user rules with the same pattern), the permissions layer's glob engine matches (a bare `*.ts` covers nested files), the file path is appended or replaces `$FILE`, each run gets a 10s timeout. After every successful write/edit the first matching formatter runs and the change feeds back as a non-error `[PostToolUse:formatter]` block — the agent sees the final on-disk form and can re-read if the reformat moved its anchors; a failed formatter latches per-command per-session (one notice, then silent); clean writes stay silent; everything fail-open (a formatter must never break an edit). Verified: 13 unit tests incl. a REAL sed rewriter through `runFormatter`; live twice — headless, the model's own answer reports 'The write tool triggered a post-write formatter that collapsed the multiple spaces … I wrote the file via printf in bash instead' (the feedback loop demonstrably reached it and it adapted); PTY 3/3 PASS against the real TUI.
- **parity: unown import — config migration** — Claude Code ships `/import codex|gemini|cursor` (the switcher-acquisition play) and Codex ships `/import` from Claude Code; Unown's `sessions import --from claude|codex` moved transcripts only. **`unown import [--from <auto|claude-code|codex|gemini|cursor|opencode>] [--dry-run] [--lane …] [--list] [--home <dir>]`**: instructions (CLAUDE.md / GEMINI.md / codex AGENTS.md / cursor `.mdc` rules) append to AGENTS.md under a marked comment section; Claude Code commands copy as-is (already unown's contract), Gemini TOML commands convert (`{{args}}` → `$ARGUMENTS`), skills and agent files land in `.unown/skills/` + `.unown/agents/`; MCP servers (`.mcp.json`, `~/.claude.json`, codex `config.toml [mcp_servers.*]` via a tolerant TOML reader incl. inline-table env, gemini `settings.json` with `httpUrl` → `url` normalization) merge into `.unown/mcp.json`. **Never overwrites**: existing targets skip with reasons, existing unown MCP servers win, a re-run of the same source appends nothing (idempotent); the mcp.json stays refuse-first trust-gated (the report says `/mcp trust`). Verified: 14 unit tests (parsers, conversion, normalization, every source's discovery, never-overwrite + idempotence + unown-wins + dry-run + lanes + CLI exit codes); live against a fabricated claude-code home — `--list` surveys, `--dry-run` plans without writing, the real import lands all five artifact kinds, the re-run skips everything with reasons, `unown mcp list` shows the imported server (untrusted + trust hint), doctor reads the new AGENTS.md.
- **parity: unown --worktree — session worktrees** — Claude Code `--worktree/-w` (+ worktree sessions in the agent view), Codex `--worktree` + `/worktree`, Gemini `-w` — parallel work without stashing; Unown had the machinery for SUBAGENTS only (the muse fan-out port). **`unown --worktree [name]` / `-w`** (TUI + print): creates a managed tree at the repo's HEAD under `.unown/worktrees/`, chdirs the whole session in, cleans up on exit per the existing remove_if_clean policy — untouched tree removed automatically, changes keep the tree for review (nothing is ever auto-merged). **The hard part, found live**: cmdPrint terminates through `process.exit()` on EVERY path — a `finally` around the session never runs; the wrapper intercepts `process.exit` for the session's duration, captures the exit, runs cleanup, then exits for real with the session's own code (bun permits the patch, measured). **The session lane's status filter**: the session's own `.unown/` runtime state (memory scaffold, receipts) does not count as changes — a session that produced no work still cleans up (the subagent lane's semantics unchanged). **`unown worktrees list|remove [--force]`**: live status per tree; remove refuses dirty trees (git's native safety), `--force` cleans up after review. Fail-closed outside a git repo (typed reason, exit 1 — never a silent shared run). Verified: 8 real-git integration tests (the session runs INSIDE the tree — cwd proven; the exit-interception lane; the full keep/remove lifecycle) + 5 parse-args grammar tests; live, the whole lifecycle: clean session auto-removes, change-making session keeps ('nothing was merged automatically'), list shows it, remove refuses dirty, `--force` cleans up.

### Fixed

- **landing: the scroll-budget breach the v0.0.12 stop caused — MAX_LISTED 3→2, the rail's own valve again** — CI 40087051206 (the v0.0.12 release commit) failed the e2e landing-budget gate deterministically: 3216 words against the 3200 contract (the same gate the pending-plates round itself had to pay at 5→3 hours earlier — the 0.0.12 stop's tile plus its 15-word restated title was the growth). The fix is the sanctioned compression, not a deletion: the changelog rail prints two listed titles per stop instead of three ("+1 more" links the third out — seven stops carried one, 86 words out, seven links back in, nothing deleted and every count stays printed), and the 0.0.12 stop's title drops its redundant "supersedes the failed tag" tail, which the release notes and CHANGELOG entry already carry. Verified: the production standalone build audited at 3167 words / 12.4vh / no band over 3vh (`node scripts/landing_audit.mjs --budget`, the same measurement the e2e spec makes at 1440x900; the rail itself 574→492 words), the changelog rail's unit suite green with the arithmetic asserted generically over the exported constant, typecheck + production build unchanged.

the report toast is taller than the pane, so its section headers scroll away — and the auto-titled session proven by /name), **harness** (the Learning Lab screen against real engine cycles driven through the engine API; the dashboard opens with focus on the section sidebar, so the lane Tabs into the content list before Enter), **router** (the real job table — dreams, harness, automations, one busy-mutex), **routines** (a real CLI-created routine — a daily 09:00 morning brief, read-only — whose manual run SUCCEEDED on record and whose scheduled 09:00 run fired live during the capture window), **skills** (the Skills screen after the self-improvement loop genuinely shipped a skill: `lint-changed-files`, dream-minted, Biome auto-fixes scoped to changed pi-repo files — plus the loop's later mints), **goals** (a real /goal run: the objective riding the next message's prompt, todo-tracked work, the completion gate closing on the FOOTER STATE `goal: ✓ complete` — prose anchors false-match the model's own text — with both kinds of cited evidence and the /goal status record), and **split-view** — FILMED (the dex copy said "Stills only — this lane ships no movie"; the movie supersedes the PTY stills cut through the audited ttyd pipeline: /split mounts the band + dock, a live turn fills the Thinking and Tool Usage panels while the files dock carries the edit diff). **Three lanes re-captured honest**: review's poster now shows the actual uncommitted diff read into the pane (`!git diff` before the still — the fixture's fix lived in the filesystem, and a plain boot frame is byte-identical to every other lane's; its 04 beat is the user's own `ls` of the saved review artifact, because the verdict and the Saved-to path land in ONE result block and no two stills could differ), subagent-explore's poster is the typed exploration ask (its boot frame was byte-identical to fleet's), and hero-bugfix's tail is the finished green turn plus the user's flake-guard `!bun test` re-run (agnes-3.0-flash lands the bash re-run and a short final answer inside one still's settle window, so the old 05/06 shipped byte-identical twice); plan-mode's poster is re-pointed at its own numbered-plan still — the face of its story. The dex now ships **22 movies + the browser-subagent stills, one deliberate pending plate** (№ 010 Parity, the checklist), and the derived default pane is № 001 Websearch. **Harness hardening found live**: tmux does not know the key name `Esc` — `send-keys` typed the LITERAL STRING into modal overlays (verified via `cat -v`), so `driver.js` gains a `KEY_ALIASES` map (Esc→Escape and friends) — the /btw overlay could never be dismissed and silently ate the next command's typing; `run_lane.sh` runs one lane end to end (prep → time-boxed driver → assemble → all three audit gates → an md5-distinctness check) and wipes the lane dir first (stale stills from renamed beats failed audits with "no pane dump found"); `pipeline-a..e.sh` chain the rounds; `skills-prep.sh` drives the real mint→run→ship loop; the goals lane anchors mid-work on the FILESYSTEM (git status) instead of pane text (boot-context prose false-matched `read`), and the /btw-sensitive captures run against a budget-throttled engine (`PI_ENGINE_MAX_CALLS_HOUR=1`) so the bridge's background lane answers the side-channel promptly — the /insights timeout was the dream queue, not the command. **New gates, so "the video doesn't work" can never again be discovered by a visitor**: `dex-media.test.tsx` (every media path the rendered DOM references exists under `public/`, every `<img>` with declared intrinsic dims matches the PNG's real IHDR size — the split-view stills shipped 1792×1042 while the dex declared 2214×1369 — every referenced mp4 has faststart, every webm the EBML magic, and **no two entries ship byte-identical posters**); `e2e/dex-video.spec.ts` (the plays-like-a-real-user gate: selecting each filmed entry drives its video to readyState 4, presents frames via `requestVideoFrameCallback`, advances `currentTime`, zero MediaError, zero failed media fetches; the pending plate stays honest with no mounted media). Also in the round: the changelog rail's `MAX_LISTED` 5→3 (the rail's own rule is "the fortieth release costs the page nothing", but listed titles cost scroll-budget words the sideways axis cannot hide — the eight-stop rail alone had grown to 585 of them; the page measured 3220 words against the 3200 gate on clean HEAD), `vercel.json` sends `/landing/*` with `s-maxage=31536000` (every media fetch was `max-age=0, must-revalidate` — repeat visits re-downloaded the audited captures), and the stale-number doc drift (tui/README № 017→018, the dex-data behavior ranges, the creature-test count, the AGENTS.md OG-set sentence) is corrected. Verified: 103 unit tests (the four media gates included), typecheck + lint unchanged, production build + standalone smoke green, full e2e 9/9 — the decode gate walks all 22 movies and every one reaches HAVE_ENOUGH_DATA and presents frames; the real-user browser audit shows 21/22 filmed, all playing, zero console errors, zero failed requests.

## [0.0.10] - 2026-10-24

### Added

- **parity: the trust round — in-session permission modes + additional working directories** — the two most user-visible trust gaps from the 2026-10-24 reverse-engineering sweep (four parallel research agents over Claude Code, Gemini CLI, Codex, OpenCode, Crush, Amp, Aider, Goose), both verified live. **`/permissions mode <default|acceptEdits|bypassPermissions|reset>`**: the session-scoped mode override (Claude Code's Shift+Tab lane, Gemini CLI's `--approval-mode`, goose `/mode`, Crush ctrl+y, OpenCode `--auto` — every competitor ships an in-session switch; Unown's modes were config-file only). The config files are NEVER written; the override survives config reloads; `UNOWN_STRICT_HOOKS=1` refuses bypass exactly like a config declaration; `/permissions status` reports `modeSource` (config|session). **`--permission-mode <m>`**: the launch twin (print + TUI; usage error exits 2). **`--add-dir <path>`** (repeatable) **+ `/add-dir <path>|list|clear`**: additional working directories (Claude Code `--add-dir` / Gemini `--include-directories` parity — the write jail was hard-coded to project+tmp, so cross-repo work was impossible). `write-roots.ts` owns the registry: user-initiated roots, realpath-resolved at add time, degenerate roots refused, dedupe, session scope only; `terminalPathOk` consults the roots after project+tmp (write/edit tools); `bashRedirectEscape`'s system-tree exemption covers them; `instruction-guard.ts` applies its FULL SA-B2 deny set relative to every added root — an added root's `AGENTS.md` / `.unown/hooks.json` / `.claude/settings.json` stay in-session-unwritable, and devices and `.ssh` are never exempt: the instruction-set gate stays final, root or no root. Tests: permissions-mode (19), write-roots (15), instruction-guard still 67, permissions 86, parse-args +4; the full suite 4154 pass / 0 fail. Live verification: `scripts/tui-capture/pty_capture_trust.py` — 6/6 PASS (launch flags land, status shows the override + dirs, live mode switch, add-dir list/clear, mode reset), plus the e2e BLOCKED/WROTE matrix: a write tool call to `../shared` BLOCKED without the grant, WROTE with `--add-dir ../shared` (real model, real tool call, receipts-verified). Docs: [permissions](docs/permissions.md) (modes + add-dir sections), [usage](docs/usage.md) (commands + trust flags), root + self-learning AGENTS.md contracts.
- **parity: the flow round — --continue/--resume launch + project settings** — the flow-round consensus gaps (Claude Code `claude -c`/`-r`, Codex resume, Crush `--continue`, OpenCode `-c`, plus the project-config layers of Claude Code/Gemini/Codex), each verified live. **`unown -c` / `--continue`**: reopen this directory's latest session — `resume.ts` scans BOTH stores (`~/.unown/agent/sessions` + the project's `.unown/sessions` imports), newest-first, header cwd realpath-compared. **`unown --resume <id|name|path>` / `-r`**: id full-or-prefix (from the filename, no file reads), name substring over the latest `session_info` (auto-titles and `/name` both feed it, newest wins, ambiguous matches list the five newest candidates instead of guessing), or exact file path (the `--session` spelling). Failures exit 1 with actionable messages; the landing notice goes to stderr so `--output-format json`'s stdout contract stays clean. Works in TUI and print mode (with `-p`: resume-and-run). **`.unown/settings.json`** (project settings layer, trust-gated like every project config): `model` / `provider` / `effort` / `permissionMode` / `addDirs` pin per-project session defaults; precedence CLI > project > user defaults — a flag the command line set is never overwritten; unknown keys warn and drop, never fatal; `/project-settings [status|trust]` in-session. Verified live: an untrusted file's keys are ignored with one boot notice; trusted, the model pin routes the session (measured: the session's `model_change` entry landed on agnes-2.5-flash). FIXED on the way (found live while verifying): the vendored pi footer and `/session` stats crashed on assistant messages without a usage block — imported sessions (the v0.0.8 cross-agent import lane writes exactly that shape) could never resume in the TUI; both call sites now guard, dist rebuilt (crash receipt: "undefined is not an object (evaluating 'usage.input')" from footer.js:147). Tests: `resume.test.ts` (17: both stores, id/name/path resolution, ambiguity, trust gate, parse validation), parse-args +4; the full self-learning suite 4260 pass / 0 fail. Live verification: `scripts/tui-capture/pty_capture_flow.py` — 4/4 PASS, plus CLI smokes: `-c` recalled the prior turn's answer, `--resume <id-prefix>` answered "3" (the real message count), `--resume <name>` recalled the crafted magic word, the ambiguous-id error listed both candidates. Docs: [usage](docs/usage.md) (resume + project-settings paragraphs, command rows), [configuration](docs/configuration.md) (the project settings section), root + self-learning AGENTS.md contracts.
- **parity: the CI round — --max-turns + --output-schema headless hardening** — the CI-round consensus gaps (Claude Code `--max-turns`/`--json-schema`, Gemini CLI `--max-turns` + documented exit codes, Codex `exec --output-schema`), each verified live. **`unown -p --max-turns N`**: the bounded headless run. A turn is ONE MODEL CALL — pi fires `message_start` per assistant message, while `agent_start`/`agent_end` bracket the whole agent LOOP for one user prompt (the first implementation counted loops and capped nothing on a multi-tool task — found live, fixed, and pinned in tests). The (N+1)th model call aborts through pi's own `ctx.abort` (the Ctrl-C path: disposal, session notes and exit semantics behave as an interrupted run); the answer produced so far stands. The JSON envelope gains `capped: true` and a `num_turns` that counts COMPLETED turns (the aborted partial's `message_end` is subtracted); a capped run WITH an answer reports subtype success — the cap is a controlled stop, not an execution error. Verified live: `--max-turns 1` on a two-turn task -> `num_turns 1, capped true`; `--max-turns 2` -> `num_turns 2, capped false`, subtype success, the real answer. **`unown -p --output-schema <file>`**: the final answer must parse as JSON (ONE wrapping \u0060\u0060\u0060json fence is stripped — the LLM padding lane) and validate against the JSON Schema (`jsonschema.ts`, the task tool's own dependency-free validator with JSON-path issues). Exit 3 on mismatch with the issues on stderr; composes with `--output-format json` (the envelope gains `schema_valid`/`schema_issues`); refused with `--mode json` (exit 2 — an event stream has no single final answer). Print-mode exit contract now documented: 0 success, 1 error/empty, 2 usage, 3 schema-invalid. Verified live: valid answer -> `schema_valid true`, exit 0; prose answer -> exit 3 + "the final answer is not valid JSON". Tests: `headless-guards.test.ts` (18: turn semantics incl. the non-assistant-events pin, abort-once, no-ctx safety, fence stripping, schema paths, hostile-schema totality, renderer cap); the full self-learning suite 4353 pass / 0 fail. Docs: [usage](docs/usage.md) (the CI-flags paragraph + the exit-code contract), root + self-learning AGENTS.md contracts.
- **docs(DOX v3): the documentation tree reinstalled on the v3 framework — every AGENTS.md rebuilt from a fresh deep scan** — the existing DOX tree (installed 2026-10-15 on the June framework revision, since grown to 1,968 lines / 276 KB with v1-era local modifications — a 6-step Read Before Editing missing the v3 child-listing step, a 47 KB root carrying diary-entry prose, per-file test-count pins the tree had already outgrown) is removed and replaced wholesale with the **v3 framework** from [agent0ai/dox](https://github.com/agent0ai/dox) (the AGENTS.md file's third revision, 2026-09-01 — "Revise AGENTS.md for project indexing requirements"), whose bootstrap instruction is followed literally: *scan the project, build the DOX tree, go deep and create nested DOX files where needed*. **The root rail** carries the v3 framework text verbatim (Core Contract, the 7-step Read Before Editing with the parent-listed-child step, Update After Editing, Hierarchy, Child Doc Shape with the leave-it-empty rules, Style, Closeout) wrapped in freshly scanned project sections — Project Overview (service map, data flow, repo layout), Root Contracts (the bin entry chain, package.json surface, bun >= 1.4 floor, .env single-source rule, the 0.0.x version contract, the six-archive release contract, the landing truth rules for the three generated data files, the media never-delete rule, the .unown surface, the Vercel + verify-deploy gate) and Git Preflight (kept at its anchored URL — CONTRIBUTING.md links it); User Preferences keeps the Agnes endpoint assignments. **Every child doc rebuilt** to the v6-section shape (Purpose / Ownership / Local Contracts / Work Guidance / Verification / Child DOX Index) with content re-derived from a four-agent parallel deep scan of the actual tree, not carried over: `src/` (the landing app), `src/components/landing/` (1,142 lines → the nine-band structure, the three GENERATED files with their generator table, the honesty rules, the full verification ladder), `docs/` (all ~50 pages grouped by kind with the conventions and same-change sync rules), `mini-services/` + `mini-services/llm-bridge/` (routing, admission lanes, failover, degenerate guard, usage log), `pi-repo/packages/self-learning/` (the five mechanisms, terminal agent, engine API + M7 auth, sandbox, automations, env conventions — with the stale per-file test-count pins deliberately NOT restated), `scripts/` (lifecycle, builders, gates, generators, brand pipeline, the TUI capture lane), `blueprint/` (the frozen rule + v1→v2 mapping); `pi-repo/AGENTS.md` keeps its upstream pi-mono ruleset untouched with a rewritten Child DOX Index. **One NEW nested doc** per the v3 "go deep" instruction: `benchmarks/AGENTS.md` — the first DOX coverage for the 25+ suite tree (methodology contract, the exact six receipt paths that feed the landing page, the per-model canonical-run rule, the receipts-vs-scratch divide). Stale cross-references fixed in the same change: CONTRIBUTING.md's sync-map pointer (the map is gone — the sync rules live in the owning child docs now, per v3 Style "put broad rules in parent docs and concrete details in child docs"), and `docs/landing-research.md`'s pointer to the root sync map updated to its new home (Root Contracts) with the move recorded. The rebased tree keeps every 2026-10-24 round contract current in the fresh docs: the trust-round launch flags (`--permission-mode`, `--add-dir`), the flow-round resume + project-settings layer, and the CI-round headless guards (`--max-turns`, `--output-schema`, the 0/1/2/3 exit contract) are all in the root and self-learning contracts. The test fixture `scripts/tui-capture/fixtures/api-server/AGENTS.md` is NOT part of the DOX tree (the scripts doc now says so explicitly); no media assets touched. Verified: `node scripts/check_instruction_chars.mjs` (42 files, 0 findings — the new tree is invisible-Unicode clean), `bun run check:docs` (all 50 pages green), `bun run check:changelog` / `check:benchmarks` / `check:claims` green, `bun run typecheck` + `bun run lint` + `bun test src` (102) unchanged-green, production build + standalone smoke green.

### Fixed

- **resume tests (Windows): the fixture wrote invalid JSON — 4 phantom source failures on the release matrix, root-caused twice** — found by the v0.0.10 release matrix (the windows-latest verify leg failed 4 flow-round resume tests while ubuntu/macos passed, red on both release attempts). The real bug: `resume.test.ts`'s `writeSession` built the session-header line with a raw template interpolation of `opts.cwd` — on Windows that is `D:\a\Unown\...`, and `\a` is an INVALID JSON escape, so the "header" line never parsed. `parseHeader` correctly rejected it, every content-dependent scan came back empty (`checked 0 session file(s)`), and the four tests that require header/name reads failed while the filename-only paths (path pass-through, id-prefix with its basename fallback) kept working — exactly the split that made the bug invisible to the flow round's Linux-local verification. The fixture now serializes with `JSON.stringify` (as the real session writer always did), so the header is valid JSON on every platform. Corrections register, in the open: the first fix attempt (readHead/readTail falling back to `readFileSync` on zero positional reads) was built on a WRONG theory — that bun's positional `readSync` mis-handles `position: 0` on Windows — and changed nothing (run 2 failed identically; the fallback never fired; the reads were never broken). That change is fully reverted: the source was never at fault, and dead defense shipped on a false claim is worse than no defense. The `usage-cli` backward scanner (always `pos > 0`) was never affected either way. A SECOND Windows blocker surfaced once the four failures cleared (the matrix's third run: 4200 pass / 0 fail / 1 error): `headless-guards.test.ts`'s `loadOutputSchema` block called `mkdtempSync("/tmp/unown-schema-")` — the literal POSIX path does not exist on Windows (ENOENT, an unhandled describe-level error bun exits 1 on) — the CI-round test file landed after the v0.0.9 release and had never faced a Windows runner until this matrix. It now uses the suite's `TEST_TMP` redirect (the established cross-platform pattern); a sweep confirmed every other tmp consumer in the suite already uses platform tmp roots or pure string logic. Verified: the JSON-escape repro (raw interpolation -> "Invalid escape character a"; `JSON.stringify` -> parses, cwd round-trips), the resume + headless-guards suites 35/35, and the full self-learning suite 4353 tests 0 fail 0 error on Linux; the Windows proof is the release matrix itself (the 4 previously-failing tests plus the previously-erroring schema block are the regression pins).

## [0.0.9] - 2026-10-24

### Added

- **browser-subagent: browser automation as a first-class subagent + the live subagent-visuals round** — the browser surface shipped as a session tool, but the 2026 consensus (reverse-engineered from nine products — Claude Code, Codex CLI, Gemini CLI, Cursor, Amp, Playwright MCP, plus Warp/Goose/Aider/OpenCode/Crush passes; the record with per-product citations lives in [docs/browser-subagent-tui-research.md](docs/browser-subagent-tui-research.md)) is that browser capability belongs to a **subagent**: Gemini ships it ONLY as `browser_agent`, Cursor ships a built-in Browser subagent, and both say why — DOM snapshots, screenshots and console logs are context poison. **The `browser` builtin** (`task {agent: "browser"}`, sync or `async: true`): the child runs the work loop (start → navigate → snapshot → act on refs → verify) and returns a compact verified report under a fixed contract — `Outcome:` / `Did:` / `Pages:` / `Notes:` — while every snapshot, screenshot and console line stays in ITS context; the toolset is `browser` + `read/grep/find/ls` (pinned at the spawn level — the child's model requests carry exactly that allowlist, asserted in the E2E), lean routing (no MCP servers, no AGENTS.md injection — the child's tokens go to page snapshots), a model fallback chain like the worker's, and a **10-minute budget** (Gemini's `browser_agent` number; the 5-minute session default starved page flows). The prompt carries a **terminal-failure contract** (Gemini's list, adapted): browser-missing launch errors, `UNOWN_BROWSER_BLOCK` denials and repeated `net::ERR_*` on the same URL are reported verbatim with their remediation — policy and environment facts are never a retry loop; stale refs are NOT failures (snapshot and retry); and the child NEVER enters credentials the task did not explicitly provide (a login wall it cannot pass is `Outcome: partial` naming the wall). **The routing description says what NOT to delegate** (reading public pages is `webfetch`'s job) — the Gemini contract. **Per-agent timeouts** (the plumbing under the budget): a `timeoutMs` frontmatter field (numbers, `"10m"`, `"90s"` all parse; snake_case `timeout_ms`/`timeout` too) clamped to 30s–30min, a session-wide `UNOWN_SUBAGENT_TIMEOUT_MS` knob (human durations accepted, junk falls back — a typo can never zero the watchdog), per-agent values always win. **Live subagent visuals, both lanes**: the parent now derives a **humanized tool label** from the `tool_execution_start` args already streaming over the child's JSONL — `browser: navigate example.com`, `bash: npm test`, `read: src/foo.ts` (Gemini's `formatToolArgs` precedence; zero child-side changes, zero new events, zero polling) — so the sync task block reads `browser: ⟳ 3 turns, 2 tools in browser: navigate example.com · 12s` and the async fleet widget row reads `◉ 00:12 form-flow — running · browser: click [e12] · 3.4k tok`. Browser-active children (the browser agent, or any child currently driving the browser tool) get the **◉ glyph** in the fleet widget, distinguishable at a glance from repo work; fleet rows carry a **live token count**; finished sync rows carry their **wall-clock total** (`browser: ✓ 7 turns, 6 tools in 1m 13s`); and `/tasks` lists children with **full lifecycle metadata** — status, model, turns, tokens, current tool, elapsed, task preview — the number-one ask across Claude Code's subagent-row issues (#89844/#93324/#94872: rows missing model and effort read as black boxes). Also fixed on the way: the sync lane's tool counter listened for `tool_result_end`, an event the child never emits (the wire name is `tool_execution_end`) — every finished task row has shown "0 tools" since the lane shipped; both names now count. `UNOWN_BROWSER_NO_SANDBOX=1` is now actually read (the launch-retry error hint advertised it; nothing did) — it opts the FIRST launch into `--no-sandbox --disable-gpu` for containers/CI, where the retry path always forced them. Verified: 106 new pure tests (label humanization incl. URL compaction/token formatting/path shortening, timeout resolution incl. clamps + duration parsing + frontmatter plumb, the browser agent's contract, widget rendering with the ◉ glyph/fallback/tokens/width caps) + **4 spawn-level E2E with a real Chromium** against a multi-turn scripted mock bridge (the harness's first queue-driven mock — the flagship scenario drives the full delegation loop and asserts the child's report quotes the LIVE page's `submitted Ada/b` confirmation, which no mock could know; the allowlist, the missing-browser remediation and the blocklist denial are pinned end-to-end) + **as a real user**: `pty_capture_browsersub.py` drives the real TUI with the real Agnes model and a real headless Chromium through the whole visual surface — 6/6 audited stills (the task row, the humanized live label, the finished row with wall clock, the child report, the async ◉ fleet row, the /tasks metadata), landing in `public/landing/tui/browser-subagent/`; docs updated across [subagents](docs/subagents.md) (the built-in table, the frontmatter contract, the widget section, the delegation guide), [browser](docs/browser.md) (the delegation callout, the no-sandbox knob), [configuration](docs/configuration.md), `.env.example`, [usage](docs/usage.md) (the stale "30 actions" — it is 32) and the README. Also fixed on the release gate itself (found by the v0.0.9 3-OS verification, introduced by the same-day trust round): the redirect-exemption's lexical system-tree check compared only the literal spelling of the target, so on macOS — where `/var/tmp` is a symlink into `/private/var/tmp` and write roots are stored realpath'd — `--add-dir /var/tmp` then `> /var/tmp/log` was denied as an escape, denying exactly the root the user had added; the lexical layer now exempts the resolved path too (the resolved-path layer already did), and the `/opt story` test runs against a per-OS system-tree dir (POSIX `/var/tmp`, Windows `%SystemRoot%\Temp`) instead of a POSIX-only literal.

## [0.0.8] - 2026-10-24

### Added

- **parity: the 2026 consensus round — /btw, /output-style, /insights, session auto-titles, TUI turn-complete attention, cross-agent import** — six obvious-but-missing workflows discovered by reverse-engineering the shipped surfaces of ten coding agents (Claude Code, Codex, Antigravity, OpenCode, Crush, Amp, Goose, Aider, Cursor, Cline — four parallel research agents, every candidate gap cross-checked against Unown's actual command surface before a line was written; the already-covered list — queueing/steering, clipboard paste, context meter, palette, resume/fork, permissions/hooks, websearch, routines — was explicitly not rebuilt). **`/btw <question>`** (the Claude Code / Antigravity / OpenCode side-channel, ported): one bridge answer about the session rendered in a dismissible overlay — never entering the transcript, the receipts ledger or AutoDream's note mining;
