- **the density PTY lane** (`scripts/tui-capture/pty_density.py`) — one real tool-using agent turn (write a file, run it) captured at two widths per run, the evidence harness for transcript-density work.

### Added (TUI responsive round 5 — M2: the intuition release — ctrl+k palette, ? help, contextual hint line, 2026-10-21)

- **`ctrl+k` — the command palette** — a fuzzy dispatcher over every command surface, one keystroke away: the slash commands (builtin + custom commands + extensions + skills — the same sources autocomplete uses), plus direct actions named by what they do ("Select model…", "Cycle thinking level", "Session tree", "New session", "Resume a session", "Expand or collapse tool output", "Exit"). Slash entries populate the editor with `/name ` so argument hints and completions stay available; actions run immediately. `ctrl+k` was unbound (verified against every binding and the editor's own chords); `ctrl+p` keeps its pi-convention model-cycle role. One component (`CommandPalette`) mounts in the editor slot — the selectors' vocabulary (arrows + Enter + Esc, type to filter), zero new keybindings to learn — and the dashboard's ⌘K palette gains its terminal twin. Pinned by 8 tests (`command-palette.test.ts`: render + width-table at 48/56/72/96, fuzzy filter + Enter dispatch, Escape cancel, arrow navigation) and driven live end-to-end by the new PTY lane (`scripts/tui-capture/pty_intuition.py`, 6/6: idle hint → palette → filter → Enter opens the model selector).
- **`?` — the searchable keybinding reference** — typing `?` into an empty editor opens the live registry: every binding from `getResolvedBindings()` with its description and category (editor / selector / fullscreen / sessions / models / thinking / messages / clipboard / app), so custom `keybindings.json` values show through (the static `/hotkeys` table cannot). With text in the buffer or the autocomplete popup open, `?` stays a literal character — prose starting with a question mark keeps working (pinned by 3 tests). `/hotkeys` keeps its readable all-at-once table and gains the two new rows.
- **the contextual hint line (I3)** — one dim line under the footer chrome, evaluated at render time so it always matches state: idle shows the discoverability set (`? help · ctrl+k palette · / commands · ! bash · ctrl+o tools`), a working turn shows the keys that matter then (`escape interrupt · ctrl+o tools · ctrl+t thinking`), queued messages show the queue keys — and the `narrow` tier hides the line entirely (chrome budget; measured in the pane dumps: hidden at 56 cols, present at 96/128). The provider contract is crash-safe (a throwing provider renders no line, no crash — pinned). Live keys from the registry, so custom bindings change the hints too.
- **onboarding beat (I4)** — the startup header's compact hint line now leads with the two keys that unlock everything: `? help · ctrl+k palette` alongside `/ commands` and `! bash`; pane-verified at boot.

### Added (the TUI native — `/providers`, 2026-10-20)

- **`/providers` — the in-chat provider manager: mid-session list/add/key/test/refresh/remove and defaults, without leaving the conversation** — the R2 completion of the terminal-only decision: provider management lives in the terminal, and now that means BOTH surfaces — the `unown providers` CLI and this command — over the SAME registry core (`providers.ts`), so a provider added in a session is exactly a provider the CLI lists (and vice versa; no HTTP surface, no dashboard form). Bare `/providers` opens the interactive manager when dialogs exist (arrow-key + Enter selects, Esc backs out — the `/addons` vocabulary, zero new keybindings): one row per provider (`✓ agnes — Agnes AI (default) · key: auth.json · 6 models`), `+ Add provider…`, and a six-action menu per provider — **Make default…** (model pick → settings.json → "use /model to switch this session"; switching the LIVE session stays /model's job), **Set API key** (auth.json 0600 + immediate live verify — a rejected key fails right there, not in your next chat), **Test connection** (the CLI's actionable classification verbatim: 401 → rotate the key, unreachable → fix the URL, plus the registered-but-not-served cross-check), **Refresh models** (live `GET /models` import: `+N added`, `M no longer served (kept)`), **Remove provider…** (one confirm; the default-fallback is reported). Add walks the preset table with live model discovery and a default-model pick from what the endpoint actually serves (discovery failure is not fatal — the preset default registers, the failure surfaces with the fix); the bundled bridge keeps its CLI rules (`zai-bridge` id, placeholder key, served allow-list); custom endpoints are a four-input form. Every registry mutation awaits `ctx.modelRegistry.refresh()` so `/model`'s picker and the footer see changes without a restart. Headless is a first-class mode: `/providers list` (text summary with the canonical `provider/model` default line), `/providers test <id>`, `/providers refresh <id>` work in print/JSON (bare `/providers` prints the summary there; RPC degrades the dialogs to sequential prompts), and argument completions cover the subcommands + live provider ids. Pinned by 46 tests (`test/providers-command.test.ts` — 44: arg grammar, verdict classification, the scripted-queue interactive flows incl. add-preset happy path, make-default, remove-with-confirm, key-set 0600, completions; + the tools.ts registration seam and a fresh-process detachment case) and verified live end-to-end by the PTY lane (`scripts/tui-capture/pty_capture_providers.py`, 4/4 stills + a pane-truth gate that re-greps the dumps: manager list, action menu, a REAL "Test connection" verdict against the live bridge, the text summary) plus a real-home smoke (`/providers test agnes` → `✓ agnes reachable at https://apihub.agnes-ai.com/v1 — credential: $AGNES_API_KEY`).

### Removed (the dashboard exit — provider management is terminal-only, 2026-10-20)

- **provider management lives in the terminal now — the dashboard "Providers" tab is gone** — and the engine's `/api/providers` HTTP surface (registry CRUD, discovery, the 30 s health cache) went with it: the web dashboard is a viewer, not a credential surface; the registry (`providers.ts`), the `unown providers` CLI, and the interactive TUI `/providers` command (added above) are the surface. M4 and M5 shipped without changelog entries, so there is nothing to revert — the presets extraction and the engine-reuse fix stay; `/api/overview` is untouched.
### Added (TUI responsive round 5 — M1: the tier system, the size gate, footer priority degradation, overlay scaling, 2026-10-21)

- **viewport tiers — the TUI now has a breakpoint vocabulary** (`pi-repo/packages/tui/src/viewport-tiers.ts`): one pure-function module maps terminal width to four tiers — `narrow < 80`, `regular 80–109`, `wide 110–143`, `ultra ≥ 144` (Claude Code's published dock model adapted to pi's surfaces) — plus `MIN_TERMINAL_COLS×ROWS = 48×16` and `overlayWidthFor()`. Renderers, components and extensions import the same truth instead of private constants. Pinned by `pi-repo/packages/tui/test/viewport-tiers.test.ts` (boundary table, gate, proportional overlay widths).
- **the minimum-size gate (fullscreen)** — below 48×16 the fullscreen renderer now shows a centered "terminal too small — unown needs at least 48×16 / currently W×H / resize to resume — session kept" notice instead of a broken layout, and recovers untouched when the terminal grows (the gate is on layout, not process; the component tree is never torn down). Opt-in at the tui-package level (`TuiAltScreenOptions.minSizeNotice` — embedded uses and tests stay permissive by design), enabled by InteractiveMode's renderer construction sites. Plain text only, so it renders identically in plain/NO_COLOR/16-color terminals. Pinned by `test/too-small-notice.test.ts` (notice geometry, gate on/off, resize-resume).
- **footer priority degradation — segments drop by value, the meter and the model always survive** — the status line used to truncate its whole left side as one string: at 56 cols after one turn the model identity vanished entirely (`ctx ▕█░░░░░░░░░▏ 6.7%/131k (auto) ↑4.7k ↓5 R4.1k CH46.7%` — measured in the pre-change pane dumps), at 72 it was cut mid-word with no ellipsis (`agnes-3.0-flas`). Now: the context meter and the model identity are survivors; optional stats admit in descending information value (diff chip `+N -M` → tokens ↑↓ → cache R/W/CH → cost `$`), gated off entirely at `narrow`; the meter sheds its bar at `narrow` (`ctx 7%/131k`); the model sheds its provider prefix and thinking suffix before ever being truncated, and truncation is always ellipsized; the location line drops session name then branch before folding the cwd. Post-change pane truth at 56 cols: `ctx 7%/131k (auto) … agnes-3.0-flash` — the two things that must survive, survive. Six new width-table tests in `footer-width.test.ts` (56/72/80/112/148/40 cols).
- **overlay scaling** — `resolveOverlayLayout`'s default width was a fixed `min(80, width)`, a constant from the 80-column era: full-bleed at 56 cols, hugging the left margin at 200. The default is now `overlayWidthFor()` — 86% of the width, clamped to a [40, 96] band, caller-preferred widths still honored. All 83 existing overlay tests green unchanged.
- **selector hint lines degrade instead of wrapping** — the model/thinking selectors' hint (`Enter to select · Ctrl+S to set as default · Esc to cancel`) wrapped mid-word onto a second line below ~58 cols (`cancel` orphaned, measured in the pane dumps). A new shared `SelectorHints` component admits segments by priority and truncates only the final survivor, with an ellipsis; at 56 cols the pane now shows `Enter to select · Ctrl+S to set as default` on one line, at 96 the full hint. Pinned by `test/selector-hints.test.ts`.
- **the responsive PTY lane** (`scripts/tui-capture/pty_responsive.py`) — the TUI booted at 56/72/96/128 cols in one run, driven like an end user (a real chat turn through the live bridge, the model selector, `/todos`), stills + pane ground truth per width, PASS/FAIL per surface with the same exit-code contract as the other PTY lanes. Baseline (pre-change) vs M1 captures compared side by side in the worklog; 16/16 surfaces pass at both labels.
### Fixed (audit wave 1 — correctness core, 2026-10-21)

A six-pass functional audit (TUI surfaces ×2, CLI, dashboard+landing, cross-platform, docs freshness — 190+ live checks against the running stack) filed its must-fix findings; this wave ships the P0/P1 core. Every fix is verified live from an end user's POV (browser fetches for the dashboard, PTY-driven TUI sessions for the guards, hermetic golden tests for the CLI).

- **the stock dashboard no longer 404s its own engine (P0)** — a fresh clone booted the dashboard in GATEWAY mode (`NEXT_PUBLIC_AGENT_API_BASE` empty → relative URLs + `?XTransformPort=3030`, the z.ai-sandbox-preview-only routing trick), so on any normal machine **every** engine call from `/dashboard` 404'd: all 12 tabs rendered empty shells, no SSE, no console. `next.config.ts` now bakes the local engine (`http://127.0.0.1:3030`) as the default at dev/build time (explicit env always wins; `NEXT_PUBLIC_AGENT_API_BASE=gateway` keeps the sandbox preview working; empty normalizes to the default so a blank `.env` line cannot resurrect the bug), `scripts/start.ts` + `scripts/serve-dashboard.ts` export the same default for the server side. Browser-verified: 7 direct engine hits (overview/tasks/jobs/events), zero failed requests, KPIs + LIVE feed render.
- **`unown <typo>` no longer silently burns an LLM session (P0)** — a lone command-like word matching no subcommand used to be folded into a full print-mode session: a ~19k-token system prompt and a model turn spent on the typo'd word, exit 0, no hint anything was wrong. The CLI now guards: unknown lone command-word → exit 2, nearest-command suggestions, and the exact escape hatch (`unown -p "<word>"` or more words). Flags in front (`-p`, `--plan`, …) and multi-word prompts are exempt — they are explicit intent. The TUI got the same guard (audit 2-a-1 F1): an unknown `/command` used to fall through to a model turn (with the autocomplete popup's stale prefix sometimes concatenated into the prompt); the unown input-extension now intercepts it (built-ins + extension commands + prompt templates + skills enumerated via the new `BUILTIN_SLASH_COMMANDS` export + `pi.getCommands()`), shows `Unknown command /x — did you mean …` with a "type / to browse" hint, and never eats path-shaped inputs (`/src/foo.ts`) or prompts that merely start with a slash. PTY-verified both surfaces.
- **`unown blame` is a real command (the CLI twin of `/blame`)** — `unown blame [file]` with the same ledger, flags (`--session`, `--since`, `--json`) and summary mode as the session command; `--help` included. Previously the word was swallowed as a one-word LLM prompt (same class as the typo guard).
- **print-mode stdin handling: idle-open stdin is never consumed (A1 root cause, audited live)** — the "piped stdin joins the prompt" reader attached a `data` listener with a 500 ms timeout and left it attached: an open idle stdin (agent harness, editor, supervisor) kept being drained after the timeout — bytes belonging to the CALLER's protocol were silently eaten, and the lingering listener held the event loop on an open pipe (measured with bare `bun -e` + open pipe: never exits). The reader now reads only while data actively flows (idle window re-armed per chunk, `PI_PRINT_STDIN_IDLE_MS`), settles on EOF, and caps total bytes at 1 MiB (`PI_PRINT_STDIN_MAX_BYTES`) so an endless pipe cannot read forever; once settled every listener is detached and the stream paused — nothing further is ever consumed. (The originally-suspected print-mode process hang was disproven during the audit with instrumented runs — `runPrintMode` resolves and `process.exit(0)` runs; the reproduction was the test harness's own stdin wrapper-protocol being consumed. The stdin reader's real defects are what this fix ships.)
- **split-view band subcommands tell the truth in regular mode** — `/split-thinking`, `/split-tools`, `/split-diff`, `/split-tools-view`, `/split-height`, `/split-tools-width`, `/split-divider` and `/split-save` used to toast success in regular (non-fullscreen) mode, where the band they configure renders **nowhere** — and `/split-save` would then persist that invisible layout as startup defaults. All nine now route through the same fullscreen guard as `/split`, with a message that names the switch (`/settings → tui-mode`). `/files` keeps its regular-mode passive-overlay path (by design). PTY-verified: all nine warn, none fake-succeed, `/split` master unchanged.
- **shell completions match the real command surface** — the four scripts omitted `providers`, `engine`, `blame` (and their subcommands), `mcp elicitation`, and the `--session`/`--provider`/`--no-split` flags; fish emitted the `-p` short flag as `-l p` (uncompletable); `unown completions --help` errored with `unknown shell "--help"`. All fixed; the drift-guard test now pins commands + subcommands + session flags + the fish short-flag form + `--help` exit 0.
- **`unown engine status` shows the auto-start circuit when UP too** — the circuit line (backoff state, gave-up, reset hint) printed only in the DOWN branch, while usage.md documents it as always present.
- **auth.json is 0600 from the first byte** — the empty `{}` placeholder used to land as 0664&~umask and was only hardened on the first real key write, contradicting the documented "mode 0600". Created with mode 0600 now, and a loose pre-existing placeholder is healed (chmod) on the next session boot; Windows keeps the ACL path on real key writes.
- **`unown automations list` output ends with a newline** (raw `process.stdout.write` meant the prompt glued onto the last line).

### Added (multi-provider support — M0 + M1, 2026-10-21)

- **the multi-provider registry — adding a provider never removes the others** — `unown setup` used to REWRITE `~/.unown/agent/models.json` with exactly one provider, silently discarding every previous one (the single biggest gap vs Cline / Roo / OpenCode / Codex). A new registry module (`pi-repo/packages/self-learning/src/providers.ts`) owns all mutations with upsert semantics: adding updates in place, preserves hand-edited fields (headers, compat, per-model metadata), and `--force` remains the explicit reset to one provider. Live-verified end to end: a session routes `--model zai-bridge/agnes-3.0-flash` through the local bridge AND `--model agnes/agnes-3.0-flash` direct to the provider in the same registry.
- **`unown providers` — the day-to-day management surface**: `list [--json]` (endpoint, key status, defaults) · `add` (preset or custom endpoint; `--key-env VAR` stores a `$VAR` reference instead of the secret — the headless/CI pattern) · `remove` (drops the provider AND its stored key; re-points the default) · `key` (rotation with live verification — a rejected key fails the command, not your next chat) · `default` (validated) · `test` (live endpoint probe with the stored credential, actionable 401/404 classification). Spawn-level golden tests pin every path.
- **Agnes AI is a first-class preset** (`agnes`, `https://apihub.agnes-ai.com/v1`, `agnes-3.0-flash`, image input) — add with `unown providers add --provider agnes --key-env AGNES_API_KEY`.
- **keys are hardened everywhere**: auth.json stays 0600 on POSIX and now gets owner-only ACLs on Windows (`icacls /inheritance:r`) via the shared `secureFilePermissions` path; a secret moving to auth.json clears any old models.json literal (one source of truth).
- **engine CORS (M0)**: the engine API answers browser preflight/requests for loopback origins by default (`UNOWN_ENGINE_CORS` = explicit list | `*` | `off`) — the local dashboard works in DIRECT mode (`NEXT_PUBLIC_AGENT_API_BASE`) end-to-end now, verified by browser fetch + VLM screenshot review.
- **doctor: bridge route-default coherence** — warns when `BRIDGE_ROUTE_DEFAULT` is not in the allow-list (the stale-upstream 404 class); `.env.example` no longer hard-codes it over `BRIDGE_MODEL`.
- **doctor-instructions is gitignore-aware** — a gitignored generated path (`src/generated/prisma` on a fresh clone) is documentation, not staleness; `git check-ignore` decides.
- **landing scroll budget back inside the contract** — 12.9vh ≤ 13 at 1440×900 (uniform `sm:py-[72px]` on the seven standard bands; receipts untouched); the e2e budget gate is green again (18/18).
- **drain-aware `agent:start`** — `waitForPortFree` waits out the engine/bridge graceful-drain window after `agent:stop` before spawning, closing the "Is port 3030 in use?" race on rapid restarts.

### Added (degraded-mode resilience — the agent that knows its engine is down, 2026-10-21)

- **the terminal agent now sees the memory engine and says so (M6) — a persistent footer, one toast per state transition, and a `/engine` diagnosis command** — after the M2 detachment the terminal agent worked perfectly with the engine dead, which is exactly why engine death was invisible: liveness was only learned at note-delivery moments and nothing in the TUI showed engine state. A session-scoped probe loop (`engine-status.ts`, 30 s cadence, 700 ms timeout, `PI_ENGINE_PROBE_*` env-tunable) now drives a five-state machine rendered as a one-line footer through pi's `ctx.ui.setStatus` seam: `memory: up` · `memory: starting…` · `memory: down · N queued` · `memory: paused · /engine` · `memory: --`. Toasts fire once per transition (never at session start — a deliberate engine-off user is not greeted by a warning on every boot; the initial state is a fact, not an event): down → "memory engine unreachable — chat unaffected, notes queue locally", paused → "won't start (repeated failures) — run `unown engine status`", up → "online — learning resumed (+ draining N)". After a spawn kick, fast re-probes (2/5/9/16 s) flip the footer as soon as the engine boots instead of waiting a full interval. The new `/engine` command is the diagnosis surface (state, pid, busy job, outbox pending/dropped, the auto-start circuit's status, and the actions for each state); `/learning` gains per-tier staleness ("updated 3h ago" per file — the k9s "cached as of" pattern, via a new `fsPath` field after the E2E lane caught `ageOf` statting the display path, which always failed silently) and an engine-health header; `unown doctor` surfaces the outbox pending count and the crash-loop circuit on its engine-down line; `unown engine status` reports outbox counts and the circuit both up and down. Verified live end-to-end from an end user's POV by the new PTY-lane journey (`scripts/tui-capture/pty_capture_engine.py`, 17/17 surfaces: SIGKILL → footer down → toast → auto-recovery; corrupt DB → paused footer + chat unaffected while paused; quit → note queued → repair → drained — receipts: stills + pane dumps + `e2e-results.json`).
- **the crash-loop circuit — auto-start that fails repeatedly now backs off and gives up instead of spawning one doomed child per session forever** — the in-process single-flight latch only deduped spawns within one CLI process; a boot-crashing engine (corrupt DB, occupied port) got a fresh doomed child per session/note-delivery, silently, forever. A tiny cross-process state file (`.run/engine-spawn-state.json`) now persists consecutive spawn attempts: exponential backoff between auto-spawns (2 s base, ×2, 60 s cap), a quiet-window reset (10 min — systemd `StartLimitIntervalSec`), and a hard stop after 5 consecutive attempts without an observed-healthy engine (supervisord's `startretries→FATAL`); any observed-healthy probe resets the circuit (k8s CrashLoopBackOff semantics). The footer flips to `memory: paused · /engine` and the user gets ONE actionable message instead of an infinite silent loop. `unown engine start` is the manual override: force bypasses and resets the circuit (the `systemctl reset-failed` equivalent — manual action always wins). `UNOWN_ENGINE_AUTOSTART=0` is the deliberate-engine-off kill switch. Pinned by 42 fresh tests (`test/spawn-circuit.test.ts` — the backoff table, gate reasons incl. exact `retryInMs`, window/healthy resets, state-file corruption tolerance, force semantics, the kill switch) plus 28 state-machine tests (`test/engine-status.test.ts` — footer/toast matrices, injected-probe loop scenarios, gave-up→paused with zero kicks, the fast-path freshness window).
- **`unown engine repair` — corrupt-DB quarantine with bounded loss** — a boot-time `PRAGMA quick_check` gate (the fast O(N) pass sqlite.org recommends for routine validation) now fails the engine's boot with an actionable repair hint instead of corrupting writes mid-dream, and `unown engine repair` does the honest recovery: triage (`quick_check` via a read-only probe — "ok" means the crash loop had another cause and it says so instead of nuking data), quarantine (db + `-wal` + `-shm` renamed aside — every byte kept for `sqlite3 .recover`; a copy would double disk), circuit reset, fresh boot. The loss is bounded by design: queued notes replay from `$UNOWN_HOME/notes/outbox` at next boot, project memory lives in `<project>/.unown/memory/*.md` untouched — only engine-internal run/dream history stays behind in the quarantine. Learned live and documented: a header-only clobber is HEALED by SQLite's own WAL recovery (the `-wal` sidecar held a valid page-1 image and the next open restored it — the E2E lane's corruption had to tear all three files, the honest power-loss-mid-write simulation). Pinned by `test/store-quickcheck.test.ts` (healthy/structurally-corrupt/boot-gate fresh-process) and the E2E S4→S5 receipts.
- **the outbox is bounded and observable** — offline note queuing could grow forever and oversized notes could bypass the HTTP lane's cap: outbox writes now truncate at the 20k content cap (parity with the engine's HTTP lane, enforced again at drain time for legacy files), `UNOWN_OUTBOX_MAX` (default 200, 0 = unlimited) drops oldest-first with a surfaced dropped-counter (`/engine`, `unown engine status`), and one delivery attempt is bounded by `UNOWN_NOTE_BUDGET_MS` (default 2.5 s) racing the whole probe+POST — on expiry the note lands in the outbox and the session proceeds (a wedged network path can no longer hold a quit). Two bugs the test suite caught before they shipped: the `assumeDown` fast path returned "queued" without ever writing the outbox (every engine-down quit would have silently dropped its session note), and the `OUTBOX_MAX` default was dead code (`Number("") === 0` passed the guard — every default install unlimited). Also fixed by the E2E lane: `ensureEngineRunning`'s early-return-on-gated-spawn composed wrong with the circuit — `engine start`'s force path records an attempt, the function's own un-forced spawn hit the fresh 2 s backoff, and the health wait was skipped entirely (engine healthy at t+2 s, cmdStart reporting "not healthy after 20 s"); a gated spawn no longer skips the wait, and cmdStart passes `skipSpawn` (it already spawned). Pinned by the extended `test/outbox.test.ts` (14 cases: cap ±boundary, oldest-first drop + counter fresh-process, unlimited, default pin, assumeDown zero-fetch instant-queue, dead-engine fallback, hanging-fetch budget cutoff, drain-side cap parity).
- **docs: `docs/resilience.md`** — the failure-mode catalog + recovery playbook (R1–R9: down / boot-crash loop / corrupt DB / wedged beyond HTTP / quit-time budget / outbox overflow / oversized note / read-only state / deliberate engine-off — each with symptom → what you see → automatic → manual), the degraded-mode surfaces reference (footer states, toast policy, `/engine`, `/learning` staleness, doctor), the bounded-loss table, and the design references (k8s liveness-vs-readiness + CrashLoopBackOff, systemd StartLimit* + `reset-failed`, supervisord FATAL, circuit-breaker half-open, sqlite.org howtocorrupt + `quick_check`, k9s/vim-airline status-row precedents, Sentry `maxCacheItems`). Touch-ups: `configuration.md` (+5 env vars), `usage.md` (`/engine`, `repair`), `troubleshooting.md` (crash-loop/corrupt-DB entry), `custom-commands.md`, README docs index, `docs/AGENTS.md` ownership, `.env.example` (the resilience block), and the removal of a stale duplicate Session-tools paragraph in `docs/BEHAVIOR.md` that predated the addon framework. Docs gate 38/38; full suite **2585 pass / 0 fail** (was 2500 before the M6 tests).

### Agent Behavior (degraded-mode awareness — the session surface, 2026-10-21)

- **the session now carries an engine-status footer, a `/engine` slash command (the 28th), and `/learning` staleness headers** — every terminal session starts a probe loop at `session_start` (footer line via the `ctx.ui.setStatus("engine", …)` seam — the same surface the MCP footer uses; one unref'd timer, no factory-scope timers, stopped at `session_shutdown` reason=quit, survives `/new`/resume/fork reloads on purpose — the engine cares about the process, not the session branch) and registers `/engine` (state + pid + busy job + outbox pending/dropped + the auto-start circuit + per-state actions). `/learning` prepends the engine-health header and per-tier mtime staleness so a weeks-stale project tier can no longer read as fresh while the engine is down; the session-note quit path uses the loop's fresh down/paused snapshot (`assumeDown`) to skip the probe+POST round trips entirely when the engine is known down. `docs/BEHAVIOR.md`'s slash-command enumeration updated (twenty-eight with the advisor on, twenty-seven in the default boot).

### Fixed (detached engine — cross-platform hardening, 2026-10-20)

- **the last runtime detachment leak is closed (governor is engine-only)** — the cross-platform review found the one RUNTIME path the import-graph tests could not see: the terminal agent's summary-context subagent brief (`tools.ts` → `llm.chat`) flows into the governor's ledger hooks, which would have opened the engine's SQLite DB from a CLI process mid-session — on Windows, precisely the bun#40659 cross-process-write hazard the detachment exists to prevent. The governor is now process-scoped: `startServer()` marks the engine process, and every ledger/breaker record is inert elsewhere. Pinned by a fresh-process RUNTIME test (a real `chat()` attempt from a CLI process leaves the DB untouched).
- **`unown engine stop` drains over HTTP first — graceful stop now works on Windows too** — SIGTERM cannot reach JS handlers on Windows (TerminateProcess skips them), so a new loopback `POST /api/shutdown` endpoint runs the exact drain path the signal handlers run (finish in-flight requests, stop router + scheduler, TRUNCATE-checkpoint the WAL, release the lock), and `engine stop` asks HTTP-first with signals as the POSIX fallback. The false-comfort path ("stopped cleanly" printed after a hard Windows kill) is gone.
- **the Windows engine spawn is actually correct now** — `cmd /c start /b` without an explicit empty window-title eats a quoted executable path (`C:\Program Files\…`) as the *title* and runs the wrong command; the empty-title form fixes spaced install paths. The pidfile is no longer written on the Windows branch (cmd.exe's own pid is not the engine's — `/api/overview`'s pid is the source of truth), the spawn's log fd is closed in the parent on both branches, and `engine serve` catches boot failures (port in use) with an actionable message instead of an unhandled rejection. The not-healthy hint is now `Get-Content -Wait` on Windows.
- **docs**: the windows guide's known-limitations table documents the three Windows realities of the detached engine (spawn escape hatch, HTTP drain, the SQLite cross-process hazard closed by construction); the README's two-ways intro + start section now tell the supervised-engine story with the design-record link; the docs index carries the two new records.

### Fixed (detached engine — the print-mode hang, root-caused, 2026-10-20)

- **`unown -p` never exits — root cause measured and fixed (F10 final act)** — the intermittent hang-forever after the answer (observed since the first live session of the day, ~50% of post-answer runs at peak) is Bun's `fetch` leaving **idle keep-alive sockets** (note delivery to the engine, bridge traffic) that hold the event loop alive: caught live with the answer flushed, two open sockets, eight threads and the process sitting there indefinitely. Two nets now bound every unattended run: the stall watchdog (`PI_PRINT_STALL_MS`, default 3 min — fired correctly in a live pre-answer wedge with its actionable message) and a **hard-exit timer armed only at a final `agent_end`** (`PI_PRINT_EXIT_GRACE_MS`, default 15 s, re-armed by nothing — the work is done and the answer is flushed, so the process leaves). Post-fix: **6/6 clean exits** where ~half previously hung forever.

### Added (detached engine — the benchmark + stability harness, 2026-10-20)

- **`benchmarks/detached-engine/` — before/after receipts for the whole detachment effort** — a runnable harness (`node benchmarks/detached-engine/runner.mjs`, scenarios `arch | crash | autostart | soak`) plus `RESULTS.md`. Measured headlines: terminal-agent fds on the engine DB **3 → 0** (live-session probe + a deterministic fresh-process suite); the runaway dream loop (9 dreams / 75 calls / 630k prompt tokens per 50 min, pre-fix) → **1 dream per 8-minute soak, 0 bursts**, with the governor's refusal verbatim in the engine log under a tight budget and zero tokens burned past the ceiling; crash recovery manual → **automatic** (idle and mid-cycle SIGKILL both heal — lock stolen, orphaned `running` rows errored, API healthy); engine auto-start from a CLI session **6.7 s**; soak verdict — engine up throughout, learning active (4 runs, 1 dream) at 13/30 of the hourly call budget, **RSS −9.8 MB** over the window, **WAL 0 B** (60 s TRUNCATE checkpoint). Before-numbers captured live on the untouched tree (`results/before-baseline.json`).

### Added (detached engine — supervision, 2026-10-20)

- **`unown engine status | start | stop | serve` — the engine supervises itself out of the terminal agent's way (validated live)** — `status` reports pid, uptime, the active router job with its busy-duration, and the governor's budget/breaker state; `start` pings and spawns a detached engine when down (in-process single-flight, stdio to `.run/engine.log` — never an unread pipe, which kills daemons on SIGPIPE; the Windows spawn uses the `cmd /c start` escape hatch because Bun's `detached:true` child dies with the parent there, oven-sh/bun#31603); `stop` is a graceful drain — SIGTERM, in-flight requests finish, the router and scheduler stop, the WAL is checkpointed, the lock released — with a SIGKILL fallback after 10 s; `serve` runs the engine in the foreground of the same binary (the systemd-user-unit / launchd-agent / Windows-service entry; the engine import is dynamic, so a terminal session still never loads the engine half of the package). Live-validated end to end: engine down → CLI session → note delivery auto-starts it fire-and-forget (the note itself stays durable in the outbox and is drained at boot) → the engine came up and immediately ran a harness cycle.
- **single-instance lock (F9)** — the engine now takes an exclusive lockfile in its data dir at boot, carried by pid, stolen only when the holder is provably dead (or, on Linux, alive but demonstrably not an engine — pid-reuse after a crash must not brick boot; `EPERM` from `kill(pid,0)` correctly means alive). A second engine exits 0 with a clear log line; the port bind remains the second guard. Same-pid re-entrancy keeps the in-process test suites working.
- **engine maintenance + honest health** — `/api/overview` now reports the engine's `pid`, `bootedAt` and `uptimeMs` (the supervisor and `engine status` consume them); a 60 s unref'd maintenance timer TRUNCATE-checkpoints the WAL (bounded growth under long-lived readers) and prunes the governor's spend ledger past 24 h; `unown doctor`'s engine-down line now points at `unown engine start`.

### Fixed (detached engine — auto-mode stability, 2026-10-20)

- **the engine's runaway dream loop is closed** — `dream-check` (event: `run:finished`) used to call `runDream()` on every run-finished event, and the dream's own canary runs emit `run:finished` from inside the dream job — a self-sustaining loop measured live at **9 back-to-back dreams, 75 LLM calls, ~630k prompt tokens in 50 minutes** before a manual disable. Three independent brakes now compose: a **self-trigger guard** (runs finishing inside a dream job no longer queue dream-checks), a **cooldown** (`PI_DREAM_CHECK_COOLDOWN_MS`, default 10 min — a run-finished storm collapses to one check per window), and the budget governor below as the hard backstop. The documented "enough undreamed notes" contract (≥3) is enforced on the event path exactly as on the interval path.
- **no router job may hold the engine hostage anymore (job watchdog)** — the router serializes all jobs behind one busy mutex, and a job whose work never returned wedged the engine forever (observed live: a regression run sat `running` >25 minutes with every other job queued behind it). Every job now races a watchdog (`PI_JOB_TIMEOUT_MS`, default 20 min, floor 60s): on timeout the job is marked errored, its still-`running` rows are healed to `error`, the mutex releases, and the late-arriving work's finalize is a no-op (finalize is now **sticky** — it only writes rows still in `running`, so watchdog/orphan recovery can never be overwritten by a zombie). `runAgent`'s per-session watchdog remains the inner belt.

### Added (detached engine — auto-mode discipline, 2026-10-20)

- **the engine keeps its own books (budget governor + upstream circuit breaker)** — auto mode now has resource discipline: every engine-side LLM call lands in an `llm_ledger` table, learning jobs (harness-cycle, auto-dream, dream-check, task-mint, regression-on-ship) refuse to start while over any ceiling (`PI_ENGINE_MAX_CALLS_HOUR` 60, `PI_ENGINE_MAX_TOKENS_HOUR` 200k, `PI_ENGINE_MAX_TOKENS_DAY` 2M; `0` = unlimited), skipping with the reason on the job row — and consecutive LLM failures trip an exponential full-jitter backoff window (`PI_LLM_BREAKER_THRESHOLD` 3, `PI_LLM_BACKOFF_BASE_MS` 30s, x4 growth, 15 min cap) so a dead upstream pauses learning instead of burning retries. Catch-up jobs (note-sync, sandbox-gc) never consult the governor — they are how the engine recovers after a throttle window. `/api/overview` reports `router.busyMs`/`activeJob` and the full governor state; `unown doctor` gains the wedged-mutex warning (busy past 2x the job timeout → restart advice).
- **print mode always exits (stall deadline)** — unattended callers (engine automations, CI) cannot press Ctrl-C, so `unown -p` now arms a stall watchdog (`PI_PRINT_STALL_MS`, default 5 min, floor 10s): any session event re-arms it, and a silent session — wedged dispose, stuck fetch, stray handle — force-exits with an actionable message instead of hanging forever. The default sits above every per-call timeout in the stack (llm.ts 240s, bridge 180s headers).
- **research record** — `docs/detached-engine-research.md`: ~90 primary sources on detached memory systems (Letta dreaming subagents, Devin's daily dream session, Claude Code auto-memory's loop defenses), daemon supervision patterns (Ollama's ping-and-spawn, launchd/systemd backoff semantics, Bun's documented Windows detach bug), SQLite-as-queue and flock lockfile practice, and the Bun-specific hazards this milestone fixes.

### Changed (detached engine — hard detachment, 2026-10-20)

- **the terminal agent no longer opens the engine's database (measured)** — `store.ts` used to run `new Store()` at module scope, and the terminal import chains (`termhooks→hooks→store`, `tools→llm→store`) dragged it into every `unown` process: **3 fds on `db/selflearning.db` per CLI boot**, a schema DDL exec and a `memory_versions` dedup `DELETE` on every startup — and on Windows, cross-process `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` exclusion is broken in Bun (oven-sh/bun#40659), so a terminal session contending with a writing engine was a real hazard. The pure PreToolUse/PostToolUse policy (`decideBash`/`jailPath`/`syntaxCheck` and the BASH_DENY rules) now lives in `bash-policy.ts`; `termhooks.ts` imports it without the engine's persistence; the store singleton is lazy (first method call opens it, with a strong global reference so Bun's GC can never close it mid-transaction). Pinned by a fresh-process test suite (`test/detachment.test.ts`): termhooks, term, llm, bash-policy and the full `cli.ts` entry chain import with **zero DB fds**, while the engine side still opens its DB on first `logHook`. Design record: `docs/detached-engine-architecture.md`.

### Added (the beauty-trilogy validation receipts — 2026-10-20)

- **tui-capture+bench: the beauty-trilogy acceptance run — full re-capture, 39/39 audit gates, and the numeric receipts the plan demanded** — all ten landing scenarios re-captured against the beauty-2/3 TUI (the landing set previously dated to pre-beauty-2 `19439ba`/`bccaba4`); two lanes needed redo during the run and got it (wiki's driver died mid-turn after one still — re-ran clean 3/3; hero's first aggregate audit was produced through the broken vision lane, all-false with `cli_error` — re-audited 6/6 PASS on the working bridge lane). New `scripts/tui-capture/beauty_critique.py` — the numeric acceptance lane [docs/tui-beautification-plan.md](docs/tui-beautification-plan.md) § Validation loop asked for, its critique prompts verbatim from the baseline run so the scores are comparable: polish mean **4.7/10** over 44 stills (baseline 3.9), motion mean **5.0/10** over 9 videos with hero-bugfix at **7.0** (the plan's headline motion lane, target ≥6), and the wall-of-green share on the hero editing frame measured at **0.45%** green-family (`green_share.py`, also new in `scripts/tui-capture/`) against the 62% pre-beauty-1 baseline — the <10% target met by an order of magnitude. The two target misses are reported as measured in **`benchmarks/tui-beauty/RESULTS.md`** with every receipt cited (per-still audit JSONs, `polish-summary.json`, `motion-summary.json`, per-video frame-sample verdicts) — the polish target was optimistic against the verbatim baseline prompt (the plan's own receipts split the story: beauty-2's showcased surfaces scored 6.7–8; the full-terminal prompt reads 4.7), and motion is dragged by inherently static scenes (plan-mode's bounded waits, the cli-doctor shell showcase) while the four real agent-turn scenarios mean **6.5**. The audited set ships in `public/landing/tui/` — 44 stills + 27 videos (9 scenarios × mp4/webm/gif), split-view staying the PTY-lane stills-only exception with 5 fresh pane-dump-verified stills.

### Added (TUI beauty-4 — 2026-10-20)

- **tui: beauty-4 — the feedback release — session diff-stat chip, subagent color lanes, and a shimmer on the working line (the gap-research follow-up to [docs/tui-beautification-plan.md](docs/tui-beautification-plan.md)'s trilogy)** — the release feeds session state, actor identity, and liveness back into the chrome; the three features the post-trilogy gap research ranked highest for screenshot-visible value at S effort. **Diff-stat chip:** a pure per-session accumulator (`session-diff-stats.ts`) counts the edit tool's display-oriented diff lines — the same `+num`/`-num` string `diff.ts` renders, so the chip agrees with the block the user just saw — and counts every write's content lines as additions (the Gemini code-changes convention: the replaced content is not available at the event seam); failed executions and unknown tools contribute nothing. `interactive-mode` feeds the accumulator off each successful edit/write's `diffLineCounts()` and resets it when a new session binds; the footer renders `+N -M` next to the context meter in the same `toolDiffAdded`/`toolDiffRemoved` tokens the transcript's edit blocks use (footer and transcript agree), hidden until the session's first edit. **Subagent color lanes:** `stableLaneIndex` (new `pi-tui/src/color-lanes.ts`) hashes a named subagent to one of 8 stable lanes — one color per actor across surfaces and turns, the affordance multi-actor fleets lacked; the `subagentLane0..7` tokens are optional through the full stack (`theme-schema.json`, `dark.json`, `light.json`, the unown brand theme) with cube-exact hexes that survive 256-color quantization onto themselves (dark/unown ladder `#5f87d7…#af5fd7` → ANSI 68/74/79/149/179/173/168/134; light carries its own darker cube-exact set for white backgrounds — the beauty-1 ladder discipline), and themes predating the tokens fall back to terminal default fg (`hasSubagentLanePalette` gates the paint). The fleet's child rows gain a `▌` lane rail + the child's name in its lane color — the 2-cell rail budgets inside the row cap, so a truncated name keeps its row color via the rail — and the task tool's child-result blocks render the child's name in its lane (`taskToolChildName` + `subagentLaneColor` in tool-execution). **Shimmer:** `pi-tui/src/shimmer.ts` yields a raised-cosine highlight band of per-character weights (12-phase period, 35% band width, wrapping) that travels the working line's verb label while the turn runs — truecolor lerps base→peak 24-bit, 256-color quantizes to a 2–3 step ramp (ladder-safe), plain mode keeps a solid base color; the loader's new `setMessageShimmer`/`advanceShimmerPhase` hooks drive the sweep on the SAME 1s tick that already advances beauty-2's `mm:ss` chip — one timer lane, no new always-on timers, and the sweep dies with the working line at turn end. Pinned by 73 new test cases across 10 files (session-diff-stats 14, subagent-lanes 12, footer-diff-stat 8, shimmer 8, tool-execution-lanes 7, loader-shimmer 6, shimmer-label 5, fleet lane rows 5, status-indicator +4, color-lanes 4); self-learning 2505 pass/0 fail, pi-tui 979 pass (its 20 fd/Kitty env failures byte-identical on the pre-change base), coding-agent 2050 pass (its 33 env failures byte-identical on the clean base — fd binary, generated provider data, terminal env); `check_theme` — unown brand 63/51 tokens + 14 vars, schema-conformant; CLI boot smoke green. Measured on the capture loop after merge: hero-bugfix re-captured with the `+1 -1` diff-stat chip pane-verified next to the context meter (`ctx ▕█░░░░░░░░░▏ 7.1%/131k (auto) +1 -1 ↑3.4k`) and its motion critique rising **7.0 → 8.0** (fleet **6.0 → 7.0**), audit gates 6/6 + 5/5 PASS — receipts in [benchmarks/tui-beauty/RESULTS.md](benchmarks/tui-beauty/RESULTS.md) § beauty-4 follow-up.
### Added (the TUI beauty trilogy — 2026-10-20)

- **tui: beauty-3 — the composure release — overlay choreography, ≤8 fps arrival pacing, and fold chips (moves 8+9 of [docs/tui-beautification-plan.md](docs/tui-beautification-plan.md))** — the plan's final milestone ships the motion tier. **Overlay choreography (move 8):** SelectList bodies paint the `overlayBg` surface through new optional hooks (`background`/`selectedRowStyle`/`hintStyle`), and the selected row becomes a full-width `selectedBg` bar with a 1-cell accent rail `▌` at column 0 — the rail keeps the marker's 2-cell lead so primary columns never jump; every visible overlay gets one surface-colored scrim row above and below via a new `setOverlayScrimStyle` factory on the TUI compositor: scrims span the full terminal width (a shadow cast across the whole row stays attached to narrow centered boxes), never paint over overlay bodies, skip the terminal edge, and are omitted in plain mode (a transparent scrim would blank rows); legacy themes keep byte-identical output. A boot bug verification caught ships with it: the scrim helper must not touch the theme singleton during construction (crash before `initTheme`) — it now applies after `applyFromSettings` in `init()`. **Arrival pacing (move 9):** streaming re-renders coalesce through `createStreamRenderCoalescer` (new `stream-render-coalescer.ts`) to ≤8 fps — 125 ms windows, leading edge immediate, trailing update never dropped, flushed at message_end / tool_execution_start / agent_end so boundaries never lag (the capture harness already proved 8 fps reads calm; the same math applied live) — while the spinner and beauty-2's completion beat keep their own fast lanes. **Fold chips (move 9):** every collapsed-output hint across bash/read/write/ls/grep/find and the tool-execution/bash-execution components renders as one dim chip — `⋯ 14 earlier hidden · ctrl+o` — the affordance the VLM critique asked for, with the expand key resolved live from the keybinding system (`keyText("app.tools.expand")`, never hardcoded) and the whole key lane omitted when unbound. Measured on the capture loop: hero-bugfix motion **8/10** (baseline 4.0, plan target ≥6) — pacing plus the status beat read as a resolved rhythm; the model-picker surface paints exact ANSI 234 at 5.0% of frame with the selected-row bar + rail verified in pane dumps. Pinned by 35 new tests (select-list-overlay-scrim 14, stream-render-coalescer 10, select-list theme-wiring 4, tool-truncation-chip 7) with the tool-execution chip assertions updated; tui 963/963, coding-agent affected suites 109/109.
- **tui: beauty-2 — the typographic release — chrome surfaces, markdown hierarchy, editor focus, and a status beat (moves 2, 4, 6, 7 of [docs/tui-beautification-plan.md](docs/tui-beautification-plan.md))** — the plan's second milestone lands its structure tier across the vendored pi-tui package and all three theme surfaces. **Chrome surfaces (move 2):** new optional `chromeBg`/`overlayBg` tokens wired through the full stack (`theme-schema.json`, the `ThemeBg` union, the 256-color ladder, the checker) paint the input editor and footer as one continuous raised band — `#16161c`/`#1c1c24` dark, `#eceef2`/`#e4e7ec` light, zinc-950 brand — while the transcript stays terminal-transparent (chrome reads as raised surface, canvas as canvas; themes predating the tokens fall back to `""`, pinned), and the footer's old hairline is gone: the editor's bottom border is the band's top edge, and a second rule would read as doubled. **Markdown hierarchy (move 4):** H1/H2 render bold with a `─` rule at heading width (min 3, capped to the container) — structure from geometry, not color — and H3+ drop the `###` prefix for bold accent text (`MarkdownTheme.accent`); the boot screen's `[Context]`/`[Skills]`/`[Themes]` bracket zoo joins beauty-1's unified `── label ──` treatment. **Editor focus (move 6):** the input box is the hero — the rest border lifts one step (borderMuted → border; the VLM had flagged it faint), brightens to `borderAccent` with a `»` chevron while focused (`buildTopBorderLine`, a pure composer that keeps the badge position stable across focus flips), bash/thinking modes ride an inline `── badge ──` on the top border instead of repainting it, and the caret becomes an accent underline, no longer inverse video. **Status beat (move 7):** the working line names the activity — verb-first `describeToolActivity` labels ("Reading pagination.ts...") with a dim `mm:ss` chip ticking 1/s on the loader's new suffix lane — and the spinner resolves to a ✓ for ~600 ms before clearing (motion gets an ending, answering the capture review's "binary jump" verdict); aborts, retries and error stops skip the beat (gated on `willRetry` + last stopReason). Harness truthfulness: the capture driver strips the host's `NO_COLOR`/`CLICOLOR` (CI sandboxes had silently rendered every still monochrome); the theme checker's optional-token acceptance landed in 9f1fe39 and the fleet's status suite reconciled to the boot labels in c521e62. Measured on the capture loop: accent-green ~0.2% of the editing frame (beauty-1 baseline 62%), the chrome band 12.6% of frame at exact ANSI 234 in 256-color mode, VLM mean 4.95/10 over 14 stills (baseline 3.9) with beauty-2 surfaces at 6.7–8 when showcased. Pinned by 60 new tests (editor-focus-theme 22, status-indicator 11, markdown-heading-hierarchy 9, loader-suffix 6, theme-wiring 6, footer chrome-band 4, scrollbar-theme 2); tui 949/949, coding-agent touched suites 77/77, check_theme 51/51 + 4 optional.
- **tui: beauty-1 — the stop-shouting release — edge rails, the One-Dark ladder, the footer bar, and 256/NO_COLOR safety (moves 1+3+5+10 of [docs/tui-beautification-plan.md](docs/tui-beautification-plan.md))** — the plan's first milestone, implemented across all three theme surfaces (dark.json, light.json, the unown brand theme). **The wall of color ends:** every message/tool background token is now transparent — state rides a colored glyph column (• running, ✓ success, ✗ error) with a 2-space gutter aligning content under the tool title, and the edit preview diff renders on the canvas, never tinted (pixel ground truth: the old agent-editing frame was 62% solid green). **Palette re-tint:** dark + light rebuilt on the One Dark / One Light ladders with semantic vars (text/subtext/muted/dim/slate); the unown brand keeps its emerald/zinc identity but drops the amber glare to amber-500 and neutralizes code-block text. **Diff emphasis halved:** intra-line added tokens are bold green; inverse video survives only on removed tokens. **User messages:** an accent `»` rail + continuation indent instead of the full-width gray slab; OSC 133 markers keep line-start placement with correct ordering on single-line messages. **Labels:** one unified `── label ──` treatment replaces the bold `[Bracket]` zoo. **Footer → status bar:** hairline divider, cwd/branch line, and a 10-cell context meter (`ctx ▕████░░▏ 42%`) with threshold colors — numbers became a visual. **256-color safety:** selection grays land on the xterm gray ramp (the old tinted fills snapped to neon cube corners like ANSI-22 #005f00 — the exact neon the captures showed). **NO_COLOR support:** plain mode passes text through untouched while keeping glyphs, rails, and structure — emphasis survives color loss. Pinned by 59/59 in the touched suites (footer geometry, OSC ordering, bash truncation under the glyph gutter, new palette triples); check_theme 51/51 tokens schema-conformant.
- **tui-capture: the PTY lane — pyte-driven addon-surface verification without tmux/ttyd** — a dependency-light second capture lane (`pty_common.py` + four `pty_capture*` scripts + [pty-README.md](scripts/tui-capture/pty-README.md)) for hosts without tmux/ttyd/Chromium: the real `unown` TUI runs in a plain 128×36 PTY, [pyte](https://github.com/selectel/pyte) is the in-process vt100 emulator, and PIL renders full-frame stills — pane text and pixels come from the same emulator state, so the pane-truth/pixel cross-check is exact by construction. Every still ships a `.pane.txt` ground-truth dump, an anchor-verified PASS/FAIL table, and near-blank variance detection; typing uses an Esc-before-Enter protocol to close the debounced autocomplete popup; `AddonsJsonGuard` restores the seeded addons.json byte-for-byte; the todo script crafts a session file carrying `unown-todo-state` entries so the overlay mounts without a model turn; the advisor script earns real notes (a plaintext credential committed into git history → a `[concern]` steer card, VLM-verified through the bridge) rather than waiting on benign-task silence. 11/11 addon surfaces captured and verified live, including the ask_user_question dialog end-to-end through the Agnes failover while Dashscope was quota-throttled; README.md and docs/tui-capture-workflow.md carry the lane's contract.

### Agent Behavior (web results store — stale-while-revalidate, 2026-10-20)

- **webcache: stale-while-revalidate — past-TTL answers serve instantly (marked) while a background refresh restores freshness (RFC 5861)** — until now a stale-but-windowed entry blocked the caller on a fresh provider round (or, if every attempt failed, fell back to the flagged stale answer). The store now implements the other half of RFC 5861: a `web_search` or validator-less `webfetch` re-ask past TTL and inside the window answers **immediately** from the store, marked `· stale — refreshing in background (61m old)` so the model knows exactly what it is reading, while a detached background refresh — collapsed through its own single-flight key, never throwing into the serve path, bounded by `UNOWN_WEB_CACHE_SWR_MS` (default: the entry's own stale-if-error window) — restores freshness so the *next* identical call is a plain hit. Production precedent: nginx's `proxy_cache_use_stale updating` and Varnish grace mode. Measured on the bench receipts: a stale re-ask that would have paid a 101 ms provider round serves in **0.88 ms** (115×), the background refresh is exactly one upstream call (asserted), and the follow-up call is a fresh hit (asserted). Fetches **with** validators keep their exact blocking 304 revalidation path and its stale-if-error fall-through unchanged — one cheap conditional GET is worth blocking on when the answer is byte-identical; the raw `webfetch` mode keeps exact semantics deliberately (power users asking for bytes). `UNOWN_WEB_CACHE_SWR=0` restores the fully blocking behavior (the stale-if-error tests pin that mode). A new `swr` bench scenario asserts the whole contract end to end. Pinned by 3 new integration tests in `test/websearch.test.ts` (search SWR serve + exactly-one-background-refresh + next-call-fresh; SWR=0 blocking control; the validator-less fetch twin) with the two stale-if-error tests now explicitly pinning `UNOWN_WEB_CACHE_SWR=0`, plus 5 `eligibleForSwr` policy tests in `test/webcache.test.ts` (fresh/stale/window/knob/bound).

### Added

- **webcache: the performance layer — an in-memory index, a hot-entry memo, RAM recency, and cacache-style writes, measured before/after with committed receipts** — the store was benchmarked end to end first (`scripts/webcache-bench.mjs` — a new seven-scenario harness: per-op latency anatomy across S/M/L payload classes × 200/2k/20k stores, O(N) scan costs, e2e cold/warm/304 through the real call paths against local mock providers, thundering-herd coalescing measured by origin-call counting (the coordinated-omission-safe metric), zipf s=1.0 workload realism, cross-process visibility, memory bounds; percentiles with warmup discard, warm-page-cache disclosure, assertions inside the receipt gating the exit code). The baseline receipt exposed the costs; the fixes follow named prior art, each measured before/after on the same box: **(1) an in-memory index** (`{key → bytes, lastUsed}`, the Chromium SimpleCache model) so `webCacheStats` and eviction stop rescanning the disk — the dashboard polls stats every 8 s and each poll was an event-loop-blocking full scan (**49.9 ms p50 at 20k entries → 0.003 ms** between rebuilds; the 30-call poll-loop over 5k entries went 311.9 → 12.9 ms, 12.74 ms of which is the one rebuild). Counts between rebuilds are cross-process approximations and are **labeled** as such (`stats.index {mode, ageMs, approximate}`; CLI prints "approx", the dashboard tab an "≈ indexed" chip) — the WebKit `m_approximateSize` trade, with eviction correctness never depending on index completeness (the cap self-heals at the next rebuild; `UNOWN_WEB_CACHE_INDEX_MS=0` is the exact always-scan mode). **(2) a hot-entry memo** (cacache's own memoization shape — 500 entries / 50 MiB / 3 min, `UNOWN_WEB_CACHE_MEMO*` knobs) so repeat hits skip the disk read, JSON parse and sha256 re-verify entirely while honoring their own entry TTL: a 48 KB page hit went **0.211 → 0.001 ms (~200×)**, the 192 KB cap class ~1,500×, e2e warm search 0.029 → 0.013 ms (10,225× vs cold). **(3) RAM recency** replaces the per-hit `utimes` metadata write (Chromium's `UseIfExists` rule; the same write-amplification Linux built `lazytime`/`relatime` to avoid, unreadable on NTFS/FAT anyway) and sweeps iterate the index — the sweep at 20k went **47.9 ms → 0.002 ms p50 (0.16 ms max over 200 consecutive sweeps)**. **(4) cache writes are tmp+rename without fsync** — the cacache write model; a cache entry is re-fetchable, so process-crash safety is the right durability point and a power-loss-torn entry is caught by the payload checksum and re-fetched (LevelDB's db_bench: fsync-per-write ≈ 150× a buffered write; npm's own history carries 3–8× install regressions from fsync-heavy phases on Docker/network mounts). `UNOWN_WEB_CACHE_DURABLE=1` restores the double-fsync; the memory tiers keep theirs — a lost learning note is gone forever, a lost cache entry is a refetch. **(5) stats reads a 128 KiB tail** of the events feed instead of the whole file. An honest correction shipped with it: the baseline RESULTS table had blamed the amortized-put p99 on the eviction sweep, but the after-run showed the same tail while 200 consecutive sweeps measured 0.002 ms — the tail was CPU contention on the 2-core test box, and `benchmarks/webcache/RESULTS.md` records the correction because both receipts' raw samples make it checkable. Receipts: `benchmarks/webcache/results/webcache-bench-1792496271356-baseline.json` (10/10 assertions) and `…-after.json` (17/17), methodology + track README under `benchmarks/webcache/`. Docs: the design record's new performance-layer section (`docs/web-results-store.md`), the knob table (`docs/configuration.md` — `UNOWN_WEB_CACHE_INDEX_MS`, `_MEMO*`, `_DURABLE`, `_SWR*`), usage + BEHAVIOR store contracts, and the CLI/dashboard approx labels. Pinned by 17 new tests in `test/webcache.test.ts` (index modes + put-tracking + clear-resets-index + index-driven sweep + memo root-scoping/corruption-bypass/TTL/clock/cap + SWR policy + durability round-trips + the events tail read) with a file-level `resetWebCacheInternals()` isolation hook; full suites green: self-learning 2504 pass / 0 fail, dashboard tab 8/8.

- **bench: the webcache benchmark track — `scripts/webcache-bench.mjs` + receipts** — see the perf-layer entry above; the harness is a first-class repo track (`benchmarks/webcache/`, indexed in the benchmarks README's testbench table), runnable as `bun scripts/webcache-bench.mjs [--quick|--only <scenarios>|--label <suffix>]`, with receipts carrying every raw latency sample (no hand-copied numbers anywhere), environment honesty (runtime, cpus, commit, warm-page-cache note), and exit-code-gating assertions so CI can run it as a correctness+perf check in one pass.

### Fixed

- **doctor: the AGENTS.md staleness audit now warns only on confident staleness — 31 false positives → 0 on this repo's own AGENTS.md** — the audit flagged any backtick-quoted token containing a slash or a known extension that did not resolve from the project root, but real AGENTS.md prose legitimately backticks package-relative shorthand (`src/cli.ts` meaning `<pkg>/src/cli.ts`), git refs (`origin/main`), bare filenames (`changelog-data.ts`), URL paths (`/api/overview`) and absolute paths (`/usr/local/bin/bun`) — none of which resolve from the root and none of which are staleness. Measured on this repo's own root AGENTS.md: **31 warns, 0 true positives**, which buried the signal the check exists to carry (nobody reads a warn list that is always 31 long). Backtick tokens — prose, a weaker signal than a link — now apply a confidence ladder: the token must be relative, contain a separator, and its **anchor** (first path segment) must exist in the project; anchored-but-missing tails still warn, because an author whose anchor resolves demonstrably knew the tree and the tail is real staleness. Markdown links keep the strict check in both directions (a link is an explicit navigation affordance, not prose emphasis); absolute/URL paths are skipped everywhere — they were never project files. Where the repo's own prose WAS confidently stale shorthand it was corrected at the source: five `src/*.ts` refs in the `bin/unown` paragraph now carry their full `pi-repo/packages/self-learning/src/…` paths (matching the paragraph's own `boot.ts` style), and the end-to-end sanity line reads provider `zai-bridge` with default model `kimi-k2.5` (no slash label). Pinned by the reworked stale-reference tests plus two new ones in `test/doctor-instructions.test.ts` (anchored-missing flags; unanchored / bare / absolute / git-ref prose never flags; links stay strict with absolute link paths skipped) — `bun bin/unown doctor-instructions` on this repo: 0 warnings.
- **scripts: `test_bridge.py` repointed at the bridge it actually tests** — the probe was written when the bridge itself listened on 3032; since the port split that port belongs to the zai-upstream shim, so the probe has been aimed at the wrong service's contract. It now targets 3031 by default (override with `BRIDGE_PORT`) and sends the `BRIDGE_API_KEY` bearer when set, matching the bridge's `authRequired` semantics. `.env.example` also gains the previously-omitted `ZAI_UPSTREAM_PORT` / `ZAI_UPSTREAM_MODEL` knobs (documented as shim-only: they are inert unless `BRIDGE_UPSTREAM_URL` points at the loopback gateway).
- **docs: the dashboard suite's warning noise is now a documented non-issue, not an unchased mystery** — `bun test src` prints ~17 identical `not wrapped in act(...)` warnings (radix `Presence`/`ForwardRef` animation ticks) plus ~26 recharts `width(0) height(0)` warnings per run, all happy-dom artifacts. Measured benign across **27/27 consecutive green suite runs** on the 2-core box (bridge ×6 incl. failover stress, self-learning ×3, integration ×3, dashboard ×2), byte-identical counts run-over-run. `docs/testing-strategy.md` §7.1 now records the signature, the investigation (a preload-level act-flush `afterEach` and an in-file 30 ms flush before `cleanup()` both left the count unchanged — the updates fire mid-test through chained rAF channels, after the last awaited assertion, where an afterEach-scope flush cannot reach), and the revisit condition, so future sessions do not re-chase it. The flake-hunt also re-verified the slow e2e tests already carry explicit generous timeouts (60–180 s) per the repo's own convention — no change needed there.

### Agent Behavior (Interactive Fleet, part 3 — the public surface — 2026-10-20)

- **fleet: the public surface — `UNOWN_FLEET_TIMEOUT_MS` wired for real, the `--help` contract, and the full documentation set** — the fleet shipped code-complete but under-documented; this change completes what a day-one user actually touches. **`UNOWN_FLEET_TIMEOUT_MS`** is now actually read (the 900 s child cap + the print-mode drain wait resolve through `resolveFleetTimeoutMs()` — it was a comment-only claim before; values below 1000 fall back to the default so a typo can never zero the watchdog). The `--help` text gains `--session <path>`, the `task_control` / `ask_question` session-tools block, `/tasks`, and the `UNOWN_FLEET_TIMEOUT_MS` + `UNOWN_WORKTREE_ISOLATION` environment rows. The docs: `docs/subagents.md` carries the comprehensive fleet treatment (the sync-vs-async lane comparison table, fire-and-forget spawns with the exact ack/result shapes, `task_control` with resume-by-name semantics, the `ask_question` park/answer/timeout contract, the fleet widget + `/tasks`, fleet state on disk with the full limits table, and completion batching) plus corrections to the three “what subagents cannot do” claims the fleet nuances (no interactive *human* stays true; persisted sessions and the 900 s cap are now lane-dependent); `docs/usage.md` gains the `task_control` tool row (fourteen session tools) + `/tasks` (twenty-six commands) + the `--session` flag note; `docs/BEHAVIOR.md` updates the tool/command counts and the fleet contract sentence; `docs/configuration.md` + `.env.example` carry the new env var. Pinned by the new `resolveFleetTimeoutMs` trio in `test/fleet.test.ts` (default pinned, valid overrides applied, garbage/sub-second values fall back).

### Agent Behavior (Interactive Fleet, part 2 — 2026-10-20)

- **fleet: the interactive subagent fleet — async spawn, mid-run steering, ask_question, and resume; the pi-interactive-subagents port on a cross-platform native substrate** — reverse-engineered from amosblomqvist/pi-interactive-subagents and rebuilt WITHOUT tmux on plain subprocesses + atomic control files (identical on Windows / macOS / Linux). The `task` tool gains `async: true` + `name`: fire-and-forget spawn with an immediate ack; the result arrives later as a steer message that triggers a new turn. A new **`task_control`** tool (the cciotti-style control-verb facade) steers a running child mid-task (delivered through pi's native steer queue — absorbed into the current turn or picked up at the next boundary), stops it, reports live status, or lists the fleet; steering a FINISHED child by name **resumes** it — the original session file plus the loadout sidecar (tools/model/effort/MCP/system-prompt/cwd), and a missing loadout is refused, never relaunched unrestricted. **`ask_question`** lets a child ask the orchestrator ONE clarifying question mid-task: the ask file lands as a steered-in message, the child parks inside the tool call until `task_control steer` answers it (a steer while parked IS the answer — live-measured deadlock fix), and a 10-minute timeout degrades to best-judgment guidance. The **live widget** above the editor (theme-aware — the upstream's hardcoded RGB colors were deliberately not ported) shows elapsed / current tool / waiting-for-answer / stalled per child, plus a footer status line and a `/tasks` command; a 1s watcher drives a 120s stall watchdog. **Completion batching** (pi-cciotti port): successes coalesce (150ms debounce / 1s max wait) so an N-child fan-out wakes the parent ONCE; failures and blocked replies bypass the batch. The **resume registry** (`.unown/fleet/registry.json`, 32-entry bound, gitignored) persists name → session across sessions; a new `--session <path>` CLI flag (`SessionManager.open`) is the substrate. Headless `-p` runs **drain**: a NUL-prefixed sentinel message rides as the final prompt, the fleet's `input` handler intercepts it (`{action: "handled"}` — never model-visible) and waits for the fleet so async results land while the process is alive. Worktree isolation composes (trees adopted, disposed on finalize). Two live-measured bugs fixed + regression-pinned: the parked-ask deadlock above, and the watcher re-delivering the standing question on every status flip (steer → active → re-ask → loop). New modules: `fleet.ts` (pure protocols: status machine, control inbox, names, loadout, registry, message builders, batching), `fleet-runtime.ts` (manager + TUI surface), `fleet-child.ts` (child-side extension, injected via cli.ts when `UNOWN_FLEET_CONTROL` is set), `context-brief.ts` (below). Pinned by `test/fleet.test.ts` (21), `test/fleet-runtime.test.ts` (16, fake-spawn harness driving real JSONL event parsing + steer/stop/ask/resume/batching), `test/fleet-child.test.ts` (8), `test/context-brief.test.ts` (9), + `--session` parse-args tests. Verified live end-to-end through the Dashscope stack: async ack → WAITING → child result steered back → new turn; ask_question → task_control steer answer → child wrote the answer to disk → result delivered → registry done.

- **subagents: context briefs — summary mode (pi-cciotti port)** — agents with `context: "summary"` frontmatter spawn with a distilled brief of the parent session prepended to their task (`Context brief from the parent session:\n<brief>\n\nTask:\n<task>`): one cheap LLM call over a newest-first projection (user text + assistant text + tool-call NAMES only, ~24k-char source budget) into a ≤4,096-char role-directed brief (per-agent `contextBrief` shapes what it keeps). Graceful degradation: distillation failures fall back to the fresh context with a visible warning, never abort; short sessions (<80 chars of source) skip the paid call; both lanes (sync task + async fleet) compose it. Live-verified: a summary agent correctly reported the feature name + file the parent had mentioned — knowledge that existed only in the parent session, delivered via the brief with zero tool calls. Pinned by `test/context-brief.test.ts` (9: projection order + tool-name-only + budget cut on the oldest line, prompt shape with/without role instruction, paid-call skip, cap, failure-to-null, delivery framing).

### Fixed

- **fleet: restricted agents regain `ask_question`** — pi's `--tools` allowlist filters extension tools too, so an async child spawned from any agent with a `tools:` frontmatter list (`explore`, `scout`, `reviewer` — every read-only built-in) silently lost the ask channel and could only reply `BLOCKED: I cannot ask questions` (measured live: an `explore` child finished in 4 s with zero tool calls, refusing the task). The fleet spawn path now appends `ask_question` to the allowlist it passes — the ask channel is fleet infrastructure, not agent surface, and the tool still degrades gracefully when no channel exists. Covers first spawn and resume (both build args through the same path). Pinned by a new argv regression test in `test/fleet-runtime.test.ts` (restricted list carries `ask_question` + the agent's own tools; unrestricted spawns gain no `--tools` flag).

### Agent Behavior (Interactive Fleet, part 1 — 2026-10-20)

- **subagents: the advertised catalog, delegation levels, role builtins, the BLOCKED protocol, the completion guard, and fallback models — the pi-cciotti port** — the parent model now *sees* the agent roster instead of guessing it: every session that is not itself a subagent gets a byte-budgeted `<advertised_subagents>` block appended to its system prompt on `before_agent_start` (every discovered agent's name + description, minus `advertise: false` opt-outs, plus the one-sentence delegation policy; 16 agents / 12,288 bytes / 512-byte descriptions hard caps, whole-agent front-dropping with `<omitted count="N"/>` on overflow — built-ins drop first so project agents survive). The policy dial is `UNOWN_DELEGATION_LEVEL` (`never|rarely|standard|aggressive`, default `standard`); `never` withdraws the catalog entirely while the `task` tool keeps working when invoked explicitly. Four role-specialized builtins join `general`/`explore`/`background-explore`: **scout** (read-only recon tuned to come back SMALL — cheap routing, compressed inventory + anchors handoff), **worker** (implementation with the deterministic completion guard ON by default and a fixed Changed/Commands/Residual-risks evidence report), **reviewer** (read-only P0/P1/P2 findings + an explicit BLOCK/OK/OK-with-notes merge verdict, P0 forces BLOCK), and **planner** (writes `plan.md` and stops — the file is the review gate; `advertise: false` so it is reached explicitly, not by browsing). Two fail-closed child contracts port verbatim-in-spirit: the **BLOCKED protocol** (every spawned subagent's system prompt gains boundary instructions ending in `BLOCKED: <short reason>` — a machine-readable refusal lane the parent parses tolerantly (fenced/padded/first-line, never a mid-document mention) and routes as a first-class outcome, never an improvisation) and the **completion guard** (`completionGuard: true` agents are wrapped in a before/after git fingerprint — `git status --porcelain -z` path list + SHA-256 over `git diff HEAD`, both with `core.fsmonitor=false`; identical snapshots + exit 0 + not BLOCKED ⇒ the run reports FAILED with "Subagent completed without making edits for an implementation task."; non-git or git-failure reports `unavailable`, never a false failure; worktree-isolated runs guard inside their worktree). **Fallback models** (frontmatter `fallbackModels`, ordered chain after the agent's `model`): a candidate advances only on a provider-style failure (auth/quota/rate/5xx/timeout/connection reset/silent non-zero exit, regex-classified) AND only when the child had done no work yet (zero tool calls — rotating mid-task would discard real work); hard-failed models get 30-minute health marks in the shared `~/.unown/agent/model-health.json` (atomic Windows-safe writes, fail-closed to try-the-model, TTL-pruned on load) so a whole fleet pays the outage once per window; the report lists the attempted chain and bills usage across it. New pure modules `subagent-catalog.ts` (catalog render + frontmatter parse + BLOCKED parse + guard snapshots; no pi imports) and `model-health.ts` (store + classification), role definitions in `subagent-builtins.ts`, wired through `tools.ts` (task tool + `before_agent_start`). Pinned by `test/subagent-catalog.test.ts` (19: level parsing + sentences, frontmatter plucking incl. snake_case, catalog rendering + count cap + byte budget + UTF-8-safe description capping, BLOCKED shapes + mid-document negative + reason cap, guard snapshots against a real temp git repo incl. tracked-edit + non-git-null paths), `test/model-health.test.ts` (11: round-trip, prune/TTL, corrupt-store fail-closed, unwritable-home never-throws, lead-trim keeps one candidate, provider-failure classification incl. the no-rotate cases), `test/subagent-builtins.test.ts` (6: roster shape, worker guard+chain, scout routing, planner unadvertised, role output contracts), and 4 new wiring tests in `test/tools.test.ts` (catalog injection into `before_agent_start` system prompts, no-event no-op, `never` withdrawal, subagent suppression). Engine suite: 2210 pass / 0 fail / 38 skip across 80 files. Standing invariant preserved: no fork of the pi substrate; the catalog lands on the documented `before_agent_start` systemPrompt seam, everything else extends `SubagentConfig` + the task tool.

### Agent Behavior (TUI addon framework — 2026-10-20)

- **addons: the TUI addon framework — `ask_user_question`, the addon-era `todo`, and the `advisor`, all user-configurable via `~/.unown/agent/addons.json` + `/addons`** — the session surface gains three addons bundled in one `unown-addons` inline extension (`src/addons/`, wired into `bootRuntime` after `unown-tools`; subagents get ask+todo but never the advisor), and the first-generation todo tool (`list`/`add`/`toggle`/`clear`) is retired wholesale. **`ask_user_question`** (a port of @juicesharp/rpiv-ask-user-question, MIT): up to 4 structured questions × 2–4 options (reserved labels `Other`/`Type something.`/`Next` rejected at runtime), an auto-appended "Type something." free-text row, per-question + global notes, single-select previews in a monospace box, a submit-review tab — and the call **blocks the turn until the user answers** (presentation tiers: full TUI dialog in place of the editor; an RPC/ACP walker over native dialogs; headless = a `before_agent_start` reconciler strips the tool from the active set both ways, with a typed decline on stray calls — print mode and JSON subagents never advertise a question nobody can answer). **`todo`** (a port of @juicesharp/rpiv-todo, MIT): `create`/`update`/`list`/`get`/`delete`/`clear` over a 4-state machine (`pending` → `in_progress` → `completed`, plus deleted tombstones; `completed` only → `deleted`), `blockedBy` dependencies with self-block rejection, live-id validation and DFS cycle rejection on the merged graph, `activeForm` progress labels, `owner`/`metadata` fields, a live panel above the editor (row-budget layout with a `+N more` summary; completed rows fade at the next agent turn boundary; model-authored text sanitized of CSI/OSC/bidi controls before rendering), `/todos`, and state riding `unown-todo-state` session entries — branch-correct across `/fork`/`/tree`/`/resume`/compaction — with the `/goal` completion gate reading the same list through `foregroundTodosForGoalGate()`. **The advisor** (a reverse-engineered port of oh-my-pi's advisor/watchdog, MIT) ships **OFF by default** (a second model roughly doubles token traffic per reviewed turn) and foreground-only: an in-process loop over `modelRegistry.complete` watching compact per-turn deltas (full user/assistant text, one-line tool cards, bounded tool IO), verifying with read-only `read`/`grep`/`find`, and advising through the emission guard — empty → noise → rank-aware dedupe (strictly-higher-rank re-raises admitted, blockers exempt) → per-update non-blocker budget with rank displacement — after which nits queue as asides at the next turn boundary (one merged message; also flushed at agent end) while concerns/blockers steer immediately (routing alone, never merging deferred asides); `immuneTurns` after a steer degrade concerns to asides, blockers never degrade; oldest-first context trim at 60% of the model window; self-halt after 3 consecutive failed reviews; `WATCHDOG.md`/`.unown/WATCHDOG.md` and per-user `instructions` append advisor-only system-prompt guidance; delivery surfaces are `<advisory>` XML to the agent plus a transcript card and footer status to the user; `/advisor status|on|off`. One config file `$UNOWN_HOME/agent/addons.json` (seeded only when absent, never clobbering user edits; unknown keys dropped, malformed values degrade to defaults, atomic tmp+rename writes; the only hot-reloaded key is `todo.maxWidgetLines`, re-read per render — everything else binds at registration, `/reload` applies), edited live by the `/addons` TUI configurator (every change persists immediately with its applies-when stated) and by `/advisor on|off`. Counts: unown session tools thirteen → **fourteen** (headless sessions advertise thirteen — ask_user_question is UI-gated); slash commands twenty-five → **twenty-seven** with the advisor on (twenty-six by default — `/advisor` rides the off-by-default addon), plus the split-view display mode's own eleven. Docs: new `docs/addons.md` (the full reference — schema, configurator, keybinding grammar, hot-reload semantics, design contracts) with README, usage, configuration, custom-commands, BEHAVIOR, the docs AGENTS.md ownership list and the self-learning AGENTS.md aligned, and the `cli.ts` HELP session-tools block extended to carry `ask_user_question` + `/addons` + `/advisor` (pinned by the help drift-guard test, now 16 commands).
